(topic: auth/configmgr-rbac-auth)

The article records the TGT rule (DOC) and the absence (DER); the lab check stays open. (topic: auth/configmgr-rbac-auth)

A lab check on an isolated ConfigMgr site (add the admin to the AD group, call AdminService with the old ticket, then again after `klist purge`) would answer it. (topic: auth/configmgr-rbac-auth)
- Mid-ticket TTL group removal (QA10 edge case): does an already-issued, TTL-capped TGT survive an admin's early removal of the membership, or is it invalidated immediately? Not stated in S1219.

The lab check stays to confirm it for PAM TTL links. (topic: auth/ad-jit-membership)

  Verification: attempt `ldap3` vs `python-ldap` vs pywin32/ADSI (`ADS_USE_SIGNING|ADS_USE_SEALING`) GSSAPI binds over plain `ldap://` against a Server 2025 DC with LDAP signing enforced → settles QA15 fully. (topic: auth/ldap-smb-signing)
  - Partly resolved 2026-09-27: python-ldap 3.4.8 builds on OpenLDAP + Cyrus SASL and ships no wheels; Windows builds are unofficial (S-mc5jjnfr); the SSF options are documented (S-qscera6a); pywin32 b312 wraps ADsOpenObject with the flags DWORD (CODE S-eu32xfgv, S-lzhs4uhl); ldap3 2.9.1 raises on a required security layer (CODE S-63f6rimh). Windows sign/seal behaviour against an enforcing DC still needs the lab. (topic: auth/ldap-smb-signing)

Recorded as a DER absence; still no GA statement. (topic: auth/transport-crypto)

An Entra or MSAL release note naming DPoP as GA would answer it. (topic: auth/transport-crypto)

Tried: Microsoft Learn identity-platform search, RFC 8693 + Entra web search. (topic: auth/delegation-kcd-obo)

No product page confirms or denies RFC 8693; recorded as a DER absence, the Q&A denial stays COMMUNITY. (topic: auth/delegation-kcd-obo)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: Learn searches for the RFC 8693 token-exchange grant and for workload identity federation; the federation pages call the external-token swap "token exchange" but use a client assertion on the normal token endpoint, not the RFC 8693 grant, and the Azure confidential ledger page calls OBO the "OAuth2 token exchange flow" linking an IETF draft. Only a Q&A answer denies RFC 8693 support; there is still no official yes or no. (topic: auth/delegation-kcd-obo)

  - Resolved 2026-09-27: AES-128-GCM default for SMB 3.1.1, AES-256-CCM/GCM from Server 2022 and Windows 11, strongest common cipher negotiated, mandatable by Group Policy (S-77zvblfr). (topic: auth/ldap-smb-signing)
- MIM PAM product support status in 2026: not researched. (topic: auth/ad-jit-membership)

### Entra / Graph / MSAL / GitLab

- Per-app-registration numeric limit on federated identity credentials (QA4): three-search budget not
  spent hunting the exact limits page; tried general searches, found the mutable-subjects feature page
  but not the limits page directly. Verification: not applicable (this is a docs lookup, not a lab check) —
  needs one more targeted fetch of an Entra app-registration/FIC "limits" or "known issues" page. (topic: auth/workload-identity)
  - Resolved 2026-09-27: at most 20 federated identity credentials per application or user-assigned managed identity (S1293, live page re-read); already in the article and now in gitlab-ci-identity.md. (topic: auth/workload-identity)
- Headless/jump-host-specific WAM caveats beyond "Windows 10+/Server 2019+ supported" (QA5): not found
  in the two WAM pages fetched. Verification: run `msal[broker]` interactive acquisition on a Windows Server
  2025 jump host with no interactive console session (e.g. RDP disconnected) -> proves whether WAM's
  window-handle requirement fails outside an active session. (topic: auth/msal-public-client)
  - Tried 2026-09-27: S1270 re-read (no session caveat); MSAL 1.39.0 falls back to the console, then the desktop window, when no handle is given (CODE S-2tlwkw6u). Recorded as a DER absence; the lab check stays. (topic: auth/msal-public-client)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: Learn searches for WAM with no interactive session, a disconnected RDP session and the window handle returned the MSAL.NET WAM page, the MSAL Python WAM page (`CONSOLE_WINDOW_HANDLE`), the MSAL Java WAM limits and the WAM errors page; none states a headless or disconnected-session caveat. A lab run of `msal[broker]` on a Windows Server 2025 jump host with a disconnected RDP session would answer it. (topic: auth/msal-public-client)
- Whether Token Protection covers a bespoke MSAL Python public client's Graph calls, not just
  EXO/SPO desktop apps (QA6): deployment guide scopes examples to Microsoft first-party apps only.
  Verification: enable a Token Protection Conditional Access policy scoped to Microsoft Graph in a test tenant,
  target a test MSAL Python public client, and check whether the refresh token issued is
  proof-of-possession bound and whether Graph rejects it without a matching device. (topic: auth/msal-public-client)
  - Resolved 2026-09-27 (negative): S1227 re-read lists Exchange Online, SharePoint Online, Teams, AVD and Windows 365 as native-app resources; Graph is not one, so Token Protection does not bind Graph calls (DER S1227). (topic: auth/msal-public-client)
- Whether nested-group membership counts toward an app-role assignment made to a group (QA7,
  `roles` claim path specifically, as opposed to the `groups`-assigned-to-application claim which is
  documented as excluding nesting): not stated in the two group-claims pages fetched.
  Verification: nest a child group inside a parent group, assign the app role to the parent only,
  sign in as a user who is only a member of the child group, inspect the `roles` claim. (topic: auth/group-claims)
  - Resolved 2026-09-27: S1310 says the assignment doesn't cascade to nested groups; only direct members get the role. (topic: auth/group-claims)
- Call-specific throttling and caching guidance for `checkMemberGroups`/`getMemberGroups`, and whether
  they honour CAE (QA18): the two API reference pages carry no throttling/CAE note specific to these
  calls. Verification: none — needs a documentation-only follow-up fetch of the Graph throttling guidance page
  cross-referenced against these two methods. (topic: auth/role-source-options)
  - Resolved 2026-09-27: me/checkMemberGroups costs 4 resource units and me/getMemberGroups 2, same for users/{id} (S526); CAE follows Graph's cp1 rule (DER S1355). No caching guidance exists on S1286, S1287 or S526. (topic: auth/role-source-options)
- `delegation-kcd-obo.md` now researched (round 2). Remaining gap: the MCP Enterprise-Managed
  Authorization / ID-JAG "stable June 2026" date and "Entra native ID-JAG not GA" claim come from a
  vendor/community blog (S1300), not modelcontextprotocol.io itself or a Microsoft Learn "what's new"
  page. Verification: none (docs lookup) — needs a direct fetch of
  `modelcontextprotocol.io/extensions/auth/enterprise-managed-authorization` and an Entra Identity
  Platform "what's new" page to upgrade this from COMMUNITY to DOC. (topic: auth/delegation-kcd-obo)
  - Resolved 2026-09-27: the ext-auth repository marks Enterprise-Managed Authorization Stable, promoted by PR #29 merged 2026-06-18 (S-hrri7kcy); Entra ID-JAG issuance is undocumented (DER S-uw3gpx3u); "Okta only at launch" stays COMMUNITY S1300. (topic: auth/delegation-kcd-obo)
- Whether MSAL Python's `client_assertion` callable parameter (supplying a pre-built JWT) is the
  supported way to back a confidential-client credential with a non-exportable CNG/TPM key (i.e. a
  custom signer calls CNG/TPM and MSAL just transports the resulting assertion): documented
  `client_credential` dict/PFX options all need MSAL to read the private key directly; the
  `client_assertion` escape hatch's suitability for this wasn't independently confirmed against a
  CNG/TPM example in this pass. Verification: build a minimal custom assertion signer using
  `ncrypt`/`cryptography` against a non-exportable CNG-backed cert, pass its output via MSAL's
  `client_assertion`, and confirm Entra accepts it -> proves the escape hatch works end-to-end. (topic: auth/key-management-options)
  - Mostly resolved 2026-09-27: pre-signed client_assertion documented since 1.13.0 (S-ftk5lzj7), callable in 1.39.0 (CODE S-wk6ahp72), Entra assertion format any JWT library can build (S-zb4abl74). End-to-end acceptance with a non-exportable CNG/TPM key is still a lab check. (topic: auth/key-management-options)
- Token Protection: no source names MSAL Python or a CLI/console app by name as in- or out-of-scope;
  the "native client" framing is the closest documented anchor. Verification: in a test tenant, apply a Token
  Protection Conditional Access policy scoped to Microsoft Graph + "Mobile apps and desktop clients",
  authenticate a test MSAL Python public client (WAM broker) against a test resource, and check
  whether the token is proof-of-possession bound / whether Graph enforces it -> proves whether Token
  Protection reaches a Python CLI in practice. (topic: auth/msal-public-client)
  - Tried 2026-09-27: S1274 re-read (updated 2026-09-24) still names no MSAL Python or CLI app; Graph is outside Token Protection's resource list (S1227), so only EXO/SPO/Teams calls could be bound. Lab check stays. (topic: auth/msal-public-client)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: the Token Protection Windows deployment guide (entra/identity/conditional-access/deployment-guide-token-protection-windows) lists supported applications (Graph PowerShell with `EnableLoginByWAM`, VS Code, Outlook and others) and resources (Exchange Online, SharePoint Online, Teams, AVD, Windows 365); MSAL Python or a generic CLI app is not named, Graph is not a protected resource, and status 1008 covers a client not integrated with the platform broker. How a Python client behaves still needs a test-tenant lab. (topic: auth/msal-public-client)

### keys, propagation, revocation, audit, threats

- DPAPI-NG `SID=` descriptor: no Microsoft page found stating (a) the KDS root key propagation delay before a newly created descriptor's key is retrievable domain-wide, (b) the minimum DC functional level/version required, (c) behaviour when the user leaves the group without a new logon, (d) recovery path if the group is deleted. Tried: `learn.microsoft.com/windows/win32/seccng/protection-descriptors`, `cng-dpapi-constants`, general web search. 3 attempts, stopped per budget rule.
  Verification: On a lab DC + lab client, create a DPAPI-NG blob with `SID=<test group>`, remove the encrypting user from the group without logging off, then attempt `NCryptUnprotectSecret` from a cached logon session vs. a fresh logon -> proves whether group removal is enforced at unprotect time or only at next logon. (topic: auth/key-management-options)
  - Partly resolved 2026-09-27: (b) GKDI servers are DCs at DS_BEHAVIOR_WIN2012 or higher and the access check is on the DC (MS-GKDI, S-53ajdzoj); (a) KDS root key 10-hour wait from S402 (gMSA page, DER for DPAPI-NG); (c) clients SHOULD cache group keys, so a removed user may decrypt with a cached key (DER). Still open: Windows cache lifetime and (d) group deletion. (topic: auth/key-management-options)
- PIM for Groups activation latency into tokens and `checkMemberGroups`, and whether it reaches on-prem AD groups (QA9): not researched by this agent (overlaps role-source-options.md territory); left `[UNK]` in propagation-latency.csv.
  Verification: Activate a PIM-for-Groups membership in a lab tenant, immediately request a token and call `checkMemberGroups`, time the delay. (topic: auth/propagation-latency)
  - Resolved 2026-09-27: membership within seconds with app-side caching (S1282), Intune roles via PIM for Groups up to 15 minutes (S-ljhugmcx), on-prem only through Cloud Sync group writeback (S1281); now in propagation-latency.csv. (topic: auth/propagation-latency)
- ConfigMgr AdminService's exact trigger for honouring an AD security-role group change (new logon vs SMS Provider cache) (QA2): left `[UNK]`, owned by `configmgr-rbac-auth.md`.
  Verification: in a disposable lab collection, add a test admin account to a ConfigMgr-RBAC-holding AD group, call AdminService immediately, then after a new Kerberos ticket, then after an SMS Provider service restart -> isolates the cache boundary. (topic: auth/configmgr-rbac-auth)
  - Partly resolved 2026-09-27: the Kerberos trigger is DOC (new TGT at lock, sign-out or expiry; S-ffrzumip); the SMS Provider cache is undocumented (DER absence); lab check stays. (topic: auth/configmgr-rbac-auth)
- Cloud Sync's exact documented sync-interval ceiling (this agent found only secondary/community figures of ~2 minutes / 10-20 minutes; no primary Learn citation was fetched due to a wrong guessed file path in MicrosoftDocs/entra-docs for the Cloud Sync FAQ page). Tried: direct raw-GitHub path guess (failed), GitHub tree search for "cloud-sync"+"faq" (no match), general web search (secondary sources only). 3 attempts, stopped per budget rule. (topic: entra/connect-and-cloud-sync)
  - Resolved 2026-09-27 (noted from the auth wave): the Cloud Sync FAQ (S1280) says users and groups approximately every 10 to 20 minutes, while What is Cloud Sync (S-2vza23mx) says every two minutes; Restart sync forces a run (S-h6idpd4a). Recorded in _conflicts.md under auth/propagation-latency. (topic: entra/connect-and-cloud-sync)
- Kerberoasting detection detail (event 4769 ticket-encryption-type field values 0x12/0x17) is stated only from secondary sources in this agent's research; no primary Microsoft Learn/Support page was fetched and cited for it (see threats.csv note). Left without a DOC-tagged source rather than mis-cited. (topic: auth/kerberos)
  - Resolved 2026-09-27: event 4769 page (S-7bjorbzz: 0x11/0x12 AES, 0x17 RC4-HMAC; monitor types other than 0x11 and 0x12) and Microsoft's Kerberoasting guidance (S-ffangyfp) with Defender alert 2410 (S-6ca4b7bg). (topic: auth/kerberos)

### Added at merge

- Verification: add a test account to an AD group, then `klist tgt` renew (not purge) and `whoami /groups` + an AdminService call → proves whether a TGT renewal refreshes PAC group SIDs (bounds AD group-change latency: renewal interval vs. new logon). (topic: auth/propagation-latency)
  - Resolved 2026-09-27 from documentation: the TGS copies the TGT's PAC into service tickets (MS-KILE, S-ltkr37vz) and renewal leaves ticket fields other than the times and session key unchanged (RFC 4120, S-r6vi3iik), so renewal does not refresh global/universal group SIDs; domain-local groups are added per service ticket (S-v5wjmc3h). (topic: auth/propagation-latency)
- Verification: from a Windows 11 24H2 client, call the AdminService by FQDN and by short name / IP, with `klist` before and after → proves Kerberos-only behaviour since 2509 and what error a non-FQDN call returns. (topic: auth/kerberos)
  - Partly resolved 2026-09-27: Windows does not try Kerberos for an IP host name and falls back to NTLM (S-5yckwasl); short-name behaviour still needs the lab. (topic: auth/kerberos)
- Verification: put a lab engineer account in Protected Users; run the `client` flows (AdminService, SQL, Graph via WAM) for >4 h → proves the 4-hour non-renewable TGT effect and any NTLM-dependent leg. (topic: auth/kerberos)
  - Partly resolved 2026-09-27: a Protected Users admin can run the ConfigMgr console for only 4 hours at a time (S-iqal4gqk); the AdminService, SQL and WAM legs still need the lab. (topic: auth/kerberos)
- **GitLab account block and non-PAT credentials.** The moderate-users doc (S-dhnetmt7) says a blocked user cannot sign in or access repositories and a deactivated user cannot use the API, and S1377 covers PAT auto-revocation only for Enterprise users; neither states what a block does to CI/CD job tokens, runner authentication tokens (`glrt-`) or open web sessions. Tried 2026-09-27: moderate_users.md and personal_access_tokens.md at pinned commits. (topic: auth/revocation)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: the raw `doc/administration/moderate_users.md` and `doc/ci/jobs/ci_job_token.md` of gitlab-org/gitlab (docs.gitlab.com is not fetched by WebFetch): a blocked user "Cannot sign in or access any repositories" and nothing is said on job tokens, `glrt-` runner tokens or open sessions; the job token page lists the job not running, erased or the project being deleted as invalid cases and does not mention a blocked triggering user. A test on a GitLab instance (block the user during a running job) would answer it. (topic: auth/revocation)
- **Windows cache lifetime of DPAPI-NG group keys.** MS-GKDI (S-53ajdzoj) says clients SHOULD cache group keys per domain and security descriptor but gives no lifetime, and no page covers a deleted group. Verification: remove a user from the SID= group, then call NCryptUnprotectSecret on the same machine without and after a reboot. (topic: auth/key-management-options)
  - Tried 2026-09-28, still open: MS-GKDI gives only the SHOULD-cache rule; no page states a lifetime. Needs a lab measurement or a protocol clarification. (topic: auth/key-management-options)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: Learn searches for the MS-GKDI group key cache lifetime, the KDS group key envelope and gMSA group-removal timing returned the KDS root key and AD schema pages, the CNG DPAPI pages and a Kerberos group-membership article (TGT valid about 10 hours); no page gives a DPAPI-NG group key cache lifetime or the effect of removing a user from the SID= group. A lab measurement with NCryptUnprotectSecret after group removal, with and without a reboot, would answer it. (topic: auth/key-management-options)

In code, EE::Approvable#eligible_for_approval_by? calls super unless the licensed feature merge_request_approvers (a STARTER_FEATURES entry, i.e. Premium+) is available; CE Approvable#eligible_for_approval_by? is only "not already approved by the user and can :approve_merge_request", and MergeRequestPolicy#can_approve? is can?(:update_merge_request) && is_container_member?. (topic: gitlab/mr-approvals)

- Whether the Tasks extension lets a second principal (another client or a subagent) read, update or cancel a task another created. Looked 2026-10-16 in the extension specification (S718): the Security Considerations section requires authentication and authorization on every task-related request and says task ids may serve as bearer tokens, and no passage defines sharing between principals. Needs the extension's SEP-2663 text or an SDK's task store code read at a pinned commit. UNK. (topic: mcp/tasks-extension)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: re-read SEP-2663 and the Tasks extension overview through the mcp-docs server (modelcontextprotocol.io/seps/2663-tasks-extension, /extensions/tasks/overview): the SEP's motivation says tasks should be bound to an "authorization context" that each server defines by its own permission model, that this binding is often impossible and then the task id is the only defence, and `tasks/cancel` is cooperative; no passage says a second principal may or may not read, update or cancel another's task. Still UNK; needs an SDK's task store code at a pinned commit (topic: mcp/tasks-extension)

## agents/doc-lookup-sources

- **Whether Claude Code's 200 `WebSearch` call limit applies to an interactive session, and from which version it refills.** Asked in a backlog research item (2026-10-13). Looked in the kb 2026-10-13: `agents/doc-lookup-sources.md:83` states at most 200 `WebSearch` calls per session counted across subagents, from v2.1.212, raised by `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_WEB_SEARCHES_PER_SESSION`, and says nothing of which session kinds it covers or of a refill. [UNK] (topic: agents/doc-lookup-sources)
  - Resolved 2026-10-13: tools reference "Session search limit" and the environment-variables page re-read 2026-10-13 (S-3yod3u7q, S745: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/tools-reference#session-search-limit). "An interactive terminal session has a limit of 200 WebSearch calls", counted across the main conversation and subagents, and the limit requires v2.1.212 or later; the page names no other session kind (the `-p` run is neither stated in nor excluded by it). From v2.1.290 an interactive terminal session's limit refills at about 100 calls per hour, `CLAUDE_CODE_WEB_SEARCH_REFILLS_PER_HOUR` sets the rate (the changelog entry for 2.1.290, 2026-10-05, adds that 0 turns the refill off); `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_WEB_SEARCHES_PER_SESSION` takes a positive whole number and the cap cannot be turned off; `/clear` resets the count unless a running workflow can still spawn subagents. The 2.1.212 changelog line says "session-wide limit" without naming the session kind. Written to the article (`agents/doc-lookup-sources.md`, the `WebSearch` facts): the interactive scope, the refill and both variables. The environment-variables page gives the refill default as 0 (refill off) in a non-interactive session; whether a `claude -p` or Agent SDK run is subject to the 200-call cap is still unstated and stays `[UNK]` there. (topic: agents/doc-lookup-sources)
    - Resolved 2026-10-23: the tools reference (S-3yod3u7q) now states it: with `-p` and in the Agent SDK the limit applies per turn, not per session, and no version is named for the 200-call limit; written to `agents/doc-lookup-sources.md`. (topic: agents/doc-lookup-sources)

## agents/agent-evaluation

- Whether Amazon Bedrock AgentCore Evaluation (`inspect_ai`) has native stdio-MCP-target support, and whether Azure AI Foundry's evaluation SDK has explicit stdio-MCP-target support beyond a UI "MCP Registry" reference, were not confirmed on the fetched pages (2 lookups: product overview + evaluation-harness pages). (topic: agents/agent-evaluation)
  - Partly resolved 2026-09-28: Inspect itself has `mcp_server_stdio()` (S-wwrpen3s); AgentCore Evaluation and Foundry's evaluation SDK were not re-read. (topic: agents/agent-evaluation)
- **self-improving lookup pipeline findings deterministic rules before model judgement** The kb was asked this in a logged lookup of 2026-09-29, and no article answered it (query log finding F-26d83d5f6e31). Looked in the kb 2026-09-29: `rag.py pack` gives `weak`, with this topic in the lead. Needs an official source that states it (`/kb-research`, or the query log's opt-in research). (topic: agents/agent-evaluation)
  - Resolved 2026-09-30: `agents/agent-evaluation.md` now states, from Anthropic (S1896, S1898, S1920, S-lkcsn2fs), OpenAI (S1894, S-m2glighe) and promptfoo (S-ll5srdcw) documentation, how code-based and rule-based graders differ from a model judge in cost, reliability and brittleness, that programmatic gates sit between model steps, and how a self-improving loop combines Python graders with an LLM judge; the run order itself is a derived design choice (topic: agents/agent-evaluation)
- **Whether any vendor documents running rule-based graders before a model judge, or skipping the judge when a rule already fails, as a fixed order.** Read 2026-09-30: Anthropic's evals article and platform docs (S1896, S1898), OpenAI's evaluation guidance and self-evolving agents cookbook (S1894, S-m2glighe), Claude Code plugin evals (S-lkcsn2fs) and promptfoo assertions (S-ll5srdcw) rank or combine graders by cost, weight and threshold, and Anthropic's prompt-chaining gate (S1920) sits between model steps; none states a run order for rule graders and judge graders. Needs a source that documents short-circuiting or staged grading. [UNK] (topic: agents/agent-evaluation)
  - Partly resolved 2026-10-21: promptfoo's LLM-as-a-judge guide (S-azh3wkvp) documents a staged order as guidance: deterministic checks first, run in a separate preflight eval to avoid paying for model-graded assertions on invalid outputs, and a tiered evaluation with deterministic and cheap-judge tiers on every row and an expensive judge only for failures, borderline cases or high-risk routes; written to `agents/agent-evaluation.md`. No vendor page read documents an automatic skip of the judge inside one run when a rule fails. (topic: agents/agent-evaluation)
- **What the kb says about designing token-efficient read and list tools for agents (filters, pagination, response size limits).** Asked in a logged lookup ("What does the kb say about designing token-efficient read/list tools for agents, e.g. filters, pagination, response size limits in tool design?"). Looked in the kb 2026-10-08: `agents/agent-evaluation.md:36` lists the principles of Anthropic's "Writing effective tools for agents" in one line, `claude/tool-output-limits.md` gives Claude Code's output cap and `agents/mcp-stress-testing.md` an example pagination target, but no article states the guidance itself (default page size, filter parameters, response format switch, truncation message). Needs that page read in full (`/kb-research`). [UNK] (topic: agents/agent-evaluation)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21: Anthropic's "Writing effective tools for agents" re-read in full text (S1935): pagination, range selection, filtering or truncation with sensible defaults, Claude Code's 25,000-token response default, a `response_format` enum (concise or detailed), and truncation and error messages that steer the agent; it names no default page size or filter parameter. Written to `agents/agent-evaluation.md`. (topic: agents/agent-evaluation)
- **which model does each census step run on: the batch judge and the phase-4 checker** The kb was asked this in a logged lookup of 2026-10-11, and no article answered it (query log finding F-8324b5892fd4). Looked in the kb 2026-10-12: `rag.py pack` gives `none`, with this topic in the lead. Needs an official source that states it (`/kb-research`, or the query log's opt-in research). (topic: agents/agent-evaluation)
  - Resolved 2026-10-13: the question is about this project's own tools, so the `_self` root answers it, not the public one: `rag.py pack --root _self` gives `good` for the tested question SQ-census-judging-steps-model and leads with line 13 of the `_self` root's `token-efficiency.md` (`census.py judge` and the phase-4 checker's passage judgments run on Haiku 5.5 [CODE _tools/census.py#cmd_compare]); `pack --root public` gives `none` for it, so the gap step packed a root that does not hold the answer: a miss of the gap step on a question the `_self` root answers, a rules finding and no public gap. (topic: agents/agent-evaluation)

- The install and licence facts cite the PyPI project page (S-6buofrld), which returned its script-challenge notice ("A required part of this site couldn't load") to a plain fetch on 2026-10-12, so no sentence of it backs those facts; the project JSON (S-v5hqqcqx) reports `1.2.14` (uploaded 2026-10-06) against the `1.2.12` the install fact records. Left open until the install fact is restated with the JSON as its source. See `QK-pypi-project-metadata-report-now-langgraph` in `_answers.md`. (topic: agents/langgraph)
  - Resolved 2026-10-12: the summary and install facts of `agents/langgraph.md` now cite the project JSON (S-v5hqqcqx) with `1.2.14` and the `MIT` licence; the HTML page's row (S-6buofrld) is superseded by it. (topic: agents/langgraph)
- **Concurrent writers to a LangGraph store.** The stores page (S-wrrbygrl) and the persistence page (S-thsymqx6), read 2026-10-14, document that `aput` stores or overwrites an item but say nothing of locking, compare-and-set or version checks when two agents update one item. Needs a store backend's own documentation (`PostgresStore`, `RedisStore`). [UNK] (topic: agents/langgraph)
  - Resolved 2026-10-14 for `PostgresStore`: its source at tag `checkpointpostgres==3.2.0` (S-26trg6rj; `BaseStore.put` at commit `bfcfea55`, S-vscgh245) writes a put as an unconditional `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (prefix, key) DO UPDATE`, with no version check and only an in-process lock, so of two writers on one key the later statement's value stays; PostgreSQL makes that upsert atomic (S-f3dlrjtw). `RedisStore` and the other backends were not read. (topic: agents/langgraph)
- **DFS Replication conflict resolution last writer wins ConflictAndDeleted folder** The kb was asked this in a logged lookup of 2026-10-15, and no article answered it (query log finding F-41fe61e1f9b9). Looked in the kb 2026-10-15: `rag.py pack` gives `none`, with this topic in the lead. Needs an official source that states it (`/kb-research`, or the query log's opt-in research). (topic: agents/langgraph)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21: the question is a Windows Server one that landed under this topic through a pack mismatch; the kb answers it in `agents/peer-state-sync.md:93` (DFS Replication keeps the version saved last and moves the other file to `DfsrPrivate\ConflictandDeleted` under the replicated folder on the server that resolved the conflict, S-trssy6ht, DOC) and in `_answers.md` QK-agent-state-synced-between-workstations-without. A Learn search of 2026-10-21 returns the same from the DFS Replication FAQ (the folder is treated as a cache, cleaned when it exceeds the configured size, with no guarantee that conflicting files are saved; `Get-DfsrPreservedFiles` and `Restore-DfsrPreservedFiles` recover them) and the troubleshooting page for the folder size (default 660 MB) Backed by `agents/peer-state-sync.md:96`, `agents/peer-state-sync.md:114` (topic: agents/langgraph)

## gpo/admx-central-store

- Whether GPMC/the Group Policy Editor chooses the Central Store over the local `C:\Windows\PolicyDefinitions` store by comparing file versions, or simply prefers the Central Store whenever present (subject only to `EnableLocalStoreOverride`), is not stated on `create-and-manage-central-store` or `group-policy-settings-show-as-extra-registry-settings`. Searched Microsoft Learn for "GPMC central store versus local store precedence"; no page found describing a version-comparison step. (topic: gpo/admx-central-store)
  - Closed 2026-09-28: answered by the 2026-09-26 closing note below (`EnableLocalStoreOverride`, presence not version). (topic: gpo/admx-central-store)
- Whether SYSVOL/Central Store replication specifically uses DFSR (versus legacy FRS on domains not yet migrated) is not stated on any fetched page for this topic; the DFSR mechanism is inferred, not confirmed, for this article. Searched Microsoft Learn for "Central Store DFSR replication SYSVOL"; no page directly names the replication engine in the Central Store context. (topic: gpo/admx-central-store)
  - Closed 2026-09-28: answered by the 2026-09-26 closing note below (DFSR at 2008+ DFL). (topic: gpo/admx-central-store)
- **Closed 2026-09-26:** no version comparison; `EnableLocalStoreOverride` 0 (default) uses the SYSVOL store when present, 1 the local one (S-xtwd545o); SYSVOL replicates with DFSR at the 2008+ domain functional level, FRS deprecated and blocked for new DCs from Windows Server 2019 (S-6glsfens, S-gvgapsup). (topic: gpo/admx-central-store)
  - Closed 2026-10-21 (re-checked): the Learn page "Group Policy settings show as Extra Registry Settings and can't be edited" (Applies to supported versions of Windows Server and Windows Client) gives `EnableLocalStoreOverride` (REG_DWORD under `HKLM\SOFTWARE\Policies\Microsoft\Windows\Group Policy`): 0 uses the `PolicyDefinitions` folder in SYSVOL if present (default), 1 always the local folder; the Central Store page says the store files are replicated to all domain controllers. The earlier answer stands. Backed by `gpo/admx-central-store.md:40` (topic: gpo/admx-central-store)

- **Whether `requests-gssapi` (or python-gssapi underneath) acquires credentials from a client keytab on its own (MIT `KRB5_CLIENT_KTNAME` / `KRB5_KTNAME`) without a prior `kinit`.** The kb earlier said it does and quoted 'having a keytab is sufficient'. Verify against the python-gssapi / MIT Kerberos client-keytab docs or in a lab; until then, run `kinit -kt` first. (topic: arch/kerberos-linux-containers)
  - Resolved 2026-09-28: MIT Kerberos documents the default client keytab (`KRB5_CLIENT_KTNAME`) as used automatically by GSSAPI client applications; `KRB5_KTNAME` is the server keytab (S-n4ns4gdd). The article now says so. (topic: arch/kerberos-linux-containers)

## agents/doc-change-detection

- **Is Learn's `?accept=text/markdown` form a supported interface?** It works (query parameter or `Accept` header) and returns front matter with `updated_at` and `git_commit_id`, but no Learn page found documents it (searched Learn 2026-09-27). Needs a Microsoft Learn platform page or release note. (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)
  - Partly resolved 2026-09-27: the documented Markdown routes are the Learn MCP fetch tool (S2176) and `mslearn fetch` (S-lylm4eqz); `learn.microsoft.com/en-us/llms.txt` returns 404 and no page documents the query or header form. Recorded as a DER in the article; a Microsoft statement on the form itself is still missing. (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)
- **Does a Learn page's ETag change on a site-template rebuild with no content change?** Stable across repeated requests on 2026-09-27; needs the same page observed before and after a Learn build (`x-buildversion` header) to know whether ETag alone can signal content changes. (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)
  - Resolved 2026-09-27: Wayback captures of the ConfigMgr introduction page (S-qvogwadn) share `Last-Modified` 2025-12-11 but carry different `x-buildversion` values and different ETags, so the ETag also changes on a rebuild without a content change. (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)
- **Does a push to a repository's wiki, an issue comment or a star move that repository's `pushed_at` or `updated_at`?** The REST OpenAPI description gives both fields no description and the REST docs read state no rule (github/docs main be38ec5d, 2026-10-10); one repository (`MicrosoftDocs/entra-docs`) showed `pushed_at` and `updated_at` seconds after its newest commit. Needs a repository whose wiki or issues changed after its last code push, read before and after, or a GitHub statement of the rule. (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: a web search for a GitHub statement found only third-party posts that disagree (default-branch pushes, metadata changes and stars are each named as moving `updated_at`) and none on wiki or issue activity; GitHub's GraphQL schema describes `Repository.pushedAt` as "when the repository was last pushed to" and `updatedAt` as when the object was last updated, with no rule. Still needs a repository whose wiki or issues changed after its last code push, read before and after (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)
- **Is an authenticated `304` free of the primary rate limit, and what moves `Discussion.updatedAt`?** The page says a `304` to a request with an `Authorization` header is not counted (S-g3ciqhyh); only the unauthenticated case was run on 2026-10-10 (no token is held here), and the GraphQL Discussions API is not reachable without one, so whether a new comment or reaction moves `updatedAt` was not read. Needs a read-only token: `x-ratelimit-used` before and after a `304`, and a Discussion's `updatedAt` before and after a comment. (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)
  - Partly resolved 2026-10-21: lab with a token (read-only requests to `repos/octocat/Hello-World`): a `200` raised `x-ratelimit-used` by one (13 to 14) and a conditional request that answered `304` left it unchanged, so an authenticated `304` was not counted, as the page says (S-g3ciqhyh). GitHub's GraphQL schema gives `Discussion.updatedAt` only the description "last updated"; whether a comment or reaction moves it needs a comment posted on a discussion, which was not done (an outward action). Still needs a throwaway repository with discussions (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)
- **Does the newest commit of a Learn page's source file always equal the page's `git_commit_id`, and does `updated_at` follow the file's commit date?** One page (Entra device identity) matched on 2026-10-10: `git_commit_id` was the newest commit for the file, `updated_at` 55 minutes after its committer date, and the commit was a `[BULK UPDATE]`. Needs a sample of pages across docsets, and a page whose newest file commit is newer than its `git_commit_id`. (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: a sample of four pages, read from their head tags and the public repositories' commit lists on the default branch: three Entra pages had `git_commit_id` equal to the newest commit of their file in `MicrosoftDocs/entra-docs`, with `updated_at` 55 minutes, 3 h 29 min and 3 h 24 min after that commit's committer date; the Microsoft Graph PowerShell page for `Get-MgUser` had a `git_commit_id` of a "make document changes live" commit of 2026-10-02, not the file's own newest commit (2026-08-11), and an `updated_at` 42 minutes after that publish commit. So the equality holds in one docset and not in another, and `updated_at` follows the build, not the file's commit. Only four pages in two docsets were read (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)
- **What request limit applies to `raw.githubusercontent.com`, and can a deleted repository be told from a private one without a token?** The REST rate-limit and troubleshooting pages (github/docs main be38ec5d, read 2026-10-12) name no raw-host limit and say an unauthenticated `404` hides private repositories; one probe on 2026-10-12 showed a raw request not charged to the REST `core` counter and no `x-ratelimit-*` header on a `200`. Needs a GitHub statement of the raw host's limits, or a run of raw requests until one is throttled (a `429`, and its `Retry-After`); telling deleted from private needs a token or a Microsoft or owner statement. (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: a web search for a documented limit of `raw.githubusercontent.com` found community threads reporting `429` answers and one second-hand figure, and no GitHub page that states a limit (the REST rate-limit pages cover `api.github.com`). A run of raw requests until a `429` was not made, since it would risk blocking this host's address. Still needs a GitHub statement; telling a deleted repository from a private one still needs a token or an owner statement (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)
- **What does Cho and Garcia-Molina's "Estimating frequency of change" (ACM TOIT 3(3), 2003) itself say about its modified estimator, and whether it gives an estimator for checks at irregular times?** Looked for on 2026-10-12: arXiv (no entry), the author's UCLA page (`oak.cs.ucla.edu`, timed out), the Stanford InfoLab report server (`ilpubs.stanford.edu`, answered 500), and a web search that found only papers citing it; the kb's estimator facts rest on a 2008 WWW poster's account of its section 4.2 and a 2021 arXiv paper. Needs the paper's text (the ACM Digital Library page or a mirror), to read the estimator's conditions and any variant for irregular intervals. (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: the ACM Digital Library page for DOI 10.1145/857166.857170 answered 403 to a plain GET, the Stanford InfoLab report url answered 500 and the UCLA host timed out; a web search returned only the citation (TOIT 3(3), pp. 256-290). Still needs the paper's text through the ACM library or an institutional copy (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)
- **Does a Tech Community post answer a client that identifies as a crawler with server-rendered HTML, and is its embedded `__NEXT_DATA__` state a supported interface?** One plain `GET` on 2026-10-13 got a shell (`<div id="__next">` with a loading placeholder), the post's text in `__NEXT_DATA__` and in the JSON-LD `BlogPosting` description, and an `isCrawler` flag `true` for the probe's own user agent; no Microsoft page read states how the site renders for crawlers or documents the embedded state. Needs the site's rendering notes from Microsoft, or the same page requested with a search crawler's user agent and compared. (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: lab on one Tech Community post: a plain GET with the probe's own user agent answered 200 with the shell and `"isCrawler":true`, while the same url with a Googlebot user agent string, sent from this non-Google address, answered 403 with a small HTML error page and no post, so the crawler path cannot be tested by user agent alone; a web search found no Microsoft note on how the site renders for crawlers. Still needs Microsoft's rendering notes or a request from a verified crawler network (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)
- **Does any standard or vendor give a period for remembering that a host refused or did not answer a connection?** Looked for on 2026-10-13: RFC 8305 (read in full; connection attempt delay and DNS caching only), Microsoft's Circuit Breaker pattern ("a few seconds" then "a few minutes", no number), Polly's and Envoy's configuration defaults (5 s, 30 s growing to 300 s). The defaults are of libraries, not rules for a documentation host. Needs a statement from a documentation provider or a standard that fixes a period, which may not exist. (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: a web search for a standard or vendor period for remembering a refused or unanswered connection found Squid's `negative_ttl` (default 0 s, its page warns that enabling it breaks the HTTP standard), Oracle's Netra proxy cache (five minutes), a 1996 Harvest paper (five minutes for DNS failures) and RFC 2308 (negative caching of DNS answers, not of connections); none is a documentation provider's rule. Still needs a provider statement, which may not exist (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)
- **A method and threshold for the agreement of two agents' research runs over the same briefs (set overlap of added facts and cited sources, Jaccard or otherwise).** Looked 2026-10-15 in the kb (`rag.py pack` on agreement and Jaccard overlap of research runs; the only Jaccard fact is the snapshot-text comparison of S-wcrrjfwh, a different measure on a different object) and in the Claude Code and MCP docs, which define no comparison of runs. Needs a published method for inter-run agreement of LLM research outputs, or the benchmark's own definition recorded as a decision. [UNK] (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: a web search for inter-run agreement of LLM research runs found arXiv papers that define run-to-run stability as the mean pairwise Jaccard similarity of repeated outputs (arXiv 2603.15840) and one that reports run-to-run F1 of citations (a legal-extraction paper); only search excerpts were read, none is about added facts and cited sources of research agents, and none is an official or peer-reviewed method. Leads only. Still needs those papers read, or the benchmark's own definition recorded as a decision (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)
- **How do browsers and screen readers expose an `aside` inside a `nav`, and is `data-type="sidebar"` (the SRE book's `aside` markup) a published convention?** Looked for on 2026-10-17 in the HTML Standard's `aside` and `nav` entries, ARIA in HTML, HTML-AAM and WAI-ARIA 1.2: they give the roles and the accessible-name rule but no sentence on an `aside` in a `nav`, and none defines `data-type="sidebar"`. Needs a browser accessibility tree dump of such a page, or the publisher's markup notes. [UNK] (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: a web search found accessibility checkers' rules (axe `landmark-complementary-is-top-level`, AccessLint) that say an `aside` should sit at the top level because a nested one may not be exposed as a top-level landmark; they are tool rules, not browser or screen-reader tests, and none defines `data-type="sidebar"`. Still needs a browser accessibility tree dump of such a page or the publisher's markup notes (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)
- The `api/` prefix fact at line 57 (404 on learn.microsoft.com/en-us/api/, no api_en-us sitemap, repo mapping for rest/api/azure/) could not be re-verified from the Learn markdown fetch of /en-us/rest/api/azure/, which carries no head tags or sitemap data. (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)
  - Narrowed 2026-10-21: read from the raw HTML, not the markdown fetch: `learn.microsoft.com/en-us/api/` answers 404, `/api_en-us/sitemap.xml` answers 404 after its redirect, and the HTML of `/en-us/rest/api/azure/` carries the head tags `original_content_git_url` (`MicrosoftDocs/azure-docs-rest-apis`, branch `live`, `index.md`) and a `git_commit_id`; that repository's API url answers 404. The tag now reads branch `live` where the fact at line 57 says `main`; the markdown fetch still carries no head tags, which is why the fact must be re-read from the HTML Backed by `agents/doc-change-detection.md:57` (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)
- The memdocs root listing at commit 4b5429df (https://api.github.com/repos/MicrosoftDocs/memdocs/contents/?ref=4b5429df8b47046c6b251e572ee61199fb5d4a5d) returned 404 Not Found on 2026-10-21; the per-product redirection file names can no longer be re-read. (topic: agents/doc-change-detection)

## agents/hybrid-retrieval

- **Does any MCP client turn MCP tool results into Claude `search_result` blocks?** The MCP 2026-07-28 tool result has no citation content type; the Claude Code docs mention `search_result` only for web search errors. Looked in code.claude.com docs (rg `search_result`, `citations`) 2026-09-27. Needs Claude Code or Agent SDK release notes. (topic: agents/hybrid-retrieval)
  - Tried 2026-09-27: platform.claude.com search-results page (S-4c46o537: blocks come only from the caller's own `tool_result`, no MCP mention), the MCP connector page (S-qpqoaaqj: `mcp_tool_result` example is text only), claude-code-docs rg `search_result`. No conversion documented; recorded in the article as a DER absence. (topic: agents/hybrid-retrieval)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: the claude-code-docs search for `search_result` and MCP tool results returned the errors page (a 400 for undecryptable `encrypted_content` in a hosted web-search `search_result` block, which Claude Code's own WebSearch avoids by recording results as plain text) and the Agent SDK custom-tools page (block shapes of a tool result come from the MCP `CallToolResult`: text, image, resource); no page documents a conversion of MCP tool results into `search_result` blocks. Still needs release notes or one session whose MCP tool result is checked in the request it produces (topic: agents/hybrid-retrieval)
- **How Model2Vec pools token vectors at inference, and whether a shipped vector table can be evaluated without `numpy`.** The v0.9.0 README (S-aqnin65g) describes distillation into per-token static embeddings but not the pooling step or the model file format. Looked in the README 2026-09-27. Needs minish.ai inference docs or `model2vec/model.py` at v0.9.0 (a CODE source). (topic: agents/hybrid-retrieval)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21: read `model2vec/model.py` and `model2vec/persistence/persistence.py` at v0.9.0 (S-vg37lid6, S-ojya3ghv): the sentence vector is the mean of the token vectors (each multiplied by a per-token weight when the model has weights, after a token mapping when it has one), zero for a sentence with no tokens, divided by the L2 norm plus 1e-32 when `normalize` is set; the model file is a safetensors file with `embeddings` (and `weights`, `mapping`) beside `tokenizer.json` and `config.json`; every step uses `numpy`, so a reader without it implements the mean itself. Written to `agents/hybrid-retrieval.md:124` (topic: agents/hybrid-retrieval)

- **How RRF breaks a tie between equal fused scores, and how it treats a document a ranking lacks.** The paper (S-g2bbx2bz, two pages, read in full 2026-10-12) defines RRF over permutations of one document set and the Elasticsearch retriever page (S-fn6fxrr5, read in full 2026-10-12) shows the absent document adding nothing but states no tie rule; Azure's page (S-btrwp2yj) was not searched for one. Needs a vendor or paper statement, or the Elasticsearch source at a pinned commit (a CODE source). (topic: agents/hybrid-retrieval)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21: the Elasticsearch source at commit b1c8813f (S-z7tb6v5x, S-acm2juhd) sums weight times 1/(rank constant + rank) over the rankings that hold a document, so an absent document adds nothing, and sorts by fused score, then by smaller shard index, then by smaller document id; written to `agents/hybrid-retrieval.md:148`. A web search for a tie rule in Azure's hybrid ranking page found none: the page states the sum and the sort and nothing on ties or on a document missing from one list (topic: agents/hybrid-retrieval)
- **What is reciprocal rank fusion and what k constant does the original paper use?** The kb was asked this in a logged lookup of 2026-10-12, and no article answered it (query log finding F-d92a053cc132). Looked in the kb 2026-10-12: `rag.py pack` gives `weak`, with this topic in the lead. Needs an official source that states it (`/kb-research`, or the query log's opt-in research). (topic: agents/hybrid-retrieval)
  - Resolved 2026-10-13: `agents/hybrid-retrieval.md:146` gives the score as the sum over the rankings of 1/(k + r(d)) and `:143` the constant: the paper's pilot fixed k = 60 as near-optimal, the choice not critical (MAP .2145 at 60; S-g2bbx2bz, DOC); the same pack, re-run 2026-10-13, leads with `:142-147` and still gives `weak`, so the article held the answer when the entry was filed. (topic: agents/hybrid-retrieval)
- **How NQC and the other score-spread query performance predictors are computed from a ranked list.** The abstracts of S-lsfxdurw and S-i6b6z3is name the predictors (NQC, UQC, retrieval-score-based unsupervised methods) but give no formula, and the article's probe reads NQC as the spread of the top scores without a source that says so. Looked 2026-10-12: those two abstracts, the abstract of S-j7z4u43w (post-retrieval predictors read the score distribution or the coherence of the top documents, no formula), and the arXiv search API for `normalized query commitment` (no hit). Needs the NQC paper or a survey that defines it on an open page. (topic: agents/hybrid-retrieval)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21: arXiv 2304.11752 (S-vjobnwrv, read as the PDF) defines NQC as the standard deviation of the top-k retrieval scores divided by P(Q|C), the similarity of the query to the collection from idf-like statistics over its terms, with the rationale that a well-defined need gives a heavy-tailed score distribution; written to `agents/hybrid-retrieval.md:185`. The probe's "spread of the top ten BM25 scores" is the numerator without the denominator. The original Shtok et al. paper was not read (topic: agents/hybrid-retrieval)
- **How a lexical analyzer reads a hyphenated version or identifier (`5-5`, a SID `S-1-5-21-...`): split into parts, kept whole, or joined into a dotted number.** The article had no tokenization facts. Azure AI Search's page (S-a2wa7lbg, read in full 2026-10-12) gives them: its standard analyzer drops the hyphens (`SVP10-NOR-00` gives `svp10`, `nor`, `00`), its `keyword`, `whitespace` and language analyzers keep the string whole, and no analyzer there joins digits into a dotted number. (topic: agents/hybrid-retrieval)
  - Partly resolved 2026-10-12: the Azure facts are in the article's "Tokenizing hyphenated strings and identifiers". Still open: how Elasticsearch's standard tokenizer and `word_delimiter_graph` read `5-5` and a SID, which needs the Elasticsearch or Lucene reference pages. (topic: agents/hybrid-retrieval)
- **lookup_eval.csv expect_paths: must the lead article be one of them, or any article in the pack** The kb was asked this in a logged lookup of 2026-10-15, and no article answered it (query log finding F-78a048b38e77). Looked in the kb 2026-10-15: `rag.py pack` gives `weak`, with this topic in the lead. Needs an official source that states it (`/kb-research`, or the query log's opt-in research). (topic: agents/hybrid-retrieval)
  - Tried 2026-10-20: the question is about this project's own eval tool, so no vendor source settles it; the code does: in `_tools/rag.py` (`judge` of `eval`), a row's path check passes when any one of its `;`-separated `expect_paths` is among the article paths of the row's pack, the lead article or not, and a row with no `expect_paths` skips the check. the `_self` root's content-rules doc (its ledgers section) names the column and not this rule: a rules finding for the `_self` root, no public gap. (topic: agents/hybrid-retrieval)
- Which cloud platforms (Amazon Bedrock, Google Cloud) support Claude search result blocks: the platform.claude.com search-results page read 2026-10-21 no longer states it; look at the Bedrock and Vertex pages. (topic: agents/hybrid-retrieval)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21: the Claude API features overview (S-aaaxxqtu) lists the search results feature with availability on the Claude API, Claude Platform on AWS, Amazon Bedrock, Google Vertex AI and Microsoft Foundry and states that a platform listed without a label offers a feature as stable and fully supported; written to `agents/hybrid-retrieval.md:73`, replacing the unconfirmed fact. The search-results page itself names no platform (topic: agents/hybrid-retrieval)

## python/stdlib-windows-portability
- **Whether a Windows console host starts with `ENABLE_VIRTUAL_TERMINAL_PROCESSING` on, from which Windows version, and what a console does with an escape sequence while it is off; and whether it honours `ESC [ 3 J` (erase saved lines).** Looked 2026-10-18 in the Learn pages "Console Virtual Terminal Sequences" and "SetConsoleMode function" (neither states a default for the flag or a Windows version; the ED table lists 0, 1 and 2 only) and in CPython 3.14.7 (`winconsoleio.c`, `posixmodule.c`, `_colorize.py` read the flag, none sets it). Needs a Learn page that states the default or a probe on a Windows host (`GetConsoleMode` on a fresh console, then a printed `ESC [ 2 J`). (topic: python/stdlib-windows-portability)
  - Narrowed 2026-10-21: the Learn page "High-Level Console Modes" says a new screen buffer starts with two output modes on (processed output and wrapping at end of line) and lists `ENABLE_VIRTUAL_TERMINAL_PROCESSING` outside them, so the flag reads as off by default [DOC S-bq7uyypb; DER in `python/stdlib-windows-portability.md`]; still open: a Windows version or console host that departs from that reading, what a console does with an escape sequence while the flag is off, and whether `ESC [ 3 J` is honoured (the sequences page lists ED 0, 1 and 2 only), which need a probe on a Windows host [UNK] (topic: python/stdlib-windows-portability)

- **Who maintains no-color.org, under which licence, and whether the Windows console renders `ESC [ 1 ; 31 m` (bold before red) as bright red.** Looked 2026-10-18 in no-color.org (page text and HTML: no author, publisher or licence line; it links the repository `jcs/no_color`), that repository's README and GitHub API record (`"license": null`) and the Learn page "Console Virtual Terminal Sequences" (its sample orders `31` before `1`; its `31` is "non-bold/bright"; no sentence on the reverse order). Needs a page that names the maintainer and licence, or a probe on a Windows console of `1;31` against `31;1`. [UNK] (topic: python/stdlib-windows-portability)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: no-color.org ("Last updated: 2026-10-12") still names no author, publisher or licence; the GitHub API record of `jcs/no_color` still shows `license` null and owner `jcs`; a web search for how the Windows console orders `1;31` returned only general SGR descriptions (parameters apply left to right) and a Wine merge request, no Microsoft statement. Still needs a page that names the maintainer and licence, or a probe on a Windows console of `1;31` against `31;1` (topic: python/stdlib-windows-portability)

- **What modification time `git clone` and `git worktree add` give the files they check out** (whether a cache keyed on `st_mtime_ns`, such as the pack index fingerprint, can match in a fresh clone of the same commit). Looked 2026-10-05 in git v2.55.0 `gitfaq.adoc` (no mention of timestamps) and `technical/racy-git.adoc` (index stat data, not checkout times). Needs a git page that states it, or a measured `stat` of a fresh clone on the Windows host. (topic: python/stdlib-windows-portability)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: a web search found only secondary explainers (git stores no per-file modification time, so checkout writes new files and the file system gives them their time) and a 2014 mailing-list request for an option to set it from the commit date; no git documentation page states it, and `gitfaq` and `technical/racy-git` (read 2026-10-05) do not. Still needs a git page that states it, or a measured `stat` of a fresh clone on the Windows host (topic: python/stdlib-windows-portability)

- **Whether `python.exe` carries the `longPathAware` manifest element, so a path over 260 characters without the `\\?\` prefix works in `os.rename` and `shutil.rmtree` once `LongPathsEnabled` is `1`.** Looked 2026-10-20 in the Learn pages "Maximum Path Length Limitation" (S1591: the opt-in needs the registry value and the manifest element) and "Naming Files, Paths, and Namespaces" (S-5ck3iec5), in the os.path 3.14 documentation (S-zyrhexwo) and in CPython 3.14.7 `ntpath.py` (S-elctquww), which handles the `\\?\` prefix but says nothing of the manifest. Needs the Python Windows usage page or the interpreter's manifest read on a Windows host. [UNK] (topic: python/stdlib-windows-portability)

- **Which error a rename or delete returns when the directory is a live process's working directory on Windows, and whether the tree is left partly deleted.** Looked 2026-10-08 on Microsoft Learn: `SetCurrentDirectory` (remarks: the current directory "is locked while the process is executing. That prevents the directory from being deleted, moved, or renamed"), `RemoveDirectory` (deletion on close), `CreateProcess` (`lpCurrentDirectory`) and the system error codes (`ERROR_SHARING_VIOLATION` is 32). No page ties the lock to `ERROR_SHARING_VIOLATION` or `ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED`, and none says what a recursive delete leaves behind. Needs a recorded Windows run of a rename and a `RemoveDirectory` on a directory another process has as its working directory, with the code read from `GetLastError` (or `winerror` in Python). - **Python os.link hard link: same filesystem requirement, EXDEV, Windows NTFS support** The kb was asked this in a logged lookup of 2026-10-05, and no article answered it (query log finding F-114b1ea76574). Looked in the kb 2026-10-05: `rag.py pack` gives `none`, with this topic in the lead. Needs an official source that states it (`/kb-research`, or the query log's opt-in research). (topic: python/stdlib-windows-portability)
  - Resolved 2026-10-08: F-114b1ea76574: POSIX `link()` lists `[EXDEV]` for paths on different file systems where the implementation does not support links between them (some implementations allow them), `CreateHardLinkW` and the Win32 hard-links overview require one volume and NTFS, and `os.link` raises `OSError` (`errno.EXDEV` is "Cross-device link"; the mapping is derived for `os.link`, stated only for `os.copy_file_range` and there only for Linux kernels older than 5.3) [DOC S-edjp43o7, S-oqrlzkwe, S-ip5ugdeb, S-5brdhqgo, S-qo63gfo7]; the Windows error number stays open [UNK]. (topic: python/stdlib-windows-portability)
- **The Windows error number `os.link` raises across volumes or on a non-NTFS filesystem** (`winerror` or `errno`). Looked 2026-10-08 on the `os` and `errno` pages of the Python 3.13 docs, the POSIX `link()` page, and Microsoft Learn's `CreateHardLinkW` and hard-links pages: they say the function fails and `GetLastError` has the code, not which. Needs a recorded Windows run of `os.link` across two volumes, or a Microsoft page that names the error. [UNK] (topic: python/stdlib-windows-portability)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: the Learn `CreateHardLinkW` page, searched again through the docs server, says the call returns zero with the code in `GetLastError`, names `ERROR_PATH_NOT_FOUND` only for a name over `MAX_PATH` without the `\\?\` prefix, and requires one volume; a web search found a Jython report and Mercurial patches, no Python or Microsoft page that names the error of a cross-volume link. Still needs a recorded Windows run of `os.link` across two volumes (topic: python/stdlib-windows-portability)
- **Whether `os.replace` is atomic on Windows, so that a reader never sees a half-written file replaced by it.** Looked 2026-10-27 in the Python `os` page (S-5brdhqgo: "this is a POSIX requirement", nothing for Windows). Needs a Microsoft page on `MoveFileEx` with `MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING` that states it, or a recorded Windows run with a concurrent reader. [UNK] (topic: python/stdlib-windows-portability)

## python/windows-python-install

- **Whether python.org's Windows installers carry an Authenticode signature, and which signer subject to expect.** The Python docs' Windows usage page (S-sjuwcuhk) names Authenticode only for install manager index signatures; python.org's Sigstore page (S-jzqdphnx) and PEP 761 describe Sigstore and OpenPGP only. Looked 2026-09-29. `_tools/install-python.ps1` checks an Authenticode signer; an official statement of the expected subject is still missing. [UNK] (topic: python/windows-python-install)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21: python.org's download page states that the Windows installers and all binaries of each release are signed with an Authenticode certificate issued to the Python Software Foundation, gives the full subject (`CN = Python Software Foundation, O = Python Software Foundation, L = Beaverton, S = Oregon, C = US`) and says the certificate authority has been Microsoft's Identity Verification Root Certificate Authority since 14 October 2024 [DOC S-2jrm4fpu]; recorded in `python/windows-python-install.md` (topic: python/windows-python-install)

## claude/settings-and-scopes

- **The `--setting-sources` flag's own definition.** Looked 2026-10-06 in the cli-reference page's flags table (searched for "setting" in the whole page): it has no row for the flag, though a dozen pages link to `cli-reference#cli-flags` for it. The valid source names and the comma-separated value form are not stated on any page read; the effect is taken from the memory, permissions and sandboxing pages. Needs the flag's row on the CLI reference, or `claude --help` output of a pinned version. [UNK] (topic: claude/settings-and-scopes)
  - Tried 2026-10-21 from the binary: `claude --help` of Claude Code 2.1.303 prints "--setting-sources <sources>  Comma-separated list of setting sources to load (user, project, local).", so the names are `user`, `project` and `local` and the value is comma-separated; a headless run with `--setting-sources project` and one with an empty value were both accepted. The CLI reference page still has no row for the flag (a text search of the English docs finds it only in the debug-your-config page and as a pointer from the settings and sandboxing pages), so this stays a statement of the pinned binary, not of the docs (topic: claude/settings-and-scopes)
- **Whether `.claude/skills`, `.claude/agents`, `.claude/commands` and `.claude/hooks` are named in the permission system's protected paths.** Looked 2026-10-06 in the permission-modes page's Protected paths section (S-zcuxapgb): it lists `.claude` as a protected directory with exceptions and does not name those four subdirectories; the sandbox page (S-l6l42j6e) names them for its own list. [UNK] (topic: claude/settings-and-scopes)
  - Narrowed 2026-10-21: re-read the permission-modes page's Protected paths section (S-zcuxapgb): the protected directories are `.git`, `.config/git`, `.vscode`, `.idea`, `.husky`, `.cargo`, `.devcontainer`, `.yarn`, `.mvn` and `.claude` "with a few exceptions", and the exceptions named are Claude's own worktrees under `.claude/worktrees/`, the session's plan files, a background session's scratch directory, markdown files of the auto-memory and subagent-memory directories, a `--plugin-dir` directory and `dev-mods/`. `.claude/skills`, `.claude/agents`, `.claude/commands` and `.claude/hooks` are neither named nor excepted, so by that list they fall under the protected `.claude` directory; that is a reading of the list, not a sentence, and no run was made (topic: claude/settings-and-scopes)
- **Whether the protected-path exception for `.claude/worktrees/` holds for a worktree nested under another worktree's `.claude/worktrees/` directory, or one made by `git worktree add` rather than by Claude Code.** Looked 2026-10-13 in the permission-modes page's Protected paths section (S-zcuxapgb) and the permissions page's Read and Edit rules (S-pmhgjvef): the list says "Claude's own git worktrees under `.claude/worktrees/`" and no page defines "own", nests one worktree in another or names a path made outside Claude Code. Observed since, 2026-10-11, in three runs of our own (headless `claude -p`, `--permission-mode dontAsk`, Claude Code version and OS not recorded): an `Edit` that an absolute `//path` allow rule named was denied for the owned files of a checkout whose path holds `.claude/worktrees` and for a scratch file at `.../.claude/worktrees/w/a.txt`, and made at `.../plain/a.txt`; the runs did not record whether the checkout or `w` was a worktree Claude Code made, nor whether the protected-path rule or the `//path` rule's matching denied it (`claude/settings-and-scopes.md`). Needs a stated rule, or a pinned-version run that writes into each case, by a `//path` rule and by `Edit(.claude/worktrees/**)`, under `acceptEdits` and `dontAsk`. [UNK] (topic: claude/settings-and-scopes)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: re-read the permission-modes list ("Claude's own git worktrees under `.claude/worktrees/`") and the worktrees page (a move by `EnterWorktree` to a path outside `.claude/worktrees/` asks approval; `--resume` re-enters a worktree "it created with git under `.claude/worktrees/`"): still no definition of "own", and nothing on a worktree nested in another's `.claude/worktrees/` or made by `git worktree add`. No run: it needs a worktree Claude Code made, one nested in it and one made by git, each written to under `acceptEdits` and `dontAsk` by a `//path` rule and by `Edit(.claude/worktrees/**)` (topic: claude/settings-and-scopes)
- **What a `claude -p --permission-mode dontAsk` run does with an output redirect (`> file`) to a path inside the working directory when no `Edit` allow rule exists.** Looked 2026-10-20 in the permissions page's Redirections section (S742, read on the raw page): it says the check covers the `Edit` allow and deny rules, protected paths and the working directories, and that `Bash(git commit *)` "allows the command, not the target"; the `dontAsk` row of the permission modes table (S742) says every call that would otherwise prompt is denied. Neither says whether a redirect target inside the working directory prompts in `default` mode, so the `dontAsk` outcome is an inference. Needs a stated rule, or a pinned-version `claude -p --permission-mode dontAsk --allowedTools "Bash(echo *)"` run of `echo x > scratch.txt` in a scratch directory, its `permission_denials` kept. [UNK] (topic: claude/settings-and-scopes)
  - Tried 2026-10-21 by a run on 2026-10-21 (Claude Code 2.1.303 on macOS, headless `claude -p --model haiku`, a scratch project, `--setting-sources project`; a run of ours, not a documentation statement), in a scratch git repository and with no allow rules: under both `--permission-mode default` and `dontAsk`, `echo x > out.txt` was denied (it is listed in `permission_denials` and the file was not created), as were `rm f.txt`, `git stash`, `git clone ../srcrepo ../cloneout` and `git branch newb`; `git diff`, `git show --stat HEAD`, `git stash list`, `git log --oneline` and `git status` ran. One run per mode (topic: claude/settings-and-scopes)
- **Which exceptions the permissions page names to the built-in read-only Bash command set, including `permissions.blockReadsOutsideWorkingDirectories` and auto mode's classifier.** Asked in a backlog research item (2026-10-13). First looked in the kb 2026-10-13, before the notes below: `claude/powershell-tool.md` stated the set as running "without a prompt in every mode" and named no exception, `claude/settings-and-scopes.md` named the setting only among what no mode auto-approves (reads outside the working directories, v2.1.257+), and no article listed the cases in which a set command still prompts or waits for the classifier. Since then the kb holds the answer: `claude/powershell-tool.md:37` names `permissions.blockReadsOutsideWorkingDirectories` and auto mode's classifier as the exceptions to "every mode" and lists the Manual-mode cases, which `claude/powershell-tool.md:38-41` give one by one (the UNC case at `claude/powershell-tool.md:33`); `claude/settings-and-scopes.md:78` holds the setting among what no mode auto-approves, and `claude/settings-keys.csv:7` its prompt-even-in-auto/`bypassPermissions` effect. [DOC S742] (topic: claude/settings-and-scopes)
  - Partly resolved 2026-10-13: live docs, not in the kb (permissions page, "Read-only commands", read 2026-10-13 via the claude-code-docs server: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions#read-only-commands). The set (`ls`, `cat`, `echo`, `pwd`, `head`, `tail`, `grep`, `find`, `wc`, `which`, `diff`, `stat`, `du`, `cd`, read-only forms of `git`) runs without a prompt in every mode "except as `permissions.blockReadsOutsideWorkingDirectories` changes for paths outside your working directories"; the page also says that "in auto mode, these commands can also wait for the classifier's review" (the permission-modes page, decision order step 2: in a session with server-side classifier review, read-only and sandboxed shell commands wait for that review and are blocked if it flags them; https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permission-modes). Settings reference: the setting makes the file tools (`Read`, `Grep`, `Glob`, `LSP`) refuse reads outside the working directories in every mode including `bypassPermissions`, v2.1.257 or later; the permission-modes page adds that with it on, recognized file-reading Bash commands prompt even in auto and `bypassPermissions` mode. In Manual mode a set command still prompts for: an unquoted glob on a command with write- or exec-capable flags (`find`, `sort`, `sed`, `git`), `docker` with a flag selecting another daemon (`-H`, `--context`, Podman's `--url`, `--connection`), `file` with `-m`/`--magic-file` or `-f`/`--files-from`, `ps` whose arguments could act as the `e` option (`ps auxe`, `ps aux -e`; the dashed-form check needs v2.1.290 or later), a network (UNC) path argument on Windows (also for the PowerShell tool), a write to a special shell variable such as `PATH` or `IFS`, a command the analysis cannot parse, and any command over 10,000 characters; and `cd` prompts when combined with `git` into a different directory, or with a redirect whose target cannot be resolved (`/dev/null` does not prompt). An `ask` or `deny` rule also makes a set command prompt, and the set is not configurable. Done 2026-10-13: `claude/powershell-tool.md:37` now names the setting and auto mode's classifier as the exceptions to "every mode" the page's first paragraph gives, and lists the further Manual-mode cases the section names (reworded 2026-10-13 from the live page, anchor re-taken). Done 2026-10-13: the `-p` denial and the command-by-command reading of the set are in `claude/settings-and-scopes.md` (Permission modes); the set's own members and the exceptions to "every mode" stay at `claude/powershell-tool.md:37`. Resolved 2026-10-13: the Manual-mode cases are four fact lines in `claude/powershell-tool.md` (Permission rules, after line 37: unquoted globs, `docker`/`file`/`ps` flags, special shell variables with unparseable and over-10,000-character commands, and the `cd` combinations; the UNC case is line 33), from the permissions page's Read-only commands section re-read 2026-10-13 (S742). (topic: claude/settings-and-scopes)
  - Resolved 2026-10-14: the entry's first paragraph now states the kb as it stands, and nothing in the question lacks a source. The exceptions to "every mode" are `claude/powershell-tool.md:37` (the setting and auto mode's classifier, from the permissions page, S742), the Manual-mode cases `claude/powershell-tool.md:38-41` and the UNC case `claude/powershell-tool.md:33`; the setting's own effect is `claude/settings-and-scopes.md:78` and `claude/settings-keys.csv:7`. Which `git` subcommands count as read-only forms stays open in its own entry below.
- **Which `git` subcommands and flags the built-in read-only Bash set counts as "read-only forms of `git`", and so which of `git stash`, `git clone`, `rm`, `git diff` and `git show` a headless `claude -p` session in permission mode `default` refuses when no `permissions.allow` rule names them.** Asked in a backlog research item (2026-10-13). Looked in the kb 2026-10-13: no article lists the set's `git` forms. Looked 2026-10-13 in the live docs, not in the kb (claude-code-docs server): the permissions page, "Read-only commands" (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions#read-only-commands) names `diff` and "read-only forms of `git`" and no subcommand; the headless page (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/headless) says that in a `-p` run with no host a request that would prompt is "denied either way"; the permission-modes page (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permission-modes) lists `rm` among what `acceptEdits` adds, so `rm` is outside the set and is denied in `default`; the changelog (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/changelog) has an entry adding `git log` and `git show` flags "in read-only mode" and one fixing `Read()` deny rules for `git diff` file operands, which read `git show` and `git diff` as in the set; no page names `git stash` or `git clone` as read-only, and the settings reference describes a `git clone` as writing "a whole tree of files". Reading, not a run: `git diff` and `git show` run, and `rm`, `git clone` and `git stash` that pushes, pops or drops are denied; whether `git stash list` or `git stash show` run is not stated. Needs a pinned-version `claude -p --permission-mode default` run of each command with no allow rule, or a page that enumerates the read-only `git` forms. [UNK] (topic: claude/settings-and-scopes)
  - Tried 2026-10-21 by the runs of the entry above (a run on 2026-10-21 (Claude Code 2.1.303 on macOS, headless `claude -p --model haiku`, a scratch project, `--setting-sources project`; a run of ours, not a documentation statement), no allow rules, `--permission-mode default` and `dontAsk`): `git diff`, `git show --stat HEAD`, `git stash list`, `git log --oneline` and `git status` ran; `git stash` (a push), `git clone`, `rm` and `git branch newb` were denied. The runs show these ten commands only, not the whole read-only git set (topic: claude/settings-and-scopes)
- **Why the permissions page exempts a `Glob` rule passed in `--allowedTools` from the startup warning for path rules the file checks never consult.** Asked in a backlog research item (2026-10-13). The permissions page (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions, Read and Edit path rules, read 2026-10-13 via the claude-code-docs server) says Claude Code accepts a path rule written for `Write`, `NotebookEdit`, `Glob` or `MultiEdit`, never consults it and warns at startup, "except for a `Glob` rule passed in `--allowedTools`", from v2.1.210; the fact is at `claude/settings-and-scopes.md` under Path patterns in Read and Edit rules. The page gives no reason, and the errors page section it links for the warning ("is not matched by file permission checks") was not found in the docs server's copy of the errors page on 2026-10-13, so the warning text and the reason for the exemption are unread. Needs that section, or a `claude -p --allowedTools "Glob(docs/**)"` run against a pinned version. [UNK] (topic: claude/settings-and-scopes)
  - Narrowed 2026-10-21: the permissions page's sentence is unchanged (a path rule for `Write`, `NotebookEdit`, `Glob` or `MultiEdit` is accepted, never consulted and warned about at startup "except for a `Glob` rule passed in `--allowedTools`") and a text search of the English docs for "is not matched by file permission checks" finds nothing, so the reason is still unstated. Two headless runs on Claude Code 2.1.303, one with `--allowedTools "Glob(docs/**)"` and one with `"Write(docs/**)"`, exited 0 and printed nothing on stderr, so a `-p` run shows no warning text for either; where the warning is shown is not known Backed by `claude/settings-and-scopes.md:106`, `claude/settings-and-scopes.md:107` (topic: claude/settings-and-scopes)

The page states no reason for the exemption. (topic: claude/settings-and-scopes)
