## claude/ci-and-headless

- **The `--tools` flag of `claude -p`, which restricts the built-in tools that are available.** Asked in a logged lookup ("claude -p --tools flag restrict built-in tools list allowedTools"). Looked in the kb 2026-10-08: `claude/cli-flags.csv:10` says `--allowedTools` does not restrict which tools are available and points to `--tools` for that, but the table has no row for `--tools` (its value syntax, `""` for none, `default`). Needs the CLI reference row (`/kb-research`). [UNK] (topic: claude/ci-and-headless)
  - Resolved 2026-10-11: the CLI reference's `--tools` row was read on the raw page and added as `claude/cli-flags.csv:24` (S1824): `""` disables every built-in tool, `default` is the default set, a list names tools, and MCP tools are untouched by it. (topic: claude/ci-and-headless)
- **Whether a result event's `modelUsage` names a model by its dated id or its alias, and in which form a subagent's Haiku calls appear.** Asked in a backlog research item (2026-10-10). Looked in the kb and in the Claude Code docs 2026-10-10 (`agent-sdk/python`, `agent-sdk/typescript`, `agent-sdk/cost-tracking`, `model-config`, `sub-agents`): the docs call the keys "model names", say `canonicalModel` may differ from "the raw model string the entry is keyed by", and give no example or rule for an alias, so the form is not documented. One observation exists (Claude Code 2.1.283, `--model haiku` keyed `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`; see the article's Facts); none for Claude Code v2.1.293 or later, where `haiku` resolves to `claude-haiku-5-5`, for a subagent's own entry, for a pinned `--model claude-haiku-4-5` or for another provider. Needs one capped real `claude -p --output-format json` run with a Haiku subagent on the current version, its `modelUsage` keys kept. [UNK] (topic: claude/ci-and-headless)
  - Partly resolved 2026-10-11: a session's own entry for a pinned `--model claude-haiku-5-5` on Claude Code 2.1.296 was keyed `claude-haiku-5-5` (`agents/headless-agent-runtimes.md:64`, DER); the alias form on v2.1.293 or later, a subagent's own entry and a pinned `claude-haiku-4-5` are still unobserved. (topic: claude/ci-and-headless)
  - Partly resolved 2026-10-13: the repo's own benchmark rows hold the key of a pinned undated `--model claude-haiku-4-5` on Claude Code 2.1.284: `kb/_self/reports/benchmarks.csv`, scenario `route-by-verdict`, record of 2026-09-29 (commit `15fb0d9`), arm `router-pinned`, whose reader `agent_bench.route` starts as `claude -p --model claude-haiku-4-5`; its cases `s1_fact` and `s3_multi` (2 runs each) carry `claude-haiku-4-5`, undated, in the `model` column, which `bench_core.bench_rows` fills from the costliest `modelUsage` key of the case's first run, so it is the key itself, not an alias resolved by the harness. The same record's `web-sonnet-5-5` case `s3_multi` and the `new-model` record's `web-sonnet-5` case `o1_offkb` (2026-09-28) have the dated `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` as their costliest key, so both forms occur on 2.1.284. Not evidence: the 2.1.293 pool rows of arm `haiku-4-5` read `claude-haiku-4-5` too, but their `model` column is the model resolved from the stream's requests, and the `subagents` rows of 2.1.282 take the request's model, not a `modelUsage` key. Written 2026-10-13 to `claude/ci-and-headless.md:116-117` with the source row S-e5rrs4ee (the benchmark rows pinned at a commit); the `partial` rows of 2.1.296 (2026-10-12, `--model haiku`) are keyed `claude-haiku-5-5`, so the alias form on 2.1.296 is observed too. A subagent's own entry (no row keeps it), the alias form on 2.1.293 to 2.1.295 and another provider are still unobserved. (topic: claude/ci-and-headless)
- **Whether `claude -p --json-schema` has a retry count or a size limit for its structured result.** Asked in a backlog research item (2026-10-11), for a batch judge that returns one verdict per review item. Looked in the Claude Code docs 2026-10-11 (`headless`, `cli-reference`, `agent-sdk/structured-outputs`) and the Claude API's `build-with-claude/structured-outputs`: the Agent SDK page says the result is an `error_max_structured_output_retries` error once the retry limit is reached and names no number; no page states a limit on `structured_output`'s size or on the items of an array in it; the API page's schema limits (20 strict tools, 24 optional parameters, 16 union parameters, a 180-second compile timeout) are stated for the API's strict schemas, and the docs do not say whether `--json-schema` passes the schema through them. Three capped runs of 2026-10-11 returned 10 and 11 results in one `structured_output` without error (`agents/headless-agent-runtimes.md:65`); a larger list is unobserved. Needs a capped run with a list near the sizes planned, or a statement in the docs. [UNK] (topic: claude/ci-and-headless)
  - Partly resolved 2026-10-21: two capped runs (Claude Code 2.1.303, Haiku, no tools, `--json-schema` of an array of objects with an integer, an enum and a string) returned 60 and then 300 elements whole in one `structured_output`, `subtype` success, two turns, so lists far above the 10 and 11 of 2026-10-11 pass; the Agent SDK structured-outputs page still names `error_max_structured_output_retries` without a retry count and now says a model-fallback retraction can end a run with the same error. No page states a size limit, and no run reached one; the runs are written as a derived fact in `claude/ci-and-headless.md:141`, not as a documentation statement (topic: claude/ci-and-headless)
- **Whether a Claude Code subscription login can authorize a call to the Message Batches API or the Messages API.** Asked in a backlog research item (2026-10-11), for a clone with no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`. Looked in the Claude API overview, the batch processing page and Claude Code's `legal-and-compliance` page 2026-10-11: the credentials the API names are an API key and a Workload Identity Federation access token, the batch examples send `x-api-key`, and the legal page limits subscription OAuth to Claude Code and Anthropic's own applications; no page says in so many words that a subscription token is refused by the API. `agents/headless-agent-runtimes.md:69` records the derived route (`claude -p --json-schema`) and not a statement. Needs an official sentence, or a rejected call with a subscription token, which this kb does not try (the legal page restricts the use). [UNK] (topic: claude/ci-and-headless)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: a web search for a statement that a Claude Code subscription token is refused by the Messages API found no Anthropic page; third-party posts quote the API answering "OAuth authentication is currently not supported" to a Claude Code token (a feature request closed in April 2026 without a visible answer) and quote Anthropic's usage policy limiting subscription OAuth to Claude Code and native apps, with Console API keys for anything built on Claude. No rejected call was made here, since the legal page restricts that use. Still needs an Anthropic sentence (topic: claude/ci-and-headless)
- **Whether `claude -p --setting-sources ""` (an empty value) is accepted and loads no settings source.** Asked in a backlog research item (2026-10-19), for a bare comparison agent that must load no user, project or local settings. Looked in the kb and in the Claude Code docs 2026-10-19 (`cli-reference`, `memory`, `debug-your-config`, `mcp`, `skills`, `agent-sdk/claude-code-features`): the CLI pages show only list values (`--setting-sources user`, a list that leaves out `local`, `project` or `user`); the Agent SDK page states that `settingSources: []` disables user, project and local settings, and that managed policy settings, `~/.claude.json` and auto memory are read whatever it says. No CLI page states the empty value. Needs one capped `claude -p` run with `--setting-sources ""` in an empty directory, its `system` init message kept, or a sentence in the CLI reference. [UNK] (topic: claude/ci-and-headless)
  - Narrowed 2026-10-21 by a run on 2026-10-21 (Claude Code 2.1.303 on macOS, headless `claude -p --model haiku`, a scratch project; a run of ours, not a documentation statement): `claude -p --setting-sources "" "reply ok"` exited 0 and answered; its `system` init message lists only the three built-in plugins (none of the user's installed plugins), no MCP server and permission mode `default`, with the bundled skills and agents still present. So the empty value is accepted and loads no user, project or local source; the claim that no source loads is read from the plugin and server lists of that one init message. Written as a derived fact in `claude/ci-and-headless.md:142`; still open: a sentence in the CLI reference that states the empty value (topic: claude/ci-and-headless)
- **Whether a headless `claude -p` run's `total_cost_usd` includes the web search fee (USD 10 per 1,000 searches on the API).** Asked in a backlog research item (2026-10-19), for a comparison of agents that use `WebSearch` with agents that use kb tools, priced from token counts. Looked in the kb and in the Claude Code docs 2026-10-19 (`agent-sdk/cost-tracking`, `costs`, `monitoring-usage`, `headless`): the `claude/ci-and-headless` article records the API fee, and the cost-tracking page says only that "per-request fees such as web search aren't multiplied" by the data-residency rate, which neither says the fee is counted nor that it is not; the result's `usage` has no search-count field in the keys the kb recorded. Needs a capped run with one `WebSearch` call whose `total_cost_usd` is compared with its counts priced at list. Until then a figure priced from the four token counts leaves out any search fee. [UNK] (topic: claude/ci-and-headless)
  - Narrowed 2026-10-21 by a run on 2026-10-21 (Claude Code 2.1.303 on macOS, headless `claude -p --model haiku`, a scratch project, `--setting-sources project`; a run of ours, not a documentation statement), with `--allowedTools WebSearch` and one search: `modelUsage.<model>.webSearchRequests` was 1 and `total_cost_usd` was 0.01528191; the four token counts priced at the list prices in `claude/models.csv` for this model (USD 0.10 and 0.50 per MTok, cache read 0.01, 1-hour cache write 0.20) come to 0.00528191, a difference of exactly 0.0100, one search at USD 10 per 1,000. So `total_cost_usd` includes the web search fee. In the same result `usage.server_tool_use.web_search_requests` was 0, so the count is read from `modelUsage`, not from `usage`. Written as a derived fact in `claude/ci-and-headless.md:143`; still open: a documentation sentence that `total_cost_usd` counts the search fee, and a run on another model (topic: claude/ci-and-headless)

## claude/hooks

- **Whether a `SessionStart` command hook cancelled at its `timeout` shows the user a notice, and whether `onFailure` applies to it.** Looked 2026-10-19 in the hooks reference (S743): the Timeouts and "Block the action when a hook fails" sections name `PreToolUse`, `UserPromptSubmit` (a transcript notice naming the hook and the timeout), `PreModelSwitch` and `SessionEnd`, and say a cancelled hook's output is discarded, but give no sentence for `SessionStart`. Needs a run of a `SessionStart` hook that sleeps past its `timeout`. [UNK] (topic: claude/hooks)
  - Tried 2026-10-21 by a run on 2026-10-21 (Claude Code 2.1.303 on macOS, headless `claude -p --model haiku`, a scratch project, `--setting-sources project`; a run of ours, not a documentation statement), with `"timeout": 1` on a `SessionStart` hook that sleeps 3 seconds and `--include-hook-events`: the stream's `hook_response` for `SessionStart:startup` has `outcome` `cancelled`, `exit_code` `1` and empty `output`; the hook wrote nothing, and the run went on to `UserPromptSubmit` and finished normally. No notice text is in the stream. Whether the interactive screen shows a notice, and `onFailure`, need an interactive session (topic: claude/hooks)
- **The shape of `tool_response` for an MCP tool call in `PostToolUse`.** The hooks reference documents `tool_response` as "the result it returned" with a per-tool schema, and shows it for Write, Bash, Agent and ExitPlanMode, but not for an MCP tool (searched `code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks.md` for `tool_response` and `MCP`, 2026-09-28). The query log capture reads it defensively (a string, a content-block list or an object). [UNK] (topic: claude/hooks)
  - Tried 2026-10-21 by a run on 2026-10-21 (Claude Code 2.1.303 on macOS, headless `claude -p --model haiku`, a scratch project, `--setting-sources project`; a run of ours, not a documentation statement), with a stdio MCP server of ours: for a tool result that carries `structuredContent`, the `PostToolUse` `tool_response` was that object (`{"echoed":"hello"}`); for one with `content` only, it was the content-block list (`[{"type":"text","text":"echo:hi"}]`). The hooks reference's examples were not re-read (topic: claude/hooks)

(topic: claude/hooks)

(topic: claude/hooks)

A parser of the files is at best derived from observed files, never `DOC`; the documented alternatives are OpenTelemetry `claude_code.api_request`, the SDK result `modelUsage` and `SubagentStop.agent_transcript_path`. [UNK] (topic: claude/hooks)

- **Whether a `claude -p` run waits for its `SessionStart` hooks before the prompt's turn, and whether `UserPromptSubmit` hooks fire for the prompt a `-p` run is given.** Looked 2026-10-07 in the hooks reference (SessionStart and UserPromptSubmit sections, `rg` for `non-interactive`, `-p` and `headless`), the headless page, the hooks guide, the CLI reference and the Agent SDK hooks page. The wait for `SessionStart` hooks is stated for an interactive start, `--continue`/`--resume` at launch and `/clear` only; `UserPromptSubmit` has no sentence about `-p`. The kb's facts are derived from the stream-json event order (hook events ahead of `system/init`), `initialUserMessage` and the rule that `-p` runs settings hooks without `--bare`. Needs a sentence on one of those pages, or a recorded run of `claude -p` with a `SessionStart` hook that sleeps and a `UserPromptSubmit` hook that writes a file. [UNK] (topic: claude/hooks)

- **Whether the normal end of a `claude -p` run (the prompt's result printed, the process exiting with code 0) runs `SessionEnd` hooks.** Looked 2026-10-07 in the hooks reference (`SessionEnd` section, the event table, the timeout rows, the lifecycle diagram text), the headless page (SIGTERM section) and the Agent SDK hooks page. The pages give the SIGTERM case only (`SessionEnd` hooks run before exit, no other hook) and the reasons `clear`, `resume`, `logout`, `prompt_input_exit`, `other`; none says that a `-p` run ends a session in that sense at a normal exit. Needs a sentence on one of those pages, or a recorded run of `claude -p` with a `SessionEnd` hook that appends its input to a file, repeated enough times to show whether it is reliable. [UNK] (topic: claude/hooks)

Still no sentence on a normal `-p` exit. New and related: `SessionEnd` never produces a `hook_started` event under `--include-hook-events`, so a recorded run cannot read it from the stream and needs a hook that writes a file (`claude/hooks.md:98`). Needs the same sentence or recorded run as above. (topic: claude/hooks)

Still no sentence on a normal `-p` exit. Two near misses, neither an answer: the Agent SDK hooks page says `SessionEnd` callbacks run "during shutdown" under the 1.5-second budget and that hooks "may not fire" at the `max_turns` limit, with no event named and no `-p` run named; the hooks guide's "Clean up on session end" example says its `clear`-matcher hook "only fires on the `clear` reason, set when you run `/clear`, not on normal exits", which describes that matcher, not a `-p` run (the first two are in `claude/hooks.md:94`). A launcher or a landing step must not count on a normal `-p` exit running `SessionEnd`; only a recorded run of `claude -p` with a hook that appends its input to a file, repeated enough to show whether it is reliable, or a sentence on one of those pages settles it. (topic: claude/hooks)

- **What happens to a `SessionEnd` hook's process, and to processes it started, when the 1.5-second budget runs out or Claude Code is stopped by SIGKILL, SIGHUP, a crash or a `--max-budget-usd` stop.** Looked 2026-10-07 in the hooks reference (`SessionEnd`, timeouts, async hooks), the headless page (SIGTERM section), the CLI reference, the changelog and the self-hosted runner reference. The pages say the hook is "cancelled" (the changelog once said "killed") at the budget, that a `-p` run kills async hooks still running at teardown and that work meant to outlive the run starts as a fully detached process, that SIGTERM runs `SessionEnd` hooks only, and that the self-hosted runner force-kills the Claude process after a grace period; none says whether the cancel reaches a child the hook started or one it detached, or whether `SessionEnd` runs at all on the other stops. Needs a sentence on one of those pages, or a recorded run with a hook that starts a detached child writing a file after the budget (and one run each for SIGKILL and SIGHUP). SIGKILL, SIGHUP, a crash and a `--max-budget-usd` stop were not run (topic: claude/hooks)

## python/imports-and-modules

- **What happens when a file run as a script is also imported by another module under its own name** (whether it loads a second time as a separate module, which matters for a facade script that its helper modules might import back): the tutorial, the import reference and the `__main__` page at v3.14.7 say only that a script's `__name__` is `"__main__"`. Looked 2026-09-29. Needs the import system reference on `sys.modules['__main__']` or a run on a planted pair. (topic: python/imports-and-modules)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21: the import reference's section on `__main__` (3.14.7) says that even where `__main__` corresponds to an importable module the two are "still considered distinct modules", because the `if __name__ == "__main__":` blocks run only when the module populates `__main__` [DOC S-lebxvs2r]; recorded in `python/imports-and-modules.md` (topic: python/imports-and-modules)

## gitlab/automated-merge-requests

- The exit code of `glab auth status` when the host is not signed in: the generated command page (S-d6wuvfpd, gitlab-org/cli @37ebe99d `docs/source/auth/status.md`) describes the checks and flags but no exit status (2026-09-28). [UNK] (topic: gitlab/automated-merge-requests)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21 from code: at gitlab-org/cli tag v1.124.0, internal/commands/auth/status/status.go (func run) returns a plain error when no host is signed in ("no GitLab instances have been authenticated with glab; run glab auth login"), when --hostname names an unsigned host ("<host> has not been authenticated with glab"), and, after printing, when any configured host fails the token or API check ("could not authenticate to one or more of the configured GitLab instances"); with --output json it returns cmdutils.SilentError instead, which internal/cmdutils/errors.go describes as "exit Code 1 without any error messaging". cmd/glab/main.go exits with ExitError.Code if the error is an ExitError, else os.Exit(1), so all these paths exit 1. docs/source/auth/status.md at v1.124.0 still states no exit status (the page lacks the word exit). Not run (no glab on this host). Written to gitlab/automated-merge-requests.md:104. (topic: gitlab/automated-merge-requests)
- A failure fingerprint for deduplicating automatic bugs from red pipelines: GitLab's and GitHub's docs describe the job and check-run status fields (already cited here) but neither documents a signature or grouping key for a repeated CI failure, so the fingerprint scheme is the project's own design, not a vendor fact. Looked in docs.gitlab.com and the kb 2026-09-29. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/automated-merge-requests)
  - Tried 2026-10-21, still open: a docs.gitlab.com search for a failure signature, fingerprint or grouping key for repeated CI job failures found none. Pages read via search results: CI/CD jobs (grouped jobs are a display grouping only), Unit test reports (test failures are classed new, existing or resolved against the target-branch baseline pipeline, at test level only), CI/CD analytics, Fix CI/CD Pipeline Flow (AI flow that can be told in AGENTS.md how to treat repeated failures) and the Orbit cookbook (groups failing jobs by job name and project, an aggregation query). No vendor fact gives a fingerprint scheme, so the scheme stays the project's own design. The job API's failure_reason field (already cited) remains the only vendor-defined failure key. (topic: gitlab/automated-merge-requests)
- **The values of the `failure_reason` field of a job in the GitLab jobs API** (`script_failure`, `ci_quota_exceeded` and the others). Asked in a logged lookup ("GitLab jobs API failure_reason field values script_failure ci_quota_exceeded allow_failure manual job status"). Looked in the kb 2026-10-08: `gitlab/automated-merge-requests.md` has the job and pipeline status values and the composite status rule, and no file of the kb names `failure_reason`. Needs the jobs API page or the `CommitStatus` failure reasons in the GitLab source (`/kb-research`). [UNK] (topic: gitlab/automated-merge-requests)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21: the jobs API page (doc/api/jobs.md) only shows example values (script_failure, stuck_or_timeout_failure) and gives no list. The full list is the Enums::Ci::CommitStatus.failure_reasons hash in app/models/concerns/enums/ci/commit_status.rb @29a9622b (unknown_failure, script_failure, api_failure, stuck_or_timeout_failure, runner_system_failure, missing_dependency_failure, runner_unsupported, stale_schedule, job_execution_timeout, archived_failure, unmet_prerequisites, scheduler_failure, data_integrity_failure, forward_deployment_failure, user_blocked, project_deleted, ci_quota_exceeded, pipeline_loop_detected, no_matching_runner, trace_size_exceeded, builds_disabled, environment_creation_failure, deployment_rejected, failed_outdated_deployment_job, ... and further bridge/runner reasons). The CI keyword reference doc/ci/yaml/_index.md (retry:when) documents a subset in prose. The set changes between releases (new reasons 19.1). Written to gitlab/automated-merge-requests.md:105. (topic: gitlab/automated-merge-requests)
- **Which Enterprise-only `detailed_merge_status` values (`not_approved`, `merge_request_blocked`, `status_checks_must_pass`, `requested_changes`, `jira_association_missing`, `locked_paths`, `title_regex`, `security_policy_violations`) can come from a `checking` result, and so clear by waiting:** the values and the community edition's checks are in the article; of the Enterprise checks only `security_policy_pipeline_check` and the `approvals_syncing` rule were read in `gitlab-org/gitlab` @9f1632e2 (its `ee/` files are source-available), so the other eight are classed as needing an action from the page's descriptions alone. Looked 2026-10-10 in `doc/api/merge_requests.md` (it marks no value as resolving alone) and the community edition's `app/`. Needs the `ee/app/services/merge_requests/mergeability/` check files read one by one. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/automated-merge-requests)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21 by reading all eight EE check files in ee/app/services/merge_requests/mergeability/ @29a9622b. Only two can return checking: check_approved_service.rb returns checking while merge_request.temporarily_unapproved? (a short approval-rules resync; the detailed status then becomes approvals_syncing, via mergeability/detailed_merge_status_service.rb), and check_security_policy_violations_service.rb returns checking when approval rules are out of sync (it schedules a resync) or while running_scan_result_policy_violations exist. The other six (check_blocked_by_other_mrs, check_external_status_checks_passed, check_jira_status, check_merge_request_title_regex, check_path_locks, check_requested_changes) return only inactive, success or failure, so merge_request_blocked, status_checks_must_pass, jira_association_missing, title_regex, locked_paths and requested_changes need an action. Not run on an instance. Written to gitlab/automated-merge-requests.md:106 and gitlab/automated-merge-requests.md:107. (topic: gitlab/automated-merge-requests)
- **What a GitHub `mergeStateStatus` of `BLOCKED` or `UNSTABLE` is made of, and whether `gh pr view --json statusCheckRollup` pages past its first 100 contexts:** the GraphQL schema gives `BLOCKED` as "The merge is blocked" and `UNSTABLE` as "Mergeable with non-passing commit status", and does not say whether a required check that is still pending gives `BLOCKED` or `UNSTABLE`; `gh`'s field query asks for `contexts(first:100)` and the pull request view's code that follows `pageInfo` was not read. Looked 2026-10-10 in the GraphQL schema, the `gh pr view` and `gh pr checks` manuals and `cli/cli` v2.102.0 `api/`. Needs GitHub's mergeability documentation for the enum or a run on a planted pull request. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/automated-merge-requests)
  - Tried 2026-10-21, still open (read in cli/cli code, not written to an article): the pagination half is answered. In cli/cli v2.102.0, pkg/cmd/pr/shared/finder.go calls preloadPrChecks when the fields include statusCheckRollup; it follows contexts pageInfo.hasNextPage/endCursor with a loop and appends every page, so gh pr view --json statusCheckRollup is not cut at 100. The enum half stays open: docs.github.com public/fpt/schema.docs.graphql still says only BLOCKED "The merge is blocked." and UNSTABLE "Mergeable with non-passing commit status."; no GitHub page says whether a pending required check gives BLOCKED or UNSTABLE. gh pr merge (pkg/cmd/pr/merge/merge.go v2.102.0) treats BLOCKED as "the base branch policy prohibits the merge" and UNSTABLE as immediately mergeable. A planted pull request would answer the rest. (topic: gitlab/automated-merge-requests)

## claude/plugins

- **What Claude Code does when it adds or refreshes a marketplace whose repository holds a submodule the user cannot read (a failed add or refresh, the submodule skipped, or the old checkout kept), and whether `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA` is touched by a failed update.** Looked 2026-10-19 in the Claude Code docs: a search for "submodule" over the docs filesystem found it only in the changelog (2.1.7, 2.1.74 and 2.1.295 fixes), the worktree pages and the trust page, and the private-marketplace, host-marketplace and troubleshooting pages (S-havfntgx, S-pelzabjq, S-wa4ujzvn) describe credential failures of the marketplace repository only; the docs say `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA}` "survives updates" (S-i7if5i7z). Needs a run of `claude plugin marketplace add` and `update` against a throwaway marketplace whose submodule is unreadable. [UNK] (topic: claude/plugins)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: a text search of the plugin-marketplaces, discover-plugins, host-marketplace and plugins troubleshooting pages for "submodule" finds no hit, so the pages still describe only credential failures of the marketplace repository itself. No run: it needs a throwaway marketplace with an unreadable submodule and `claude plugin marketplace add` and `update` (topic: claude/plugins)
- **CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA directory location plugin-dir inline** The kb was asked this in a logged lookup of 2026-09-28, and no article answered it (query log finding F-b3b7f7b7c766). Looked in the kb 2026-09-28: `rag.py pack` gives `good`, with this topic in the lead. Needs an official source that states it (`/kb-research`, or the query log's opt-in research). (topic: claude/plugins)
  - Resolved 2026-09-28: `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA}` is `~/.claude/plugins/data/<id>/` with non-alphanumeric characters other than `_` and `-` replaced by `-` (S-i7if5i7z), so a `--plugin-dir` plugin (`<name>@inline`, S-toe7z3kj) uses `data/<name>-inline/`; answer QK-claude-plugin-data-plugin-loaded-plugin. (topic: claude/plugins)

## agents/codebase-mapping

- **Machine-readable Gradle dependency output.** The Gradle 9.8.0 Viewing Dependencies page documents only the text tree of `gradle dependencies` (searched for "json" and "machine", 2026-09-28); a JSON map likely needs the Tooling API or a custom task, not read yet. (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
  - Resolved 2026-09-29: the Project Report plugin writes `dependencies.txt` and an HTML report, no JSON (Gradle 9.8.0 source, S-hssujrgr); the Tooling API returns dependency models as objects from a JVM client but evaluates the build to do so (S-a7wduz2b). A JSON dependency map therefore needs a custom task or a Tooling API client, both running build code; no official page documents a ready JSON output. (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
- **`go vet -json` output schema.** cmd/go at go1.27.1 documents the flag ("emit JSON output") but not the schema; the cmd/vet or `golang.org/x/tools/go/analysis/unitchecker` docs were not read (2026-09-28). (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
  - Resolved 2026-09-29: read in golang.org/x/tools v0.50.0: a tree of package ID to analyzer to a diagnostic list or `{"error"}`, diagnostic fields `category`, `posn`, `end`, `message`, `suggested_fixes`, `related` (S-nkxy23mu, S-cltv4idz); a CODE fact, not a documented schema, since cmd/vet documents only the flag. (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
- **`npm ls --json` output shape.** The npm-ls page at v11.20.0 describes the tree but no JSON schema (2026-09-28). (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
  - Resolved 2026-09-29: read in npm v11.20.0 `lib/commands/ls.js`: nested `dependencies` objects with `version`, `resolved`, `overridden`, and `missing`, `invalid`, `extraneous`, `problems` markers; a CODE fact (S-vstmwlcx). (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
- **TypeScript 7 native `tsc` and `--showConfig`/`--listFilesOnly`/`--explainFiles`.** The TypeScript website pages (commit 6556b08) are not versioned by release; whether the native 7.x compiler supports the same flags was not checked (2026-09-28). (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
  - Resolved 2026-09-29: npm `typescript` 7.0.2 is the native compiler; its source at typescript-go tag `typescript/v7.0.2` declares `showConfig`, `listFilesOnly`, `listFiles` and `explainFiles` (S-sipjyeoi, S-gfjnysog). It differs from the handbook in one way: files on the command line with a `tsconfig.json` present is an error unless `--ignoreConfig` is given (see `_conflicts.md`). (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
- **Dev container `image` pinned by digest.** The devcontainer.json reference (spec @c95ffee) names a registry image but says nothing about `@sha256:` digests for `image`; only Features have digests in the lock file (2026-09-28). (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
  - Tried 2026-09-29 (partly resolved): Docker documents `NAME[:TAG|@DIGEST]` and pulling by digest as pinning (S-cx5yd4ae); the Dev Container reference still says nothing about a digest in `image` (re-read at the pinned commit, S-223vfyli). Whether devcontainer tools accept `@sha256:` in `image` needs the reference implementation's tests or a lab; left open. (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
  - Resolved 2026-09-29 (from code, not a documented promise): the Dev Container CLI at commit 3e363f6 passes `image` unchanged to `docker image inspect`, a registry lookup and `docker pull`, and its reference parser accepts `@sha256:` digests (S-kxcc7a7a, S-zelwtfks); the spec page stays silent, so a digest-pinned `image` is supported by the reference implementation only. (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
- **Minimum SDK for `dotnet package list --vulnerable`.** The dotnet-package-list page ties "9.0.300" to `<AuditSources>` in a sentence that may or may not date the option itself (2026-09-28). (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
  - Resolved 2026-09-29: the NuGet auditing page's feature table gives `dotnet list package --vulnerable` from NuGet 5.9 and the .NET 5 SDK (5.0.200); `auditSources` support in that command starts at NuGet 6.14 and the .NET 9.0.300 SDK, which is what the dotnet page's "9.0.300" dates (S-r6oc37x2). (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
- **An official page for `MSBUILDDISABLENUGETSDKRESOLVER`.** The variable's effect is known only from the NuGet SDK resolver's code (value `1` disables it, S-7j4xac2a); no documentation states it. (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
  - Tried 2026-09-29: Microsoft Learn search for the variable (returns only MSB4236, MSB4271 and other MSBuild error pages), the "Use MSBuild project SDKs" page (S-e54zbsgw) and the MSBuild command-line reference (S-5mnhlz54); a GitHub code search finds it only in NuGet.Client and dotnet/dotnet sources and their string resources. Needs a NuGet or MSBuild docs page (for example an MSBuild environment-variables page) that names it. (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: the Learn site search (`provider.py search learn`) for the variable found no hit, a web search found no page that names it, and the `dotnet/msbuild` environment-variables wiki page does not list it; a GitHub code search needs a login. Still needs a NuGet or MSBuild docs page that names it (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
- **Which hosts honour global.json `sdk.paths` besides the `dotnet` CLI.** The global.json page (S-zihpu7lj) says the paths apply to commands that engage the .NET SDK and not to an apphost, `dotnet app.dll` or `dotnet exec`; it does not say whether `MSBuild.exe`, Visual Studio or other MSBuild API hosts, whose .NET SDK resolver calls hostfxr's `hostfxr_resolve_sdk2` (S-42ml7nxg), apply them too. (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
  - Tried 2026-09-29: the global.json page, the "Use MSBuild project SDKs" page and the SDK 10.0.401 resolver wrapper code; hostfxr's SDK resolution (dotnet/runtime `src/native/corehost/fxr`) was not read. Needs that code at the runtime tag shipped with SDK 10.0.401, or a page that lists the hosts. (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
  - Narrowed 2026-10-21: read in dotnet/runtime `main` (not the tag shipped with SDK 10.0.401): `sdk_resolver::get_search_paths` in `src/native/corehost/fxr/sdk_resolver.cpp` takes the custom `paths` of the nearest global.json, and `hostfxr_resolve_sdk2` in `hostfxr.cpp` builds its resolver from that file, so a host that calls `hostfxr_resolve_sdk2`, as the MSBuild .NET SDK resolver does (S-42ml7nxg), gets the custom paths from hostfxr itself. No Learn page lists the hosts. Still needs the same files at the runtime tag of SDK 10.0.401 (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
- **cargo metadata --no-deps invokes rustc .cargo/config.toml build.rustc** The kb was asked this in a logged lookup of 2026-09-29, and no article answered it (query log finding F-68c59a20a679). Looked in the kb 2026-09-29: `rag.py pack` gives `weak`, with this topic in the lead. Needs an official source that states it (`/kb-research`, or the query log's opt-in research). (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
  - Resolved 2026-10-08: query log finding F-68c59a20a679. The Cargo book (S-7jjocmag, S-53fkmfzh) documents `build.rustc` and `RUSTC` but not which commands use them; the Cargo source at 797e8a9 settles `cargo metadata`: with `--no-deps` it runs no `rustc` and ignores `build.rustc` (S-ubjrtutg, S-oplh5k3d), without it `rustc -vV` and target-info queries run through `RUSTC` or `build.rustc` (S-gkpddy5i, S-trq5hdxv, S-f6y5we3c). CODE facts, not a documented promise. (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
- **Cost of a `tokenize` pass and of `ast.walk` against source size.** The CPython 3.14.7 `tokenize`, `token` and `ast` pages (S-k6rik7wf, S-ot3h76t5, S-ci5jq2sq) describe the token and node types but state no timing or complexity (read 2026-10-14). Needs a measurement on the tree in question, not an official page. (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: no official page states a cost; lab on this repository's own Python files with CPython 3.13.2 (not 3.14.7), one run on one machine: `ast.parse` took 0.35 s, parse plus `ast.walk` 0.52 s and `tokenize` 0.26 s for the whole set, so a walk adds about half the parse time and tokenizing is cheaper than parsing. Still needs a measurement on the tree in question, which is a lab result and not a page (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
- **The `lineno` of a decorated function or class in `ast`.** The `ast` page at CPython v3.14.7 (S-ci5jq2sq) states the `lineno` fields but not whether a decorated `FunctionDef` or `ClassDef` starts at its first decorator or at `def` or `class` (read 2026-10-26). (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
  - Resolved 2026-10-26 (from code, not a documented promise): `_PyPegen_function_def_decorators` and `_PyPegen_class_def_decorators` at v3.14.7 (S-zhmvdb3z) build the decorated node from the undecorated node's position, so it starts at `def`, `async` or `class`; one run on Python 3.13.2 agrees. (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)
- **Cost of `symtable.symtable` against `ast.parse` for a whole repository.** The `symtable` page at CPython v3.14.7 (S-gcxnhiij) states no timing or complexity (read 2026-10-26), and no lab run compared it with a plain tree walk. Needs a measurement on the tree in question, not an official page. (topic: agents/codebase-mapping)

## windows/powershell-static-analysis

- **Explicit statement that `Parser.ParseFile` does not run the script.** The Parser, ParseFile and ParseInput API pages describe parsing only; no sentence says in so many words that nothing executes (Learn, 2026-09-28). (topic: windows/powershell-static-analysis)
  - Tried 2026-09-29: Learn search and the Parser class, ParseFile and ParseInput pages (S-k7hdd27i), about_Parsing, and the PSScriptAnalyzer pages; the Parser class page says it "parses PowerShell script and returns a ScriptBlockAst, tokens, and error messages" and no page says nothing executes. Recorded as a derived reading; a statement from the PowerShell team (an SDK conceptual page or an issue) would still settle it. (topic: windows/powershell-static-analysis)
  - Resolved 2026-09-29 (from code, not a documented promise): at PowerShell v7.6.6 `ParseFile`/`ParseInput` tokenize, build the AST and run symbol resolution and semantic checks, and run none of the script's code; the side effects are `using module` resolution through `Get-Module -ListAvailable` plus parsing the module's root script for classes, and DSC keyword loading for `configuration` (S-ia7is5xj, S-4bjgu6jw, S-ouafwanl, S-vbfv4per). The about_Foreach example and the PowerShell Team's PSScriptAnalyzer and AST blog posts were read and state no more than the API pages. (topic: windows/powershell-static-analysis)
- **Full `DiagnosticRecord` field list.** Invoke-ScriptAnalyzer and Using PSScriptAnalyzer show `RuleName`, `Severity`, `ScriptName`, `Line`, `Message` and `SuggestedCorrections` in examples, not a field reference (2026-09-28). (topic: windows/powershell-static-analysis)
  - Resolved 2026-09-29: `DiagnosticRecord.cs` at PSScriptAnalyzer 1.25.0 lists `Message`, `Extent`, `RuleName`, `Severity`, `ScriptPath`, `ScriptName`, `RuleSuppressionID`, `SuggestedCorrections`, `IsSuppressed` (S-3olari7o); the custom-rule guide names the four required ones (S-62qzauot). (topic: windows/powershell-static-analysis)

## agents/coding-agent-codebase-context

- **Gemini CLI: just-in-time ancestor loading versus an eager subdirectory scan.** At v0.61.0 `docs/cli/gemini-md.md` describes GEMINI.md files loaded when a tool touches a directory (ancestors up to a trusted root), while `docs/reference/configuration.md` describes a scan below the working directory limited to 200 directories; which one current builds do, or both, was not settled from the docs (2026-09-28). (topic: agents/coding-agent-codebase-context)
  - Tried 2026-09-29 (partly resolved): v0.61.0 `memoryDiscovery.ts` has an upward walk to the `.git` boundary and a just-in-time loader for a touched path, and no downward scan; `discoveryMaxDirs` (default 200) is in `config.ts` but its consumer was not found in the two files read (S-bci74yyr, S-fema44tc). The configuration page's subdirectory sentence stays unconfirmed; finding the consumer needs a code search of the repository, which needs a login here. (topic: agents/coding-agent-codebase-context)
  - Resolved 2026-09-29 (from code, not a documented promise): a shallow clone of v0.61.0 (commit `bb523741c7429a44d03e964bc124c7c92df59d5f`) shows `discoveryMaxDirs` is passed from the CLI settings to the core config and read only by `getDiscoveryMaxDirs()`, which has no caller outside tests; the async breadth-first search that honours `maxDirs` has no caller outside tests, and the one sync caller, the path corrector, uses a fixed 50. The 200-directory limit therefore has no consumer at that version (S-mxqyhxx7, S-fema44tc, S-clksl3oh, S-e7vrnq3a). (topic: agents/coding-agent-codebase-context)
- **Cursor embeddings-based codebase indexing.** Older Cursor docs URLs for codebase indexing and semantic search now redirect to the Search page, which describes a local Instant Grep index with no stored embeddings; no current official page describes an embeddings index (checked cursor.com docs, security and data-use pages, 2026-09-28). (topic: agents/coding-agent-codebase-context)
  - Tried 2026-09-29: cursor.com/docs/context/codebase-indexing redirects to the Search page, which says the Instant Grep index is local and that Cursor "does not store embeddings of your codebase for search" (S-4owuypm5); the `llms.txt` page list of the docs has no indexing or semantic-search page. No official embeddings-index page exists now, so the entry stays as a documented absence. (topic: agents/coding-agent-codebase-context)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: re-fetched `cursor.com/docs/context/codebase-indexing`: it still serves the Search page, which says Instant Grep builds its index on the machine and that Cursor "does not store embeddings of your codebase for search" (S-4owuypm5); no embeddings-index page exists, so the entry stays a documented absence (topic: agents/coding-agent-codebase-context)
- **VS Code local workspace index size limits.** The workspace-context page (DateApproved 9/16/2026) states no file-count limits for a local index (2026-09-28). (topic: agents/coding-agent-codebase-context)
  - Tried 2026-09-29: re-read the current workspace-context page (main branch) for limit, size and file-count statements; it says only that binary files and files excluded by `files.exclude` or `.gitignore` are not indexed and that the approach serves workspaces from five to 500,000 files, with no limit (S-v2sqknkh, same page). Left as a documented absence. (topic: agents/coding-agent-codebase-context)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: re-read `code.visualstudio.com/docs/copilot/reference/workspace-context`: it still says binary files and files excluded by `files.exclude` or `.gitignore` are not indexed and states no limit for a local index; the sentence on five to 500,000 files was not found in this fetch (reworded or moved), and the remote-index passage says only that a repository with hundreds of thousands of files may take some time. Left as a documented absence (topic: agents/coding-agent-codebase-context)

MergeRequests::MergeService (app/services/merge_requests/merge_service.rb) and PostMergeService contain no pipeline creation; the target branch is moved by Repository#merge / ff_merge (Gitaly). The only pipeline creation for a branch update is Git::BaseHooksService#create_pipeline, which calls Ci::CreatePipelineService#execute_async(:push, ...) (app/services/git/base_hooks_service.rb), so a default-branch pipeline after a merge is a push-source pipeline. Not read: the Gitaly post-receive call chain that reaches Git::BranchHooksService after UserMergeBranch, and merge trains (merge_request_event pipelines). No docs page states it. (topic: gitlab/detecting-mr-merges-in-ci)

- **What git documents about a file that a side branch added and removed again.** Looked 2026-10-13 in rev-list-options and git-log at v2.55.0 (S-dwkxab7d, S-45u5kgbq) and in `revision.c` (S-zwbgb73y): the History Simplification section gives one worked example (the same change made on both sides) and says nothing of an add followed by a delete; the answer in the article rests on that rule, the code and one run on git 2.50.1 on macOS. Needs the same commands on another git version and on Windows, or git's own history-simplification tests at a pinned tag. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/git-history-queries)
  - Tried 2026-10-21, still open (read in git's tests, not written to an article): read git's own tests at v2.55.0, t/t6012-rev-list-simplify.sh and t/t6111-rev-list-treesame.sh (raw.githubusercontent.com/git/git/v2.55.0/t/). Neither contains a side branch that adds and then deletes a file; t6111 comments the nearest case ("I changes it, but J reverts it, so K is TREESAME to both parents", merge K in the graph header), which is the same net-zero-on-the-side-branch shape for a path that exists before and after. t6012 only has the "modified the file identically" case. So no pinned upstream test or doc covers add-then-delete. Still open: the same commands on Windows and on 2.55.0, or a new test upstream. (topic: gitlab/git-history-queries)

## gitlab/git-commit-paths-and-symlinks
- **What git documents about a path under a symbolic link for `git add`, `git commit` and `git log`, and what it does with `core.symlinks=false`.** Looked 2026-10-13 in git-add, git-commit, git-update-index and the core configuration page at v2.55.0 (S-ziyhuwyc, S-zflhytlw) and in `pathspec.c`, `builtin/add.c` and `builtin/commit.c` (S-piib4adi, S-eud6nij7, S-kg2qicnp): no page states that a path beyond a directory link is refused or that `git commit` and `git log` treat it differently from `git add`; the article's facts rest on the source and one run on git 2.50.1 on macOS. Needs the same commands on Git for Windows with `core.symlinks` true and false. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/git-commit-paths-and-symlinks)
  - Partly resolved 2026-10-21: code at v2.55.0 shows where the refusal comes from. pathspec.c (parse_pathspec) dies with "pathspec '%s' is beyond a symbolic link" when the flag PATHSPEC_SYMLINK_LEADING_PATH is set and has_symlink_leading_path() is true; builtin/add.c (interactive_add and cmd_add, three parse_pathspec calls) is the only one of builtin/commit.c, rm.c, mv.c, checkout.c, log.c, revision.c, update-index.c, dir.c that passes that flag (builtin/commit.c does not), so only git add refuses. Documentation/config/core.adoc says core.symlinks=false checks symlinks out as small plain files with the link text and add and update-index will not change the recorded type. Not run on Git for Windows; behaviour of commit and log with core.symlinks=false still needs that run. Written to gitlab/git-commit-paths-and-symlinks.md:36 and gitlab/git-commit-paths-and-symlinks.md:42. (topic: gitlab/git-commit-paths-and-symlinks)

## gitlab/git-test-repositories
- **How git prints a non-ASCII remote url.** Looked 2026-10-05 in git-clone's GIT URLS section (S-vupwsv3m): it gives the url forms but not the byte encoding `git remote -v` or `git config --get remote.<name>.url` prints for a url with non-ASCII characters. Needs the git-config or git-remote documentation on output encoding, or git's source at a pinned tag. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/git-test-repositories)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21 from code plus a local run. At v2.55.0 builtin/remote.c (get_one_entry) formats the list as strbuf_addf("%s (fetch)", remote->url.v[0]) with no re-encoding, and config.c (write_pair) writes a value byte for byte, escaping only newline as \n, tab as \t, and backslash and double quote with a backslash. So a non-ASCII url is stored and printed exactly as the bytes passed in, and a percent-encoded url stays percent-encoded. Run on git 2.50.1 on macOS (LC_ALL=C): an UTF-8 url came back from git remote -v, git config --get and git ls-remote --get-url as the same UTF-8 bytes; a Latin-1 byte was kept as that byte; no escaping or percent-encoding was added. Windows console output encoding not tested. Code half written to gitlab/git-test-repositories.md:333. (topic: gitlab/git-test-repositories)
- **Why git refuses to switch or rebase a branch that another worktree has checked out.** Looked 2026-10-13 in the git-worktree, git-checkout, git-switch and git-rebase pages at v2.55.0 (S-j4qkzgvv, S-3au2dknw, S-hwicl2h6) and in `branch.c`, `builtin/checkout.c` and `builtin/rebase.c` (S-3qf7ndi6, S-i3btr2xr, S-sx4rosvv): the pages state the refusal and its override, and the only rationale is a source comment ("will not make things any worse"). No page says what breaks. Needs the commit message or list thread that added the check. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/git-test-repositories)
  - Tried 2026-10-21, still open (read in git's commit history, not written to an article): the commit that added the refusal, 5883034c61 "checkout: reject if the branch is already checked out elsewhere" (Duy, 2014-11-30, read in a blobless clone of github.com/git/git), says only "One branch obviously can't be checked out at two places (but detached heads are ok)" and lists the ways out: --detach, -b new-branch, switch the other checkout first, or cd there. b5cabb4a96 (Sunshine, 2020-02-23) says git-rebase "switches to <branch> unconditionally" unlike switch, checkout and worktree, and curbs that; 1d0fa898 (--ignore-other-worktrees) has no body. No message states what breaks (index, HEAD or working tree of the other worktree). The 2016 list thread was not readable (lore.kernel.org returned 403). (topic: gitlab/git-test-repositories)
- **What git documents about `index.lock` when two processes share a checkout.** Looked 2026-10-05 in git-update-index and git-worktree (S-wyfuoqs5): neither page describes the lock file or the error a second writer gets. Needs git's api documentation of lockfiles or its source at a pinned tag. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/git-test-repositories)
  - Partly resolved 2026-10-21: at v2.55.0, lockfile.h documents the lock file API: a writer creates <file>.lock with O_CREAT|O_EXCL so that it notices and fails if someone else holds it, then renames it over the file; lock files "only block other writers", readers do not block; the lock is removed on exit, die() or a signal. lockfile.c (unable_to_lock_message, unable_to_lock_die) gives the second writer "Unable to create '<path>.lock': File exists" plus a hint that another git process may be running or the lock may be stale, then die(), status 128. core.lockfilePid in Documentation/config/core.adoc (new, default false) adds an index~pid.lock file that lets the message say whether the holder still runs. Run on git 2.50.1 on macOS: an existing .git/index.lock made git add and git commit exit 128 with that text; git status exited 0. Code half written to gitlab/git-test-repositories.md:334. (topic: gitlab/git-test-repositories)
- **git worktree add path branch checks out an existing local branch after its worktree was removed** The kb was asked this in a logged lookup of 2026-10-13, and no article answered it (query log finding F-bc9e9d4c3ffd). Looked in the kb 2026-10-13: `rag.py pack` gives `weak`, with this topic in the lead. Needs an official source that states it (`/kb-research`, or the query log's opt-in research). (topic: gitlab/git-test-repositories)
  - Resolved 2026-10-20: gitlab/git-test-repositories.md:231, gitlab/git-test-repositories.md:234 and gitlab/git-test-repositories.md:238 answers it: `git worktree add <path> <branch>` checks an existing branch out when no worktree holds it (S-j4qkzgvv), `git worktree remove` touches no ref (S-ali5r4n7), so the branch is checked out again at its tip with every commit made in the removed worktree; the entry of a hand-deleted directory still holds the branch until `git worktree prune` (gitlab/git-test-repositories.md:248). (topic: gitlab/git-test-repositories)

## gitlab/git-trailers-and-hooks
- **Which shell Git for Windows runs a hook script with, and the process tree from `git commit` to the hook** (whether `sh.exe` from the Git for Windows installation runs it, and what sits between `git.exe` and the script). Looked 2026-10-07 in the kb (`gitlab/git-trailers-and-hooks`, `claude/hooks`: the latter is Claude Code's own hook shell, not Git's) and in a Microsoft Learn search for Git for Windows hooks, `sh.exe` and MSYS2: nothing states it. The git `githooks` page at the pinned tag (`Documentation/githooks.adoc`, tag `v2.55.0` as the other git sources) and the Git for Windows release notes were not read: a sprint worker's shell could not fetch them (`curl` needs approval, no web-fetch tool). Needs `githooks.adoc` and the Git for Windows `compat/mingw.c` shebang handling at a pinned tag (a `CODE` fact), then a process listing taken from a hook on the Windows host (parent chain read as in `windows/process-parent-and-command-line`). [UNK] (topic: gitlab/git-trailers-and-hooks)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: Git for Windows' `compat/mingw.c` at commit 5cf10d74486d1e59b9ae6cedaedb4e26314e1557 (main on that day; read raw, not yet a pinned `CODE` source) has `parse_interpreter`, which opens the program, reads a `#!` first line, takes the name after the last `/` or `\` (options after a space cut) and the spawn code (calls at its lines 2449 and 2476) looks that name up, with its own handling of `sh` and `sh.exe`; so a `#!/bin/sh` hook is run through an `sh` found on the path. A web search found only secondary accounts (a GitPython pull request, an old msysgit thread: hooks run as `sh hook`) and no `githooks` sentence on Windows. Still needs a pinned tag of that file as a source row and a process listing from a hook on the Windows host (topic: gitlab/git-trailers-and-hooks)
- **The exit status of `git ls-remote` when the connection fails or times out, and whether a git server finishes a ref update after the pushing client's connection has dropped.** Looked 2026-10-17 in `git-ls-remote.adoc`, `git-push.adoc` and `gitprotocol-pack.adoc` at `v2.55.0`: the ls-remote page says only that status 0 means it talked with the remote and 2 (with `--exit-code`) that nothing matched; the pack protocol page says the server updates the refs after it has validated the pack and reports afterwards, and says nothing of a client that disconnects in between. Needs `builtin/ls-remote.c` and `transport.c` (the die paths and their status) and `builtin/receive-pack.c` (the handling of a closed connection after the commands are read) at `v2.55.0`, as `CODE` facts, or a run against a local bare repository with a hook that delays the update. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/git-trailers-and-hooks)
  - Partly resolved 2026-10-21. Code at v2.55.0: builtin/ls-remote.c (cmd_ls_remote) ends in return status (status 1 only if transport_disconnect fails, 2 with --exit-code and no match), but a failed connection dies inside transport_get_remote_refs, and die() exits 128 (usage.c). builtin/receive-pack.c runs sigchain_push(SIGPIPE, SIG_IGN) around its reporting, so a vanished client does not stop the update. Run on git 2.50.1 on macOS: ls-remote exited 128 for a missing local path, a refused http or git:// port, a failing ssh command and an http stall under http.lowSpeedLimit; with --exit-code also 128. A git:// server that accepts and never answers hung with no timeout. A push whose client was killed 1.5 s in, while a pre-receive hook slept 4 s, still ran the hook to the end and the bare repository's ref was updated. Code half written to gitlab/git-trailers-and-hooks.md:81 and gitlab/git-trailers-and-hooks.md:82. (topic: gitlab/git-trailers-and-hooks)
- **Which refusal reason a server gives a push whose old id is stale because a second push moved the ref first, and whether two pushes that hold the ref lock at the same moment fail or wait.** Looked 2026-10-18 in `git-receive-pack.adoc`, `git-push.adoc` and `git-update-ref.adoc` at `v2.55.0` (none states what a concurrent update to one ref does) and in `builtin/receive-pack.c` at `v2.55.0` (`update()` passes the client's old id to `ref_transaction_update` and reports `failed to update ref` or `ref_transaction_error_msg`'s text; the kind a stale old id or a held lock takes is decided in the refs code, not read). Needs `refs.c`, `refs/files-backend.c` (the lock and the old-id check) and `ref_transaction_error_msg` at `v2.55.0` as `CODE` facts, or a run of two simultaneous pushes to a local bare repository, the first held by a `pre-receive` hook that sleeps. A pusher that fetches, rebases and pushes again after any refusal needs neither answer to recover. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/git-trailers-and-hooks)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21 from code plus local runs. At v2.55.0, refs/files-backend.c check_old_oid() sets "cannot lock ref '%s': is at %s but expected %s" and returns REF_TRANSACTION_ERROR_INCORRECT_OLD_VALUE for a stale old id; refs.c ref_transaction_error_msg() maps that to "incorrect old value provided", and builtin/receive-pack.c update() reports that text (only the generic error gives "failed to update ref"). A held ref lock: lock_raw_ref() retries hold_lock_file_for_update_timeout for get_files_ref_lock_timeout_ms(), default 100 ms (core.filesRefLockTimeout; 0 no retry, -1 indefinite), then fails with the generic error. Run on git 2.50.1 on macOS (server and client): a stale push got "failed to update ref" with the "is at ... but expected" text; with the lock held it failed after 0.3 s ("Unable to create ...main.lock: File exists"); with core.filesRefLockTimeout=-1 on the server it waited 2.6 s, then failed on the stale id. 2.55.0 itself not run. Code half written to gitlab/git-trailers-and-hooks.md:83. (topic: gitlab/git-trailers-and-hooks)
- **What a trailer command that reads the index returns for `git commit <path>` and `git commit -o` in a `commit-msg` hook.** Looked 2026-10-19 by running git 2.50.1 (macOS) in a scratch repository: the hook saw `GIT_INDEX_FILE=.git/index` for a plain commit and for `--amend`, `.git/index.lock` for `-a` and `next-index-<pid>.lock` for `commit <path>`; a trailer command (which runs without `GIT_INDEX_FILE`) read the staged file for the plain commit and `--amend`, and missed the files of `-a`. The command's output for `commit <path>` and `commit -o` was not kept, git 2.55.0 itself was not run, and `githooks.adoc` (S-7dwkyip6) does not say which index file a hook sees. Needs a run of those two cases with a `commit-msg` hook that logs `git diff --cached --name-only` from a trailer command, or the `builtin/commit.c` index handling at `v2.55.0` as `CODE` facts. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/git-trailers-and-hooks)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21 from code plus a local run. builtin/commit.c prepare_index() at v2.55.0 returns the real index file for an as-is commit, the locked real index (index.lock) for -a or -i with paths, and a temporary index for a partial commit (paths without -i, or -o); commit passes that file to hooks as GIT_INDEX_FILE (run_commit_hook). Run on git 2.50.1 on macOS with a commit-msg hook and a trailer command running git diff --cached --name-only (the trailer command has no GIT_INDEX_FILE, so it reads .git/index): staged a.txt, unstaged b.txt. Plain commit and --amend: hook saw .git/index, trailer matched the staged files. commit b.txt and commit -o b.txt: hook saw next-index-<pid>.lock, trailer listed a.txt but the commit held only b.txt. -i b.txt and -a: hook saw index.lock, trailer listed only a.txt while the commit held a.txt and b.txt. Code half written to gitlab/git-trailers-and-hooks.md:84. (topic: gitlab/git-trailers-and-hooks)
- **Whether an untracked file that an incoming commit adds, or a file the incoming commits delete, stops `git merge --ff-only` when the checkout has a local change to it, and which paths `git merge` counts as overlapping.** Looked 2026-10-20 in `git-merge.adoc` at `v2.55.0` (it says only that local uncommitted changes overlapping files the merge "may need to update" stop it) and by one git 2.50.1 run in scratch repositories (a working-tree edit to a file the incoming commits change stopped it with exit 1; edits to other files, staged or not, did not). Untracked-file collisions, deletions and renames were not run, and the `unpack-trees.c` checks behind the refusal were not read. Needs those cases run, or `unpack-trees.c` at `v2.55.0` as `CODE` facts. A monitor that says why a checkout cannot fast-forward is right for the modified-file case and may miss the others. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/git-trailers-and-hooks)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21 from code plus local runs. At v2.55.0 builtin/merge.c calls checkout_fast_forward() (merge.c), which runs unpack_trees with twoway_merge, update=1. unpack-trees.c: merged_entry/deleted_entry call verify_absent() (untracked file in the way, error text "The following untracked working tree files would be overwritten by merge", skipped for an excluded path) and verify_uptodate() (local change, "Your local changes to the following files would be overwritten by merge"). Run on git 2.50.1 on macOS, merge --ff-only exit 1 for: an untracked file at an incoming added path (even identical content); a worktree or staged edit to a file the incoming commits delete, rename, or mode-change; a staged different new file at an added path; an untracked file under a directory that becomes a file. Exit 0 for: an ignored file at the added path (overwritten), a staged identical new file, a file already removed locally, an unrelated edit. Code half written to gitlab/git-trailers-and-hooks.md:85. (topic: gitlab/git-trailers-and-hooks)

## windows/process-parent-and-command-line
- **What the Python standard library documents for a process's parent on Windows** (`os.getppid()` on Windows and the id-reuse note, and `ctypes` with `ctypes.wintypes` and `WinDLL` for `kernel32` and `ntdll` calls such as `CreateToolhelp32Snapshot`). Looked 2026-10-07: Microsoft Learn has no Python standard-library pages, and the CPython docs (`Doc/library/os.rst`, `Doc/library/ctypes.rst` at tag `v3.14.7`, the tag the other Python sources use) were not read in this session: a sprint worker's shell could not fetch them (`curl` needs approval, no web-fetch tool). Needs those two pages read at the pinned tag. [UNK] (topic: windows/process-parent-and-command-line)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21: read CPython `Doc/library/os.rst` and `Doc/library/ctypes.rst` at tag v3.14.7. `os.getppid()` "Return the parent's process id"; when the parent has exited, on Windows "it is still the same id, which may be already reused by another process"; availability Unix and Windows, not WASI; Windows support added in 3.2. `ctypes`: `WinDLL`/`windll` (stdcall, `int` return by default, Windows only) and `ctypes.wintypes` (`HWND`, `DWORD`, `MSG`, `RECT` and others); the page lists no `PROCESSENTRY32` or `PROCESS_BASIC_INFORMATION`, so a caller declares them. Recorded in windows/process-parent-and-command-line.md (S-ew7mucsg, S-5ieww4en). (topic: windows/process-parent-and-command-line)
- **Whether a non-elevated user can read its own parent's command line through `OpenProcess` plus `ReadProcessMemory`, `NtQueryInformationProcess` or `Win32_Process.CommandLine`.** Looked 2026-10-07 in the `OpenProcess`, "Process Security and Access Rights", `NtQueryInformationProcess`, `PEB`, `RTL_USER_PROCESS_PARAMETERS`, `CreateToolhelp32Snapshot`, `PROCESSENTRY32` and `Win32_Process` pages (Microsoft Learn): they give the access rights and the `SeDebugPrivilege` rule, and no same-user worked case. Needs a run on the Windows host, elevated and not (the `kg_hooks.parent_args` proof run). [UNK] (topic: windows/process-parent-and-command-line)
  - Tried 2026-10-20: the Learn `Win32_Process` page (S-hgt26zgs) has no per-property access note, and the `Get-CimInstance` example read for the `ps` topic (S-v6442u3l, python/ps-process-cpu.md) runs PowerShell as administrator and says the query it shows returns no results without it, which is a lead that a non-elevated query may return nothing, not a statement about `CommandLine` of one process. Still needs a run on the Windows host, elevated and not, naming the account of the target process. (topic: windows/process-parent-and-command-line)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: a web search found tutorials that open the target with `PROCESS_QUERY_INFORMATION` and `PROCESS_VM_READ` to read the PEB and its process parameters, none that states the same-user, non-elevated case; the Learn `Win32_Process` page has no per-property access note (read 2026-10-20). Still needs a run on the Windows host, elevated and not, naming the account of the target process (topic: windows/process-parent-and-command-line)

## agents/test-suite-size-and-agent-overengineering
- **Whether monitoring or logs can stand in for a regression test.** Looked 2026-10-06 in Microsoft Learn's shift-left and shift-right pages (S-wxn7kar4, S-claarsij) and in the Google SRE book's Testing for Reliability chapter (S-flv3lmzn): they describe testing in production and monitoring as an addition to tests before release, and none says when a defect that a log shows needs no test. Also not found: a slow-test threshold in seconds in GitLab's testing guide, and a time limit for Microsoft's L2 and L3 tests. (topic: agents/test-suite-size-and-agent-overengineering)
  - Partly resolved 2026-10-10: the deadlines of a quarantine are stated in GitLab's quarantine handbook (S-jg2qnt5d), which the testing guide's `unhealthy_tests` page points to, and the topic carries them (a fast quarantine 3 days at most, a long-term one 3 months at most, the deletion merge request a week before the end); only the slow-test threshold and the L2 and L3 time limit stay not found, and the question of monitoring or logs in place of a regression test stays open (topic: agents/test-suite-size-and-agent-overengineering)
- **what keeps a test suite small: which tests to delete, test suite size limits and test budget** The kb was asked this in a logged lookup of 2026-10-05, and no article answered it (query log finding F-ae2f85ba904e). Looked in the kb 2026-10-05: `rag.py pack` gives `weak`, with this topic in the lead. Needs an official source that states it (`/kb-research`, or the query log's opt-in research). (topic: agents/test-suite-size-and-agent-overengineering)
  - Resolved 2026-10-10: which tests to delete and when (GitLab's quarantine handbook: fix, delete, convert or quarantine, deletion after 3 months, 3 quarantines as a removal candidate, S-jg2qnt5d, S-f65kifg4; Software Engineering at Google on unexplained tests, S-ocdzembr) and the size limits and time budgets (S-3dlofilm, S-wxn7kar4) now answer it in the topic; the pack gives `good` (topic: agents/test-suite-size-and-agent-overengineering)
- **A budget for the number of tests in a suite, and a cadence for a full run outside per merge.** Looked 2026-10-10 in GitLab's testing guide, quarantine handbook and pipelines page (S-jg2qnt5d, S-nxhqpwaf, S-f65kifg4), Software Engineering at Google chapters 11, 12 and 23 (S-xpnhjuy4, S-ocdzembr, S-bmz46flb), the SRE book (S-flv3lmzn) and Bazel's test encyclopedia (S-3dlofilm): they state per-test time limits and ratios of test kinds, and no cap on the count of tests; they give each organisation's own schedule for runs after the merge (a two-hour post-submit CI, GitLab's `maintenance`, `nightly` and `weekly` pipelines) and no rule for how often a smaller repository should run its full suite, or how a failure found by a scheduled run is traced to one merge (query log finding F-6ac5244b1e3d asked the second). Also not read: Google's research papers on test selection (found by search only, not read) and GitLab's handbook page on master-broken incidents. Needs those pages read, or an operator's own number. [UNK] (topic: agents/test-suite-size-and-agent-overengineering)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: GitLab's handbook page on master-broken incidents answers 403 behind a bot check to a plain GET; a web search found its incident issues (fields for pipeline, branch, commit, merge request, source and trigger, the merge request empty for a scheduled pipeline) and its triage steps (retry a possibly flaky job, search the tracker, revert or fix the culprit merge request), and no rule that traces a scheduled run's failure to one merge beyond reading the commits between the last green and the first red run, which is an inference. Google's "Taming Google-Scale Continuous Testing" (ICSE-SEIP 2017) shows its abstract on research.google and the course-hosted PDF now answers 404; its text with the cadence numbers still needs an institutional copy (topic: agents/test-suite-size-and-agent-overengineering)
- **Who may change a suite's declared limit or ceiling, and what an agent does when a whole-suite run is refused on a loaded host.** Looked 2026-10-12 in the kb (`rag.py pack` gives `weak`, the test-suite topic and `claude/settings-and-scopes` in the lead) and in Bazel's test encyclopedia (S-3dlofilm): it states a `--test_timeout` override for a person running tests by hand under conditions known to be slow, and no rule for who changes a declared timeout; GitLab's and Google's pages (S-nxhqpwaf, S-bmz46flb, S-jg2qnt5d) state who owns a quarantine and its deadlines, not who may raise a limit. Claude Code's permission pages (S-zcuxapgb) state that a deny rule at a higher scope cannot be overridden and that a protected-path write is never auto-approved, which is the mechanism, not a rule for test limits. Needs a source that states one; the project sets its own rule otherwise. [UNK] (topic: agents/test-suite-size-and-agent-overengineering)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: a web search for who may raise a declared test timeout or CI limit found only quarantine governance (an owner, an expiry and an issue link per quarantined test; Harness says test policies are usually set by platform engineers or QA leads, not individual developers; Trunk recommends a team-wide fix SLA for quarantined tests) and no rule for who changes a limit or what an agent does when a whole-suite run is refused on a loaded host. Still needs a source that states one; the project sets its own rule otherwise (topic: agents/test-suite-size-and-agent-overengineering)

## gitlab/git-refs
- **The documented exit statuses of `git update-ref` and `git for-each-ref`** (which status a failed `<old-oid>` check, an existing ref under the create form, and a `-d` of a missing ref give, and whether `for-each-ref` with no matching ref exits 0). Looked 2026-10-08 in git-update-ref, git-for-each-ref and for-each-ref-options at tag v2.55.0 (S-sgbkwka4, S-cacomkjs, S-xahbgevi): no exit status is stated; the article records one run with git 2.50.1 on macOS (exit 1 for a wrong old value under `-d`, 128 for the create form on an existing ref, 0 for `-d` of a missing ref). Needs `builtin/update-ref.c` and `refs.c` read at tag v2.55.0 (`CODE` facts), or a run with git 2.55.0 and on Windows. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/git-refs)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21 from code at v2.55.0. builtin/update-ref.c (cmd_update_ref): without -d it returns refs_update_ref(..., UPDATE_REFS_DIE_ON_ERR), and refs.c refs_update_ref() then die()s on any transaction failure (wrong old id, existing ref under the create form with a zero old id), so status 128. With -d it returns refs_delete_ref(), which prints error() and returns 1 on any failure (wrong old id gives 1) and returns 0 on success; deleting a missing ref without an old id commits an empty-effect transaction, so 0. builtin/for-each-ref.c (for_each_ref_core) ends in return 0 with no check of how many refs matched, so no match exits 0 (usage errors via die or usage_with_options). This matches the article's git 2.50.1 run. Written to gitlab/git-refs.md:44. (topic: gitlab/git-refs)

## python/stdlib-sqlite3-csv
- **How the stdlib `sqlite3` module runs `VACUUM`, and what `auto_vacuum` reclaims after a `DELETE` through it.** Looked 2026-10-09 in the module's page at CPython v3.14.7 (S-rle6nqpc), the SQLite VACUUM page (S-vc2lihhb) and the PRAGMA page (S-nnljaqtm): the Python page never mentions `VACUUM` or `auto_vacuum`, and the SQLite pages say a transaction open on the connection makes `VACUUM` fail without naming the `sqlite3` module's implicit `BEGIN`. The article joins them as a `DER` fact and records one probe (Python 3.13.2, SQLite 3.45.3, macOS). Needs a run on Windows and on Python 3.14 to widen the probe. [UNK] (topic: python/stdlib-sqlite3-csv)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: the SQLite PRAGMA page (sqlite.org/pragma.html, read again) says `auto_vacuum=incremental` frees pages only when `incremental_vacuum` is invoked, and the `sqlite3` module page still never mentions `VACUUM` or `auto_vacuum`. Still needs a run on Windows and on Python 3.14 to widen the probe (topic: python/stdlib-sqlite3-csv)
- **Whether `PRAGMA incremental_vacuum` frees one page or the whole freelist per `execute` call.** Looked 2026-10-09 in the PRAGMA page (S-nnljaqtm) and the `sqlite3` page (S-rle6nqpc): neither says that the pragma frees a page per row stepped. One probe (Python 3.13.2, SQLite 3.45.3) freed one page for a bare `execute` and the whole freelist for `fetchall()` or `executescript`. Needs the SQLite C interface documentation for `sqlite3_step` on this pragma, or the probe on other versions. [UNK] (topic: python/stdlib-sqlite3-csv)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: the SQLite PRAGMA page (read again) says the pragma "causes up to N pages to be removed from the freelist", has no effect outside `auto_vacuum=incremental` mode and says nothing of the statement being stepped; a bare `execute` freeing one page therefore rests on the probe. Still needs the SQLite C interface documentation for `sqlite3_step` on this pragma, or the probe on other versions (topic: python/stdlib-sqlite3-csv)
- **Whether a `DEFERRED` read-then-write transaction in SQLite waits out the busy timeout or fails at once when another connection has written since the read, and what a busy handler does for `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`.** Looked 2026-10-16 in the transaction page (S-b4zlktyl), the WAL page (S-232dvyc5) and the busy-timeout page (S-5vbo75zy): they say the upgrade "can" fail with `SQLITE_BUSY` and that `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` may fail with it, and say what the busy handler does for a locked table, but none ties the handler to the lock `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` takes or to the failed upgrade. The article's `DER` fact on racing claims rests on the first reading. Needs the SQLite locking pages or a two-process probe on the Python 3.13/SQLite 3.45 pair the article's other probe used. [UNK] (topic: python/stdlib-sqlite3-csv)
  - Narrowed 2026-10-18: the busy-handler page (S-mvz475h7) says the busy timeout is a handler that sleeps and then returns `SQLITE_BUSY`, that a connection has none by default, and that SQLite skips the handler where waiting could deadlock; a two-connection probe (Python 3.13.2, SQLite 3.45.3, rollback-journal mode) showed `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` waiting out the timeout (0.34 s for 0.3, 1.07 s for 1.0) and a read-then-write upgrade failing at once with `timeout=5`. Still open: the same probe in WAL mode and on another version, and the SQLite page that ties the handler to the `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` lock by name. (topic: python/stdlib-sqlite3-csv)
- **How a long-poll request is held and ended on `http.server.ThreadingHTTPServer` (idle timeout of a held connection, a client that disconnects while waiting, handler thread limit).** Looked 2026-10-16 in the `http.server` page (S-rs27i36t) and the `socketserver` page (S-uj2rumru): they document thread-per-request handling, `daemon_threads`, `shutdown()` and the `timeout` of `handle_request()`, but no long-poll pattern and no cap on threads for the threading mixin; a handler's socket timeout was not looked up. Needs a run of a held request against the server or a stdlib networking source. [UNK] (topic: python/stdlib-argparse-json)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: a web search found the `http.server` pages again (thread per request, no idle timeout or thread cap stated) and one author's long-poll pattern (a `threading.Event`, a `BrokenPipeError` on the next write after the client closes), a lead and not documentation; a handler's socket `timeout` attribute was not read. Still needs a run of a held request against the server or a stdlib networking source (topic: python/stdlib-argparse-json)
- **What a client that is still sending a request body sees when an `http.server` handler answers and closes without reading it (the early response or a connection reset).** Looked 2026-10-18 in the `http.server` page (S-rs27i36t), `Lib/http/server.py` (S-hqcmecwc) and `Lib/socketserver.py` (S-5x4j2vvx): the framework never drains `rfile` and `finish` just closes it, so the outcome is the operating system's TCP behaviour on a close with unread data, which no Python page states. Needs a TCP stack source (an RFC 9293 passage on close with unread data) or a client run per platform. [UNK] (topic: python/stdlib-argparse-json)
  - Partly resolved 2026-10-21: RFC 1122 says a TCP that closes while received data is still pending, or receives data after the close, "SHOULD send a RST to show that data was lost" [DOC S-tbohxt23], recorded in `python/stdlib-argparse-json.md` with the inference that a handler's early answer and close can reach the client as a reset; still open: which of the early response or the reset a client's stack hands to its application per platform, which RFC 1122 does not state and needs a client run [UNK] (topic: python/stdlib-argparse-json)
