## gitlab/runner-registration
- **Whether a group access token (a bot user) may call `POST /user/runners`.** Looked 2026-10-11 in the Users API page (S-udvn7k24), the Runners API page (S-ytnvr75r), the access token scopes (S-jk4rtq6g) and the token overview (S-5ohrrj7d), all at master (the Users API page re-read at @a1faf261 on 2026-10-22): the endpoint creates a runner "linked to the current user" and lists an administrator or the Owner role and a token with `create_runner` as prerequisites; `create_runner` is a scope of group access tokens, but no page says whether a group's bot user passes the "current user" and Owner checks. Needs a call from a group access token with `create_runner` and the Owner role (`access_level` 50) on a lab instance. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/runner-registration)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21 from code (no lab call): a group access token's bot user can call POST /user/runners if it has Owner on the group and the create_runner scope. lib/api/user_runners.rb @29a9622b allows scope :create_runner for POST, runs authenticate! and passes current_user to Ci::Runners::CreateRunnerService, whose GroupRunnerStrategy#authorized_user? is user.can?(:create_runners, group). config/authz/roles/owner.yml lists create_runners in the group raw_permissions, applied through GroupPolicy role permissions (define_role_permissions); GroupPolicy only prevents it when runner registration is disabled for the group (and not admin). Nothing excludes bot users. Maintainer is not enough; a real call would confirm. Written to gitlab/runner-registration.md:35. (topic: gitlab/runner-registration)

## gitlab/pipelines-rules
- **Which permission and which answers the pipeline schedule `play` endpoint has.** Looked 2026-10-11 in the Pipeline schedules API page (S-aibu3bke, master @9f1632e2) and the scheduled pipelines page (S448): the API page shows one example response (`201 Created`) and names no role; the user page says a manual run uses the permissions of the user who runs it, at most once per minute, and names no refusal. Not stated: the role needed, the answer for a second call within a minute, and what a user who may not push or merge to a protected target gets. Needs a call on a lab instance as a Developer, a Maintainer and a user outside the project. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/pipelines-rules)
  - Tried 2026-10-21, still open (read in GitLab code, not written to an article): lib/api/ci/pipeline_schedules.rb @29a9622b declares the play route with authorize! :play_pipeline_schedule and failure codes 401, 403, 404, success 201; Ci::PipelineSchedulePolicy enables play_pipeline_schedule for anyone who can :create_pipeline, and prevents it when the schedule's ref is protected for that user (ref_protected?), so a user without push/merge rights to a protected target is refused (403). The docs (doc/ci/pipelines/schedules.md) say once per minute and the run uses the caller's permissions. PlayService enqueues RunPipelineScheduleWorker (deduplicated until_executed); no rate-limit code or a distinct answer for a second call within a minute was found. Still open: that second-call response. (topic: gitlab/pipelines-rules)

## gitlab/package-registries
- **Whether `--index-url` with `--no-index` installs from the GitLab PyPI index.** Looked 2026-10-11 in the PyPI registry page (S-v25mipeh, master @9f1632e2): it advises the two flags together so that packages come from GitLab only, and the kb holds no page of pip's reference for `--no-index` (searched the kb for `no-index`, none found). Needs pip's reference page for `--no-index` and `--index-url`, and a run against a GitLab index. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/package-registries)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21: no, the two flags must not be combined. pip's reference (pip install, pip documentation v26.2.2, https://pip.pypa.io/en/stable/cli/pip_install/) says "--no-index: Ignore package index (only looking at --find-links URLs instead)" and "--index-url: Base URL of the Python Package Index". GitLab master's doc/user/packages/pypi_repository/_index.md now says "Do not use the --no-index flag. The flag forces pip to ignore every package index, including the GitLab registry set with --index-url." GitLab commit 4826a8b0 (2026-10-16) "Remove the pip --no-index advice from the PyPI registry docs" says those commands fail with "from versions: none". The article fact gitlab/package-registries.md:24 already records the warning against --no-index, so the entry's premise (the page advises the two flags together) was out of date. Not run against a GitLab index. (topic: gitlab/package-registries)
- **Pulling a container image by digest from the GitLab registry.** Looked 2026-10-11 in the container registry page, its API page and the registry authentication page (S-5lpubwd5, S-gqpmeuse, S-owkwtkdm): the tag API returns the `digest` and the interface shows manifest and configuration digests, but no GitLab page shows a pull by `@sha256:`. Docker's reference documents the form (S-cx5yd4ae); the article derives the pin from the two. Needs a pull by digest from a lab registry, with the metadata database both on and off. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/package-registries)
  - Tried 2026-10-21, still open: the GitLab container registry administration page (doc/administration/packages/container_registry.md, master, Tier Free, Premium, Ultimate, GitLab Self-Managed), section "Understanding the content-addressable layers", says that after a tag is overwritten the old image is still accessible "when pulling the image <my.registry.com>/<my.group>/<my.project>@sha256:<111111...>", and the garbage collection section says "users can keep pulling untagged images by digest". That is an official statement of a pull by digest from the GitLab registry, written for the legacy (non-metadata-database) registry. Still open: whether it holds with the metadata database on (that section is not marked); a lab pull would settle it. (topic: gitlab/package-registries)

## gitlab/multi-project-pipelines
- **Whether a job that starts a downstream pipeline through the trigger API with `CI_JOB_TOKEN` can wait for and take the downstream status, as `trigger:strategy: mirror` does for a trigger job.** Looked 2026-10-15 in the downstream pipelines page (S-pt32lcdt), the `trigger` keyword reference (S-s67nwj2e) and the pipeline trigger tokens API page (S-xz7iisq3): the API page says the calling job becomes linked to the upstream pipeline, and the strategy options are documented only for `trigger` jobs. Needs a GitLab page on API-triggered downstream status, or a lab run that polls the downstream pipeline from the calling job. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/multi-project-pipelines)
  - Tried 2026-10-21, still open (read in GitLab code and docs, not written to an article): no, the calling job does not take the downstream status on its own. The downstream pipelines page (doc/ci/pipelines/downstream_pipelines.md) documents strategy mirror/depend only for trigger jobs and says API pipelines triggered with a job token are linked as downstream of the calling job's pipeline. In code, Ci::PipelineTriggerService#create_pipeline_from_job @29a9622b links the new pipeline to the calling job; status inheritance (inherit_status_from_downstream, mirrored?, dependent?) lives only in Ci::Bridge, and Ci::Pipeline#bridge_waiting? asks source_bridge&.has_strategy?, where source_bridge is a Ci::Bridge association; a normal script job is a Ci::Build. So the script must poll the downstream pipeline itself. Polling not run. (topic: gitlab/multi-project-pipelines)

## security/linux-nftables
- **The `nft` syntax-check-only option and how each family loads its ruleset file at boot.** Looked 2026-10-11 in the nftables wiki pages Atomic rule replacement, Sets and Scripting (S-nvc4ejpk, S-oz7g4upk, S-h3233m5r): they cover `nft -f`, sets and includes, not a dry-run flag or the distributions' `nftables.service` and `/etc/nftables.conf`. Needs the `nft(8)` manual and each distribution's package documentation. [UNK] (topic: security/linux-nftables)
  - Partly resolved 2026-10-13: the `nft(8)` manual (S-t7vpftda) lists `-c, --check`, "Check commands validity without actually applying the changes" (topic: security/linux-nftables); how each distribution loads `/etc/nftables.conf` at boot is still not read.
- **Whether `flush ruleset` from another process skips or fails on a table created with the `owner` flag.** Looked 2026-10-13 in the `nft(8)` manual (S-t7vpftda: `flush ruleset` removes all tables; an `owner` table excludes other processes from manipulating it) and the wiki's Atomic rule replacement page (S-nvc4ejpk): neither says how the two combine. Needs a lab test on a host with a process-owned table or the nftables release notes. [UNK] (topic: security/linux-nftables)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: the kernel commit that added the table owner flag (torvalds/linux 6001a930ce03, netfilter: nftables: introduce table ownership) says owned tables are skipped by the global flush ruleset and that non-owners updating or removing them get EPERM, and nft(8) says an owned table is removed when its owner exits unless persist is set; the commit wording came from a summarised fetch, so a lab test would confirm it. (topic: security/linux-nftables)
- **Whether a set declared with `elements` in a `table` block of an `nft -f` file replaces or adds to the elements of a set that already exists, what `add set` does for an existing set, and the nft release that introduced `destroy`.** Looked 2026-10-14 in the `nft(8)` manual (S-t7vpftda: `add`/`create` for tables, `destroy set`, `flush set`, `add element`; no word on a re-declared set or the release of `destroy`) and the wiki's Atomic rule replacement page (S-nvc4ejpk: only that importing an existing set is allowed from Linux 3.17). Needs a lab run of a file loaded twice with a changed element list, or the nftables release notes. [UNK] (topic: security/linux-nftables)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: `destroy` arrived in nftables 1.0.7 (announcement 13 March 2023, 'New destroy command', kernel 6.3 or later; changes-nftables-1.0.7.txt) and nft(8) says `add table` does not error on an existing table; no page read says whether a re-declared set with elements replaces or adds to the existing elements, so that still needs a lab run of a file loaded twice. (topic: security/linux-nftables)

## security/linux-patching-and-pins

  - Tried 2026-10-21 (negative): apt sources.list(5) and apt-secure(8) (manpages.debian.org), the dnf conf_ref repo options and apk-repositories(5) document no option that pins a repository by a hash of its Release or index file; pinning is by signing key (apt Signed-By, dnf gpgkey and repo_gpgcheck) or by snapshot (apt Snapshot), and the apk-keys and apk-verify pages were not read. (topic: security/linux-patching-and-pins)

No AlmaLinux page that documents holding a host on one minor was found, and whether the mirrorlist answers for a minor is not confirmed (see the entries under that topic). (topic: security/linux-patching-and-pins)

## prior-art/drift-detection
- **A static pull agent that compares git commit ancestry before applying.** Looked 2026-10-11 in the ansible-pull page (S-wbcdphyr: `-C`, `-o`, `--verify-commit`, no ancestry rule) and the TUF specification (S-im2mzbgr: version rule for metadata, not git); no source read documents `git merge-base --is-ancestor` or `pull --ff-only` as a rollback guard. Needs the git manual pages for both commands. [UNK] (topic: prior-art/drift-detection)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21: the git manual pages say `git merge-base --is-ancestor` checks whether the first commit is an ancestor of the second and exits 0 if so and 1 if not (S-2ckkpa2t), and `git pull --ff-only` updates only when there is no divergent local history, the default when no reconcile method is given (S-7boud67e); both are facts in `prior-art/drift-detection.md`. No read source ties them to a rollback guard of a pull agent, which the article still says (topic: prior-art/drift-detection)

## logs/loki-tempo-grafana-retention
- **Default Grafana data directory of a Linux package install.** Looked 2026-10-11 in the v13.2.3 configure-grafana page (S-rmnhrvck: `[paths] data` is "usually specified via command line in the init.d script or the systemd service file", only the macOS path is named) and conf/defaults.ini (S-47axdg7z: `data = data`, relative to the working directory); no page read names the deb/rpm default. Needs the Grafana install-on-Debian/RHEL pages or the packaging files. [UNK] (topic: logs/loki-tempo-grafana-retention)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: the Grafana packaging files at tag v13.2.3 set the data directory of a deb or rpm install. raw.githubusercontent.com/grafana/grafana/v13.2.3/packaging/deb/default/grafana-server (and packaging/rpm/sysconfig/grafana-server) contain "DATA_DIR=/var/lib/grafana" and "PLUGINS_DIR=/var/lib/grafana/plugins"; packaging/deb/systemd/grafana-server.service passes "cfg:default.paths.data=${DATA_DIR}" to `grafana server`, so the packaged service overrides the relative `data = data` of conf/defaults.ini. The install-on-Debian docs page at grafana.com/docs/grafana/latest/setup-grafana/installation/debian/ does not name the path (searched). Source is packaging code, not a documentation promise. (topic: logs/loki-tempo-grafana-retention)

## logs/otel-collector-otlp-pipeline
- **Whether Prometheus accepts OTLP logs or traces.** Looked 2026-10-11 in the Prometheus OpenTelemetry guide (S-5tvo6y34) and the v3.15.0 command-line flags page (S-one7nth5): both document only the metrics path `/api/v1/otlp/v1/metrics` and the flag text says "OTLP write requests"; no page read states what happens to logs or traces. Needs the Prometheus OTLP receiver source or a statement on prometheus.io. [UNK] (topic: logs/otel-collector-otlp-pipeline)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21: no Prometheus page states that logs or traces are rejected in so many words, but two sources settle what is accepted. Prometheus OpenTelemetry guide (prometheus.io/docs/guides/opentelemetry/) says the receiver serves "OTLP metrics" on /api/v1/otlp/v1/metrics and, for a Python SDK example, tells the reader to "Turn off traces and logs" with OTEL_TRACES_EXPORTER=none and OTEL_LOGS_EXPORTER=none. The v3.15.0 source web/api/v1/api.go registers only r.Post("/otlp/v1/metrics", ...) (line 490), and the handler in storage/remote/write_otlp_handler.go implements only ConsumeMetrics. So no log or trace OTLP route exists. Backed by `logs/prometheus-retention-exposition.md:87`, `logs/otel-collector-otlp-pipeline.md:50` (topic: logs/otel-collector-otlp-pipeline)
- **Whether Loki's OTLP endpoint accepts OTLP over gRPC.** Looked 2026-10-11 in the Loki OpenTelemetry pages at v3.7.8 (S-j7chz4co, S-wpvglutf, S-n4qvimrb): they say Loki ingests OTLP logs over HTTP and require the `otlphttp` exporter; no gRPC statement either way. Needs the Loki distributor or API reference. [UNK] (topic: logs/otel-collector-otlp-pipeline)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: Loki v3.7.8 pkg/loki/modules.go (initDistributor) registers the OTLP endpoint only as an HTTP route, t.Server.HTTP.Path("/otlp/v1/logs").Methods("POST") pointing at distributor.OTLPPushHandler (pkg/distributor/http.go); the only gRPC push registration is logproto.RegisterPusherServer (Loki's own push service, lines 402 and 772), and no OTLP collector gRPC service is registered (grep for plogotlp and OTLP gRPC found none). The docs page grafana.com/docs/loki/latest/send-data/otel/ says "Loki natively supports ingesting OpenTelemetry logs over HTTP" and "you must use the otlphttp exporter". No sentence says gRPC is unsupported; that follows from the code. (topic: logs/otel-collector-otlp-pipeline)

- **Whether the trace and log OTLP/HTTP exporters resolve endpoint, headers and timeout like the metric exporter.** Looked 2026-10-18 at the shared helpers in `exporter/opentelemetry-exporter-otlp-proto-http/.../_common/__init__.py` (S-4kd2fl7f), which take the environment variable name as an argument, and at the metric exporter's calls (S-3ocg3rma); the trace exporter's and the log exporter's calls (S-qtwfnvws imports them) were not read line by line. Needs those two files at v1.45.1. [UNK] (topic: logs/otel-python-sdk)

In _common/__init__.py the per-signal variable wins as-is, else OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT plus "/v1/<signal>"; headers and timeout fall back to the generic variable; default timeout is 10 s. Backed by `logs/otel-python-sdk.md:34`, `logs/otel-python-sdk.md:36` (topic: logs/otel-python-sdk)
- **Which release moved the OTLP/HTTP client into `opentelemetry-exporter-otlp-common` and `opentelemetry-exporter-http-transport`, and whether the retry and header behaviour read at v1.45.1 holds for older exporter releases.** Looked 2026-10-18 at the PyPI requirement lists (S-omxbv3ul) and the source at v1.45.1 (S-mi6kw3ft); no changelog or older tag was read. Needs the repository changelog or the exporter at an earlier tag. [UNK] (topic: logs/otel-python-sdk)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: CHANGELOG.md at v1.45.1 (github.com/open-telemetry/opentelemetry-python/blob/v1.45.1/CHANGELOG.md) dates it. Version 1.45.0 (2026-09-25) lists "opentelemetry-exporter-otlp-proto-http: refactor to use shared opentelemetry-exporter-otlp-common and opentelemetry-exporter-http-transport packages and switch default HTTP backend to urllib3" (PR 5389); the common package was added in 1.44.0 (PR 5252) and the http-transport package in 1.43.0 (PR 5194). The exporter at v1.44.0 still used requests.Session and its own retry loop (_MAX_RETRYS = 6, 2**n backoff with 0.8-1.2 jitter). Whether header and retry behaviour is identical to v1.45.1 was not compared line by line. (topic: logs/otel-python-sdk)
- **The Python documentation's stability statement for the logs signal and the `_logs` import paths.** Looked 2026-10-18 at the PyPI classifiers (S-wz5eea44) and the API and SDK source (S-x74lzie5, S-tx2jwcse); the modules are underscore-prefixed and no page read says whether that marks them unstable. Needs the opentelemetry-python documentation's logs pages. [UNK] (topic: logs/otel-python-sdk)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: the project states the logs signal is not stable. The opentelemetry-python README at v1.45.1 shows a status table "Traces Stable, Metrics Stable, Logs Development*" with the note "We are working on stabilizing the Log signal which would require making deprecations and breaking changes"; opentelemetry.io/docs/languages/python/ shows "Traces Stable, Metrics Stable, Logs Development"; CHANGELOG.md carries the same "Breaking changes ongoing" warning. None of these pages says what the underscore in `_logs` modules means, so linking the underscore to that status is an inference. (topic: logs/otel-python-sdk)
- **Whether the SDK log provider builds its default resource with `Resource.create` as the meter and trace providers do, and what the trace provider does after a fork.** Looked 2026-10-19 in `sdk/resources/__init__.py`, `sdk/metrics/_internal/__init__.py` and `sdk/trace/__init__.py` at v1.45.1 (S-4cxou2yj, S-bpum5cvg, S-ai2idcjy): the first two read around the `Resource.create({})` calls, the log provider (`sdk/_logs/_internal/__init__.py`, S-tx2jwcse) not for its resource and no fork handler of the trace provider read. Needs those two code paths at v1.45.1. [UNK] (topic: logs/otel-python-sdk)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: at v1.45.1 the log provider builds its default resource like the others. opentelemetry-sdk/src/opentelemetry/sdk/_logs/_internal/__init__.py LoggerProvider.__init__ (line 883-886) uses Resource.create({}) when resource is None; the same call is in sdk/trace/__init__.py TracerProvider.__init__ (line 1270-1271) and sdk/metrics/_internal/__init__.py (line 447). After a fork: TracerProvider registers os.register_at_fork(after_in_child=...) (line 1289-1296), whose _handle_fork (line 1298-1300) recreates its tracers lock and merges _get_process_dependent_resource() into the resource; LoggerProvider and MeterProvider do the same. The span processor also re-creates its thread pool after fork (line 244-254). (topic: logs/otel-python-sdk)
- **OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_PROTOCOL http/json OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_LOGS_ENDPOINT logs export interval** The kb was asked this in a logged lookup of 2026-10-05, and no article answered it (query log finding F-a098cb2db3d2). Looked in the kb 2026-10-18: `rag.py pack` gives `good`, with this topic in the lead. Needs an official source that states it (`/kb-research`, or the query log's opt-in research). (topic: logs/otel-python-sdk)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21: the spec (specification/protocol/exporter.md on main) lists protocol values grpc, http/protobuf, http/json via OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_PROTOCOL and OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_LOGS_PROTOCOL, says SDKs MUST support at least one of grpc and http/protobuf and "MAY support http/json". The logs endpoint variable OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_LOGS_ENDPOINT is used as-is; otherwise the base endpoint gets v1/logs. Python SDK v1.45.1 sdk/_configuration/__init__.py maps only "grpc" and "http/protobuf" and raises RuntimeError("Unsupported OTLP protocol ... is configured") for http/json. ## logs/prometheus-retention-exposition
- **Whether the Rust `prometheus` crate (tikv/rust-prometheus) 0.14.0 appends `_total` or unit suffixes and rewrites invalid metric names.** Looked 2026-10-11 in the crate's README, Cargo.toml, `src/lib.rs` and `src/encoder/text.rs` at v0.14.0 (S-fr4rpivt, S-lggz7o42, S-fmkur47t): only the format constant and the gather-and-encode flow are stated. Needs the crate's `src/desc.rs` and counter/histogram modules at the same tag. [UNK] (topic: logs/prometheus-retention-exposition)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: read tikv/rust-prometheus v0.14.0 src/desc.rs, src/counter.rs, src/histogram.rs, src/encoder/text.rs. There is no "_total" or unit suffix logic anywhere: the text encoder writes the registered family name unchanged for counters and gauges (write_sample(writer, name, None, ...)) and appends only "_bucket", "_sum" and "_count" for histograms and summaries. Names are not rewritten: Desc::new returns Err(Error::Msg("'<name>' is not a valid metric name")) when is_valid_metric_name fails (desc.rs), after build_fq_name joins namespace, subsystem and name with "_". A counter must therefore be named with "_total" by the caller. (topic: logs/prometheus-retention-exposition)

## auth/http-message-signatures
- **A documented derivation of an Ed25519 signing key from an age X25519 identity.** Looked 2026-10-11 in the age specification (S-bi6fdfos), the age Go `agessh` code (S-xzzrbowg), the libsodium conversion page (S-n3swpr6x) and RFC 7748 (S-s3lqlvdi): all convert Ed25519 to X25519, none the reverse; no page prescribes a key-separated derivation (for example HKDF with a label). Needs a published construction or the operator's choice of one. [UNK] (topic: auth/http-message-signatures)
  - Partly resolved 2026-10-13: Signal's XEdDSA specification (S-lzpaeug7) is a published construction that signs with the X25519 scalar itself and fixes the Edwards sign bit at zero, so an age identity can sign without a derived key, and its recipient is the verification key only when XEdDSA's `k` is the clamped X25519 scalar (the recipient is `X25519(identity, basepoint)`, which clamps; XEdDSA takes `k` as any scalar) (topic: auth/http-message-signatures); a key-separated derivation, such as HKDF with a label, is still not described by any page read.
- **How the 32 bytes of an age X25519 identity become the scalar `k` that XEdDSA signs with.** Looked 2026-10-13 in the XEdDSA specification (S-lzpaeug7: `k` "any scalar", `calculate_key_pair`, no byte-string decoding or clamping stated) and the age specification (S-bi6fdfos: identity is 32 random bytes, recipient is `X25519(identity, basepoint)`): neither says whether RFC 7748 clamping is applied before `calculate_key_pair`, and neither names an age binding or library for XEdDSA. Needs the XEdDSA reference code (libsignal) or an age or sops statement. [UNK] (topic: auth/http-message-signatures)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: libsignal `rust/core/src/curve/curve25519.rs` on main (unpinned read, CODE) applies `scalar::clamp_integer` when a private key is built from 32 bytes, and its signature code then derives the Edwards key from the clamped bytes, with a comment citing the XEdDSA specification; that is Signal's binding only, and no age or sops statement connects an age identity to XEdDSA. A pinned libsignal read plus an age or sops statement would answer it. (topic: auth/http-message-signatures)

- **The byte limit of the unix-socket directory path.** Looked 2026-10-11 in the runtime-config, server-start and creating-cluster pages (S-qudkq4hc, S-ltydjlxa, S-4o7okj5l): none states a number; only a comment in `pqcomm.h` (S-f3p4t6bi) speaks of "100 or so bytes". [UNK] (topic: arch/postgres-bundled-binaries)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21: the PostgreSQL pages state no number, the OS limit is in unix(7) (S-r76zhmhp): 108 bytes on Linux, as short as 92 on some implementations (the macOS and BSD value is not on that page). (topic: arch/postgres-bundled-binaries)
- **Whether the executable bit of a binary inside a wheel's package tree survives an install.** Looked 2026-10-11 in the binary distribution format spec (S-ewbubv2q): it names the bit only for `scripts/`. Needs an installer's documentation or a test. [UNK] (topic: arch/postgres-bundled-binaries)
  - Partly resolved 2026-10-21: the PyPA installer library at 1.1.0 (S-l27ufv76) keeps the execute bit of any wheel member that has it in `external_attr`; pip's own unpacking code and a built-wheel test were not read, and a wheel built without the bit would lose it. (topic: arch/postgres-bundled-binaries)

## arch/postgres-dump-canonical-hash
- **Row order of table data in a `pg_dump`.** Looked 2026-10-11 in the pg_dump page (S-vykvhstk) and the dump source (S-vor6jmfj): COPY runs without ORDER BY and no sentence promises an order. Needs the PostgreSQL statement or a clustered-table test. [UNK] (topic: arch/postgres-dump-canonical-hash)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21: the SELECT page (S-baxltdzt) guarantees no order without ORDER BY and the pg_dump and COPY pages say nothing of row order, so the dump's data order is not promised; a dump-after-UPDATE-and-VACUUM comparison would show the practice. (topic: arch/postgres-dump-canonical-hash)
- **Whether `-Z` gzip output of a plain dump is byte-stable.** Not checked; the article advises hashing the uncompressed output. [UNK] (topic: arch/postgres-dump-canonical-hash)
  - Partly resolved 2026-10-21: the pg_dump page says a nonzero level on plain output compresses the whole file "as though it had been fed through gzip", and compress_gzip.c at REL_18_6 writes through zlib, whose gzip header (deflate.c at v1.3.1) carries mtime 0; the output is therefore expected to be byte-identical for the same input, level, zlib version and OS code, but no source promises it and no two compressed dumps were hashed. Hashing the uncompressed text stays the design. Backed by `arch/postgres-dump-canonical-hash.md:18` (topic: arch/postgres-dump-canonical-hash)
- Whether pg_dump rejects --restrict-key for non-plain formats with a fatal error is not shown in dumputils.c (REL_18_6); would need pg_dump.c at the same tag, which this run was not permitted to fetch. (topic: arch/postgres-dump-canonical-hash)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21: pg_dump.c at REL_18_6 (S-vor6jmfj) calls `pg_fatal` for `--restrict-key` with any non-plain format; not run. (topic: arch/postgres-dump-canonical-hash)

## logs/python-rust-json-stdout-logs
- **Flushing of Python's `StreamHandler` on a pipe and of a `tracing-subscriber` stdout writer at exit.** Looked 2026-10-13 in the `logging.handlers` page (S-7v3dgzq4: `StreamHandler.flush` calls the stream's `flush`; no statement on when it is called) and in `tracing-subscriber` 0.3.23's `fmt` sources (S-fmhy7rbl); the non-blocking appender crate was not read. Needs the `StreamHandler.emit` source at a tag and the `tracing-appender` documentation. [UNK] (topic: logs/python-rust-json-stdout-logs)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: CPython Lib/logging/__init__.py at v3.14.0, StreamHandler.emit does stream.write(msg + self.terminator) and then self.flush() inside the same try, so each record is flushed; logging also registers atexit.register(shutdown) which flushes and closes handlers. Rust: tracing-subscriber 0.3.23 fmt defaults to io::stdout (fmt/mod.rs: make_writer: io::stdout), and std Stdout at 1.90.0 (library/std/src/io/stdio.rs) wraps a LineWriter, with a FIXME that it is not tty/pipe aware, so each full line is written at the newline. tracing-appender 0.2.4 non_blocking writes on a background thread; WorkerGuard flushes on drop, but a panic abort or std::process::exit may lose lines. (topic: logs/python-rust-json-stdout-logs)
- **Where a Rust panic message goes beside a `tracing` JSON subscriber.** Looked 2026-10-13 only in `tracing-subscriber` (S-fmhy7rbl, S-bkz7cb64), which does not mention panics. Needs the standard library's panic hook documentation and the `tracing-panic` or hook examples of the `tracing` project. [UNK] (topic: logs/python-rust-json-stdout-logs)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: the std::panic::set_hook page (doc.rust-lang.org/std/panic/fn.set_hook.html) says "The default hook, which is registered at startup, prints a message to standard error and generates a backtrace if requested", and the hook can be replaced with set_hook. Source library/std/src/panicking.rs at 1.90.0 default_hook writes to panic_output(), which is stderr, or to a captured test output. tracing-subscriber 0.3.23 does not install a panic hook (no panic hook code in fmt), so without a custom hook the panic text goes to stderr as plain text, not as a JSON log line on stdout. Third-party hook crates such as tracing-panic were not read. (topic: logs/python-rust-json-stdout-logs)

Still needs a probe on a Windows and a macOS host (topic: agents/headless-agent-runtimes)

(topic: agents/headless-agent-runtimes)

Billing-and-usage pages may carry it. (topic: agents/headless-agent-runtimes)

## python/rusage-maxrss-units
- **The unit of `ru_maxrss` in the usage that Python's `os.wait4` and `resource.getrusage` return, as Python's own pages state it.** Looked 2026-10-26 in Python's `resource` and `os` pages (S-2ua7dl2m, S-5brdhqgo): the `resource` table says "maximum resident set size" and defers to `getrusage(2)`, `os.wait4` defers to `getrusage`, neither names a unit. ## python/percentile-nearest-rank

- **Which quartile method an outlier fence should use, and the smallest list for which a fence is meaningful.** Looked 2026-10-19 in the `statistics.quantiles` section of the Python `statistics` page (S-dh7f43kc) and the NIST handbook page on outliers (S-4hnlw34j): the handbook's one worked example computes its quartiles at position `p * (N + 1)` (the `exclusive` method) and states the 1.5 and 3 multipliers, and the Python page says only that the data should hold more points than `n` and when each method suits; neither names the method a fence must use or a minimum sample. A search of Python's page for outlier or fence words found none. Needs a statistics text or standard that states the quartile definition behind Tukey's fences, or the original source of the 1.5 and 3 multipliers. [UNK] (topic: python/percentile-nearest-rank)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: a web search found only calculators and blogs that credit Tukey's hinges and the 1.5 and 3 multipliers and note that the quartile definition moves the fences; Tukey's 1977 book was not reached, and `statistics.rst` at v3.14.7 documents `quantiles(data, *, n=4, method='exclusive')` with nothing on fences. Still needs a statistics text or standard that states the quartile definition behind the fences, or the original source of the multipliers (topic: python/percentile-nearest-rank)
- **Whether `statistics.quantiles` gives the same cut points on every Python 3.8 to 3.13 release.** Looked 2026-10-19 in the same page: it records the 3.8 addition and the 3.13 change for a single data point, and lists no other change; the kb ran only 3.13.2. Needs the "What's New" pages or the CPython history of `quantiles` for 3.8 to 3.12, or a run on those releases. [UNK] (topic: python/percentile-nearest-rank)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: `statistics.rst` at v3.14.7 searched for `quantiles`: the signature and the method text, no version-history sentence beyond the 3.8 addition and the 3.13 single-point change already noted. Still needs the "What's New" pages or the CPython history of `quantiles` for 3.8 to 3.12, or a run on those releases (topic: python/percentile-nearest-rank)

## gitlab/commit-signing-and-provenance
- **Whether the push rule Reject unsigned commits needs a signature GitLab can verify for the pushing user, or accepts any valid signature.** Looked 2026-10-15 in the push rules page (S-lu25cylz) and the signed commits, GPG, SSH and X.509 pages (S-jut4uqct, S-hthqd5dx, S-gqe643np, S-gfhrhfsb), all at master @9f1632e2: the rule asks only for "a valid cryptographic signature" and rejects invalid or corrupt ones. Needs the push rule check in the GitLab source read at a pinned commit, or a docs statement. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/commit-signing-and-provenance)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21 from code: the push rule checks only that the commit has a signature, not that GitLab can verify it for the pusher. ee/lib/ee/gitlab/checks/push_rules/commit_check.rb @29a9622b returns "Commit must be signed" when !updated_from_web? && !push_rule.commit_signature_allowed?(commit); ee/app/models/concerns/push_ruleable.rb commit_signature_allowed? returns commit.has_signature?; app/models/commit.rb has_signature? is signature_type && signature_type != :NONE (type from Gitaly's commit data), with no key or user lookup. The docs (doc/user/project/repository/push_rules.md) say signed with GPG, SSH or X.509 and "Commits with invalid or corrupt signatures are rejected"; the code path read does not show a validity check beyond the type, so any difference lies in Gitaly's type detection (not read). Written to gitlab/commit-signing-and-provenance.md:111. (topic: gitlab/commit-signing-and-provenance)
- **Whether a service account's generated no-reply email counts as a verified email for commit signature verification and the Reject unverified users push rule.** Looked 2026-10-15 in the service accounts page (S-yg2mm5c6), the user keys API (S-wyjdebjb) and the signed commits and push rules pages (S-jut4uqct, S-lu25cylz): the service account page gives the generated prefixes and says a custom email needs confirmation, and no page says whether the generated address is verified. Needs the service accounts API page or the user model code read at a pinned commit. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/commit-signing-and-provenance)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21 from code: the generated service account address is created confirmed. Users::ServiceAccounts::CreateService#skip_confirmation (app/services/users/service_accounts/create_service.rb @29a9622b) returns true when auto_generated_email_address? (email == username_and_email_generator.email, domain User::NOREPLY_EMAIL_DOMAIN); a custom address goes through confirmation unless email confirmation is off. User#verified_emails includes email when primary_email_verified? (app/models/user.rb), and User#verified_email? checks that list, which the committer check of the push rules uses. The docs (doc/user/profile/service_accounts.md, "Service account emails") say only that custom emails need confirmation. Whether a commit signature verifies for the account depends on its keys, not read. Written to gitlab/commit-signing-and-provenance.md:112. (topic: gitlab/commit-signing-and-provenance)
- **The author and committer address GitHub puts on a commit made in its web interface or by a merge button, and whether a rewrite of history keeps a commit's own signature.** Looked 2026-10-19 in the commit email, email address reference, signature verification and multiple-author pages of docs.github.com (S-5636dvek, S-k6iukhsi, S-ijgc3zl2, S-jw2of5h6), the `gitmailmap`, `git-filter-branch`, `git-commit-tree` pages at v2.55.0 and `git-filter-repo` at v2.47.0 (S-anknmecs, S-tt4wcwww, S-4kcovuch, S-27g42e5z): GitHub's pages name the two `users.noreply.github.com` forms and say web commits are signed with the key at `github.com/web-flow.gpg`, none names the address; the filter-repo page names the stripping of signed tags only. Needs GitHub's page on web-made commits and the filter-repo or fast-export documentation of signed commits, or a probe on a test repository. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/commit-signing-and-provenance)
  - Tried 2026-10-21, still open: the committer address is partly stated. github/docs main (commit efd4783c2a), available-rules-for-rulesets.md: for committer-email restrictions the pattern must also include GitHub's own `noreply` address at the `github.com` domain "for web-based merges and other commits created on" github.com (the page spells the address out). The author address of a web commit is not stated on any page read (the user's chosen address). On rewrites: git-fast-export at v2.55.0 says --signed-commits defaults to "strip" ("same as how earlier versions ... behaved"), while git-fast-import defaults to "verbatim"; git-filter-branch only says a signed tag has its signature stripped. filter-repo not re-read. A probe commit would show the author line. (topic: gitlab/commit-signing-and-provenance)
- **Whether bot users of access tokens or service accounts may approve merge requests.** Looked 2026-10-15 in the approval rules page (S442), the approval settings page (S440) and the project access token page (S-cfpw3yy4): eligibility asks for direct membership and a role, and none names bot users or service accounts. Needs an approvals page or the approval eligibility code read at a pinned commit. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/commit-signing-and-provenance)
  - Partly resolved 2026-10-21 from code, no lab run: no bot exclusion was found. MergeRequestPolicy#can_approve? @29a9622b is can?(:update_merge_request) && is_container_member?; EE adds group approvers and role_enables_admin_merge_request; ApprovalService#execute and ApprovalState#eligible_for_approval_by? (ee/app/models/approval_state.rb) use user.can?(:approve_merge_request) plus author/committer rules, none referencing bot or service_account types. A project or group access token bot user and a service account are members, so they pass if their role and the rules allow. The approval pages (approvals/_index.md, rules.md, settings.md) name no bot users. Still open: a call as a bot (approvals by bots may be filtered in approval rule eligibility or UI; not read). The policy and approval-state half is written to gitlab/mr-approvals.md:39 and gitlab/mr-approvals.md:40. (topic: gitlab/commit-signing-and-provenance)

## agents/shared-state-concurrency
- **Whether Linux file systems (ext4, XFS, tmpfs) hand a freed inode number to the next file created, and how soon.** Looked 2026-10-18 in POSIX.1-2024 `sys/stat.h` (S-7my6ujm5), which allows reuse after the last unlink and the space being freed and sets no delay, and by a macOS probe (200 create, unlink, create rounds, no number reused). Needs the Linux `inode(7)` or file-system documentation, or the same probe on Linux. [UNK] (topic: agents/shared-state-concurrency)
  - Narrowed 2026-10-21: the Linux `inode(7)` page says an inode number is unique within a file system and says nothing on reuse; the kernel's ext4 allocator (`fs/ext4/ialloc.c` at commit 98c949b9, S-3az5pk5l) skips a free inode deleted less than 60 seconds ago (360 when its table block is dirty) only on a file system without a journal, so a journaled ext4 hands a freed number out at once; written to `agents/shared-state-concurrency.md:289`. XFS and tmpfs were not read, and no probe ran on Linux (topic: agents/shared-state-concurrency)
- **Whether MinIO accepts the `If-None-Match: *` create-if-absent form, and which conditional headers the community edition supports.** Looked 2026-10-15 in MinIO AIStor's S3 compatibility page (S-ukfteilx), which lists the headers but not the `*` form or an edition split, and the closed issue minio/minio#20346 (S-opdaj4dk), where maintainers showed the bare `*` accepted in 2024 (COMMUNITY). Needs a MinIO docs statement or a test on the deployed version. [UNK] (topic: agents/shared-state-concurrency)
  - Narrowed 2026-10-15: the open-source code at its last commit treats `*` as matching any ETag in `checkPreconditionsPUT`, wired into PutObject and the multipart create and complete handlers (S-75jmw3lf, S-s7uvhjek, S-3l6vhq2q), and the repository README says it is no longer maintained (S-vseohdra). Still open: AIStor's own statement on `*`, and whether the check and the write are atomic under a race. (topic: agents/shared-state-concurrency)
- **SeaweedFS conditional writes: which operations and bucket modes enforce If-Match and If-None-Match.** The S3 API wiki (S-a3umo362), read 2026-10-15, only lists the four headers. Needs the SeaweedFS S3 docs or source read at a pinned commit. [UNK] (topic: agents/shared-state-concurrency)
  - Narrowed 2026-10-15: `checkConditionalHeaders` and `validateConditionalHeaders` at master @84512927 (S-jnn4ww4t) reject `If-None-Match: *` on an existing latest version. Still open: whether two racing conditional writes can both pass, since the check returns before the write. (topic: agents/shared-state-concurrency)
- **Beyond the keys attached to an etcd lease being deleted when the lease expires or is revoked (answered in the article): may a client other than the lease holder write a leased key, or attach its own key to that lease ID?** Looked 2026-10-17 in the etcd API learning page (S-6hgplow2: keys and leases are tied by the `lease` ID; no owner, holder check or permission on a lease) and the gRPC API reference for `PutRequest` (its `lease` field is "the lease ID to associate with the key", `ignore_lease` keeps the current lease; no word on who may use an ID; the reference is not a registered source). Needs the etcd authentication guide (permissions are per key range) and the lessor code at a pinned commit (a `CODE` fact), or a lab put with a second client. [UNK] (topic: agents/shared-state-concurrency)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: the etcd v3.6 RBAC page says nothing on leases; the NVD record of CVE-2026-44283 (published 2026-05-14, advisory GHSA-x35m-3gp4-4fh5) says that before 3.4.44, 3.5.30 and 3.6.11 a Put inside a transaction could attach a lease despite insufficient read or lease-related permissions, which implies attachment is checked against permissions outside transactions in fixed versions, and does not say whether a client may attach its key to a lease ID another client holds. Still needs the lessor code at a pinned commit or a lab put with a second client (topic: agents/shared-state-concurrency)
- **NATS JetStream object store, key-value replicas and authentication, and Valkey publish/subscribe were not researched** in the 2026-10-15 pass, which read the NATS key-value learn pages (S-ulnpjwb7, S-x3firlbx, S-pheyvvix, S-ntlmfsuw) and the JetStream concepts page (S-zv2dqsow). Needs the object store and security pages of docs.nats.io and the Valkey pub/sub topic. [UNK] (topic: agents/shared-state-concurrency)
  - Narrowed 2026-10-15: the NATS object store (S-523ddbfr) and security (S-v7s32e7q) deep dives and Valkey Pub/Sub (S-bayxg4xz) are now facts. Still open: replica counts and their limits, and whether the object store has a conditional put. (topic: agents/shared-state-concurrency)
- **Are the keys attached to an etcd lease deleted when the lease expires or is revoked, and may a client other than the lease holder write a leased key?** The kb was asked this in a logged lookup of 2026-10-17, and no article answered it (query log finding F-f5aa2bfb15f5). Looked in the kb 2026-10-17: `rag.py pack` gives `weak`, with this topic in the lead. Needs an official source that states it (`/kb-research`, or the query log's opt-in research). (topic: agents/shared-state-concurrency)
  - Resolved 2026-10-20: the first half was in `agents/shared-state-concurrency.md` (keys attached to a lease are deleted when it expires or is revoked, S-6hgplow2); the second is answered by etcd v3.6.5's auth applier (S-jxkwd67p): no field names the client that granted a lease, a put naming a lease needs write permission on every key already attached to it (unless authentication is off or the user is an admin), and `LeaseRevoke` has the same check. The etcd documentation pages say nothing of an owner. (topic: agents/shared-state-concurrency)

  - Resolved 2026-10-21 (from code, not a run): report.rb and status.rb at commit e227c27 (S-o4hirh7b, S-y5y7ikkk) show only successful events set the change bit, so a noop run with pending changes and no failures gives status 0; a run with `--noop --detailed-exitcodes` on a lab node would confirm it. (topic: arch/puppet-agent-run-noop-reporting)
- **Which of `corrective_change` and `noop_pending` a PuppetDB or console consumer should read for noop drift.** Looked 2026-10-15 in `lib/puppet/transaction/report.rb` and `lib/puppet/resource/status.rb` at commit e227c27 (S-wwls72e7, S-xsvufwtx): both fields exist on the report; the code comments say `corrective_change` includes noop corrective changes, and no page read says which one drift reporting is meant to use. Needs the PuppetDB report format page or Puppet Enterprise node-status documentation read in full. [UNK] (topic: arch/puppet-agent-run-noop-reporting)
  - Partly resolved 2026-10-21: the field comments in report.rb (S-o4hirh7b) define both (`noop_pending` is the flag for changes not applied because of noop); no page says which drift reporting should read, and the PuppetDB report format page was not read. (topic: arch/puppet-agent-run-noop-reporting)

## security/almalinux-repositories-vault-pins
- **An AlmaLinux page that documents holding a host on one minor release, and whether `mirrors.almalinux.org` answers a mirrorlist request for a minor such as `9.4`.** Looked 2026-10-15 in the wiki's repositories pages and FAQ (S-46pbwuai, S-abrr4vdk, S-23s23lr2), the `almalinux-release` repo files (S-radwxzmz, S-k5qsqa2h) and a web search limited to almalinux.org and git.almalinux.org: only the 9.4 directory README (S-ecy4u4kc) says to use the vault; the mirrorlist endpoint was not requested. Needs the wiki or blog page on staying at a minor, or a read-only request to the mirrorlist url. [UNK] (topic: security/almalinux-repositories-vault-pins)
  - Partly resolved 2026-10-21: a read-only request to mirrors.almalinux.org/mirrorlist/9.4/baseos answered HTTP 200 with the single url http://vault.almalinux.org/9.4/BaseOS/$basearch/os/, while /mirrorlist/9/baseos returned 9.8 mirrors, and the vault index lists 8.3 to 8.10, 9.0 to 9.8 and 10.0 to 10.2; no wiki or blog page documenting how to hold a host on one minor was found, and the FAQ is silent. Backed by `security/almalinux-repositories-vault-pins.md:25` (topic: security/almalinux-repositories-vault-pins)
- **The `$releasever` value dnf derives on an AlmaLinux 9 host and which package provides it.** Looked 2026-10-15 in dnf's variable documentation (S-b3frgffj: derived from the RPM database) and the repo files (S-radwxzmz); the release package's provides were not read. Needs the `almalinux-release` spec or a package query. [UNK] (topic: security/almalinux-repositories-vault-pins)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21: the almalinux-release spec on branch a9 (git.almalinux.org/rpms/almalinux-release) has Provides system-release(releasever) = %{major} and system-release(releasever_minor) = %{minor}, so $releasever on AlmaLinux 9 is the major number 9 and almalinux-release provides it; the dnf docs (dnf.readthedocs.io conf_ref) say only that $releasever comes from the RPMDB. Backed by `security/almalinux-repositories-vault-pins.md:31`, `security/almalinux-repositories-vault-pins.md:36` (topic: security/almalinux-repositories-vault-pins)

## security/zsh-completion-system
- **Whether the script printed by `dsc completer zsh` starts with a `#compdef dsc` line or is meant to be evaluated.** Looked 2026-10-15 in the Zsh manual's completion chapter (S-wkxevnxu: the `#compdef` and `compdef` conventions) and in the kb's DSC article (`dsc/cli-reference.md:34`, S116: the command and its shells only); the generated script itself was not read. Needs the output of `dsc completer zsh` from the pinned DSC 3.3.0 release or the `completer` source at that tag. [UNK] (topic: security/zsh-completion-system)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21: ran `dsc completer zsh` from the pinned 3.3.0 macOS arm64 release (S116, archive sha256 matches the source row): the script starts with `#compdef dsc`, defines `_dsc` and ends with `if [ "$funcstack[1]" = "_dsc" ]; then _dsc "$@"; else compdef _dsc dsc; fi`, so it works as a file named `_dsc` found through `fpath` and when evaluated. Recorded in dsc/cli-reference.md. (topic: security/zsh-completion-system)

## security/secret-shapes-in-text
- **An official list of the key names that mark a `key=value` assignment as a secret.** Looked 2026-10-17 in the HTTP, URI and PEM RFCs (S-wct3uoax, S-bs6jgunr, S-kskf5yv7), the SqlClient and Purview connection string pages (S-xnlryf7y, S-k57sryj7), the AWS and Claude API pages (S-d2kitjup, S-bcbpadtc) and detect-secrets 1.5.0 (S-spzetxhy): the specifications fix the value grammar of a header, a URL and a key block, Microsoft's detector names `Password` and `pwd` for connection strings only, and the only list of general assignment keys is one community tool's deny-list. Needs a standard or vendor list, or a decision that the tool's list is the working one. [UNK] (topic: security/secret-shapes-in-text)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: no standard fixes secret key names, but Sentry's server-side scrubbing page (docs.sentry.io/security-legal-pii/scrubbing/server-side-scrubbing) lists password, secret, passwd, api_key, apikey, auth, credentials, mysql_pwd, privatekey, private_key, token and bearer as default key-name matches, the same set as Relay's PASSWORD_KEY_REGEX, and the OWASP Logging Cheat Sheet names data classes only; whether the kb accepts the Sentry list as the working one is a decision, not a source. (topic: security/secret-shapes-in-text)
- **Authorization schemes other than Bearer and Basic, and headers that carry a credential outside `Authorization`.** Not read 2026-10-17: the research covered the two schemes with a registered grammar (RFC 7617, RFC 6750) and the `x-api-key` header of the Claude API; Digest (RFC 7616), vendor private-token headers, cookies and `Set-Cookie` values were left out. Needs those specifications or vendor pages read for a separate pass. [UNK] (topic: security/secret-shapes-in-text)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: the IANA HTTP authentication scheme registry lists Basic, Bearer, Concealed, Digest, DPoP, GNAP, HOBA, Mutual, Negotiate, OAuth, PrivateToken, SCRAM-SHA-1, SCRAM-SHA-256 and vapid; in RFC 7616 Digest only `response` derives from the password; RFC 6265 gives the cookie syntax and RFC 9110 lists Authorization and Proxy-Authorization; vendor private-token headers beyond x-api-key and the RFC 9110 credentials ABNF were not read. (topic: security/secret-shapes-in-text)
- **Which URL path parameter names carry a credential.** Looked 2026-10-17 in RFC 3986 (S-bs6jgunr) section 3.3: it says producers often use `;` and `=` for segment parameters and gives `name;v=1.1`, and names no parameter that holds a secret; no standard or vendor list of such names was read. Needs a vendor page that documents a credential carried as a path parameter. [UNK] (topic: security/secret-shapes-in-text)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: the Jakarta Servlet 6.0 specification (jakarta.ee/specifications/servlet/6.0), section 7.1.3 URL Rewriting, requires the session id path parameter to be named `jsessionid`, so `;jsessionid=<id>` in a path segment carries a session credential; this is one documented name, not a complete list, and RFC 3986 names none. (topic: security/secret-shapes-in-text)
- **How a deployed scanner reads escaped JSON (`\"key\": \"value\"`).** Not read 2026-10-17: the escape facts rest on RFC 8259 (S-4rjxcq6v) alone, and the detect-secrets rules (S-spzetxhy) were read for the key and separator patterns, not run against a doubly escaped string. Needs that behaviour observed or a scanner's documentation that states it. [UNK] (topic: security/secret-shapes-in-text)
  - Partly resolved 2026-10-21: the detect-secrets keyword plugin source (raw.githubusercontent.com/Yelp/detect-secrets/master/detect_secrets/plugins/keyword.py) has a quote class of single quote, double quote and backtick, a value pattern that excludes quotes and no backslash handling, so by reading it an escaped quote would end the value early; this was not observed in a run and no scanner documentation states it. Backed by `security/secret-shapes-in-text.md:77`, `security/secret-shapes-in-text.md:78` (topic: security/secret-shapes-in-text)
- **A statement of the bearer-token rule for plain `http`, and how Markdown renderers other than CommonMark read `[label]:` lines.** Looked 2026-10-18 in RFC 6750 (S-neu52zjd), searched for `plain` and `http`: the RFC states the TLS MUST and says nothing of a plain `http` request; the CommonMark 0.31.2 spec (S-jkdywrys) fixes the definition syntax, and no other renderer's rules (GitHub-flavoured, chat-app Markdown) were read. Needs the RFC's updating text (RFC 9700 or the OAuth 2.1 draft) and a renderer's documentation read for a separate pass. [UNK] (topic: security/secret-shapes-in-text)
  - Partly resolved 2026-10-21: RFC 6750 section 5.3 says 'Clients MUST always use TLS (https) or equivalent transport security when making requests with bearer tokens' without naming plain http, RFC 9700 (updates 6750) only recommends end-to-end TLS in the part read, and the OAuth 2.1 draft (draft-ietf-oauth-v2-1-16) repeats the TLS requirement; the GFM spec (github.github.com/gfm) defines link reference definitions as CommonMark does, and chat-app Markdown renderers were not read. Backed by `security/secret-shapes-in-text.md:26`, `mcp/authorization.md:30` (topic: security/secret-shapes-in-text)
- **Which comment prefixes (`#`, `//`) a pasted key block keeps on its lines.** Looked 2026-10-23 in the kb (`rag.py pack`, `rag.py search --index`), RFC 7468 (S-kskf5yv7), the CommonMark and GFM specs (S-jkdywrys, S-cbxnheng): they fix the quote marker and the table cell, and RFC 7468 says only that a parser should skip non-base64 characters; the comment marker belongs to each language (`#` in shell and YAML, `//` in C-family languages), so no single standard says a key block keeps its line structure behind one, and no language specification was read. Needs the comment rules of the languages the scan cares about, or a decision that the shape is treated as a scan rule with no source. [UNK] (topic: security/secret-shapes-in-text)
- **How a lenient PEM reader treats a body line behind a prefix.** Looked 2026-10-23 in RFC 7468 (S-kskf5yv7): its advice is that a parser should ignore non-base64 characters while its lax grammar allows only whitespace and base64 characters between the boundaries; no decoder (OpenSSL, Python `ssl`, a PGP tool) was run against a prefixed block. Needs one run of a named reader against a placeholder block with `> ` or `# ` before each line. [UNK] (topic: security/secret-shapes-in-text)
- **Unicode NFC normalization and casefold for text matching** The kb was asked this in a logged lookup of 2026-10-27, and no article answered it (query log finding F-4570b8e9ee15). Looked in the kb 2026-10-27: `rag.py pack` gives `none`, with this topic in the lead. Needs an official source that states it (`/kb-research`, or the query log's opt-in research). (topic: security/secret-shapes-in-text)

## windows/cmd-exe-syntax-quoting-redirection
- **Whether `2>&1` written before `> file` sends stderr to the console or to the file.** Looked 2026-10-15 in the "Using command redirection operators" page (S-gp367igh, a Windows XP-era TechNet article under `previous-versions`) and the `cmd` page (S-yna7gijv): the examples put `> file` before `2>&1` and no sentence states the order rule. Needs a probe on a Windows host (`cmd /c "dir nofile 2>&1 > out.txt"` against `dir nofile > out.txt 2>&1`) or a current Learn page that states it. [UNK] (topic: windows/cmd-exe-syntax-quoting-redirection)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: no current Learn page states the order rule. Microsoft's Old New Thing post "Beware of digits before the redirection operator" (devblogs.microsoft.com/oldnewthing/20060517-00) reads `some-command >output 2>&1` left to right: "Put the normal output into the file output, and then change the error output stream (2) to refer to the normal output stream (1)", so the reverse order would point stream 2 at the console's stream 1 before the file is attached; the reversed example itself is not shown anywhere read. Needs the probe on a Windows host. (topic: windows/cmd-exe-syntax-quoting-redirection)
- **At which stage `cmd` expands `%` and how `^` and quotes interact with it in a batch file against the prompt.** Looked 2026-10-15 in the `cmd`, `set`, `echo`, `call` and `for` pages (S-yna7gijv, S-jbeh3qg6, S-gydqaolr, S-5h7fuwx3, S-5b57ykp4): they give the escapes for `& | < > ^ !` and `%%` for `for` variables but no parsing order. `set /?` and `cmd /?` hold more text but are not on Learn. Needs the output of `cmd /?` and `set /?` read on a host, or a Microsoft article on batch parsing. [UNK] (topic: windows/cmd-exe-syntax-quoting-redirection)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: Microsoft-hosted sources fix the first step only. The DelayedExpansion registry entry page (Windows Server 2003 reference, previous-versions) says "By default, Cmd.exe expands variables when it reads a line of text", and Raymond Chen's post (devblogs.microsoft.com/oldnewthing/20060823-00) says that on the command line and in batch files "environment variable expansion occurs when the command is read"; his post 20091029-00 says each parsing pass added to the batch processor needs another layer of escaping. No Microsoft page gives the full order of `%`, `^` and quote handling. Needs `cmd /?` and `set /?` on a host or a probe. (topic: windows/cmd-exe-syntax-quoting-redirection)
- **Whether the 8191-character limit holds on Windows 10, Windows 11 and current Windows Server.** Looked 2026-10-15 in the KB 830473 article (S-e4zz24dq): its "Applies to" line names Windows Server 2012 R2, Windows Server 2008 R2 SP1 and Windows 7 SP1 only, and the `cmd` page (S-yna7gijv) gives the 8,192-byte and 65,536-character environment figures without a version. Needs a current Microsoft statement or a probe of a 8,200-character `cmd /c` line on a supported release. [UNK] (topic: windows/cmd-exe-syntax-quoting-redirection)
  - Narrowed 2026-10-21: the Learn page for KB 830473 (troubleshoot/windows-client/shell-experience/command-line-string-limitation) now carries the header "Applies to: Supported versions of Windows Client" above a body line that still names Windows Server 2012 R2, Windows Server 2008 R2 SP1 and Windows 7 SP1; the 8191 figure is unchanged and no current release is named, and the `cmd` page gives separate environment limits (8,192 bytes per variable, 65,536 characters in total). Needs a probe of a line over 8191 characters on a supported release. Backed by `windows/cmd-exe-syntax-quoting-redirection.md:66` (topic: windows/cmd-exe-syntax-quoting-redirection)

## windows/cmd-exe-variables-expansion-errorlevel
- **A worked case for delayed expansion (`!var!` against `%var%` inside a `( ... )` block or `for` body).** Looked 2026-10-15 in the `cmd`, `set` and `setlocal` pages (S-yna7gijv, S-jbeh3qg6, S-33ugnqz2): they say `!` substitutes at run time and how to switch it on, with no example that shows the difference. Needs the `set /?` help text from a host or a Microsoft article on delayed expansion. [UNK] (topic: windows/cmd-exe-variables-expansion-errorlevel)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: the Microsoft-hosted blog post "Delayed Environment Variable Expansion" (learn.microsoft.com/archive/blogs/msdn/ben/delayed-environment-variable-expansion) reproduces the Windows 2000 FAQ example: with `for %%i in (*) do set LIST=%LIST% %%i` the variable is expanded once per statement, so `LIST` ends with only File3.txt; with `cmd /V:ON` or `setlocal ENABLEDELAYEDEXPANSION` and `set LIST=!LIST! %%i` it ends with File1.txt File2.txt File3.txt. Source type: an employee blog kept in the Learn archive, not a product page. (topic: windows/cmd-exe-variables-expansion-errorlevel)
- **Which commands are internal to `cmd.exe` and which are separate executables.** Looked 2026-10-15 in the Windows Commands index and A-Z list (S-ikuzvclu), the `cmd` page's list of commands changed by extensions (S-yna7gijv) and the `help` page: none marks internal commands. Needs the output of `cmd /c help` on a host (it lists the internal commands) or a Microsoft statement. [UNK] (topic: windows/cmd-exe-variables-expansion-errorlevel)
  - Tried 2026-10-21, still open: re-read the Learn `cmd` page (it names "the built-in directory commands cd, md, or rd" and the error "is not recognized as an internal or external command") and the `help` page; none lists which commands are internal. Needs the output of `cmd /c help` on a host or a Microsoft statement. (topic: windows/cmd-exe-variables-expansion-errorlevel)
- **The exit code of `cmd /c "<program>"` against the program's own.** Looked 2026-10-15 in the `cmd` and `exit` pages (S-yna7gijv, S-z3njred5): they say `exit` sets the interpreter's process exit code and `cmd /c` ends after the command, not what code results. Needs a probe (`cmd /c exit 3` and `cmd /c "program"` read with `echo %errorlevel%` from another `cmd`). [UNK] (topic: windows/cmd-exe-variables-expansion-errorlevel)
  - Tried 2026-10-21, still open: the Learn `cmd` page says "If a command completes an operation successfully, it returns an exit code of zero (0) or no exit code" and the `exit` page says that when quitting the command interpreter "the process exit code is set to that number"; neither states what `cmd /c "<program>"` returns when the program fails. Needs the probe on a Windows host. (topic: windows/cmd-exe-variables-expansion-errorlevel)

## windows/robocopy
- **The longest path robocopy copies, and whether long paths work without `/256` being named.** Looked 2026-10-15 in the robocopy page (S-3lm4tsvh) and its public mirror source (`robocopy.md`, ms.date 03/17/2025): the only sentence is the `/256` row, "turns off support for paths longer than 256 characters"; the xcopy page (S-w4neuhkd) names an insufficient-memory error above 255 characters. No limit figure, no mention of `\\?\` paths or the `LongPathsEnabled` policy. Needs a Microsoft statement or a probe with a path over 260 characters on a host. [UNK] (topic: windows/robocopy)
  - Tried 2026-10-21, still open: re-read the Learn robocopy page (last updated 2025-03-17): the `/256` row is unchanged ("Turns off support for paths longer than 256 characters"); no length figure, no `\\?\` form, no `LongPathsEnabled` mention. Needs a Microsoft statement or a probe with a path over 260 characters. (topic: windows/robocopy)
- **The exit-code bit flags, the value 4 and the values above 8 (the fatal-error case).** Looked 2026-10-15 in the robocopy page (S-3lm4tsvh): the table lists 0, 1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 7 and 8 and the note "8 or greater is a failure"; it names no flag table, no 4 and no 16. Needs a Microsoft statement (the `robocopy /?` footer is not on Learn) or a probe on a host. [UNK] (topic: windows/robocopy)
  - Tried 2026-10-21, still open: the Learn robocopy page and KB 954404 (troubleshoot/windows-server/backup-and-storage/return-codes-used-robocopy-utility) still listed only 0, 1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 7 and 8 with "8 or greater" as failure. A Microsoft-hosted archive post by a Microsoft Services consultant (learn.microsoft.com/archive/blogs/deploymentguys/robocopy-exit-codes) listed 1 files copied, 2 extra files, 4 mismatched files, 8 copy failures after the retry limit, 16 nothing copied (command line or rights), which fits a bit-flag reading (5 = 1 + 4); no product page states the bit table, 4 or 16. The article holds no tagged fact for the KB or the archive post. Needs the `robocopy /?` footer or a probe. (topic: windows/robocopy)
- **Whether `/MIR` and `/PURGE` delete destination files that `/XF` or `/XD` exclude.** Looked 2026-10-15 in the robocopy page (S-3lm4tsvh): the `/XF`, `/XD`, `/XX` and `/PURGE` rows do not say how they combine. Needs a probe with a scratch tree (`/L` run with `/MIR /XF <name>`) or a Microsoft statement. [UNK] (topic: windows/robocopy)
  - Tried 2026-10-21, still open: the Learn robocopy page says `/purge` "Deletes destination files and directories that no longer exist in the source" and `/mir` is "equivalent to /e plus /purge"; the `/xf` and `/xd` rows say only that matching files and directories are excluded. Nothing states how they combine; the Azure File Sync migration pages only repeat that `/MIR` purges target items missing on the source. Needs a `/L` probe on a scratch tree. (topic: windows/robocopy)
- **The ACL a copied file receives when `S` is not in `/COPY`, and what `/NODCOPY` does.** Looked 2026-10-15 in the robocopy page (S-3lm4tsvh): the `/COPY` and `/SEC` rows list the flags but not the resulting ACL, and the `/NODCOPY` row reads "Copies no directory info (the default /dcopy:DA is done)". Needs a probe on NTFS volumes or a Microsoft statement. [UNK] (topic: windows/robocopy)
  - Tried 2026-10-21, still open: the Learn robocopy page still gave the `/COPY` default as DAT (D, A, T) with S for the NTFS ACL and the `/NODCOPY` row unchanged; it states no resulting ACL. KB 310316 (troubleshoot/windows-client/windows-security/permissions-on-copying-moving-files, about Windows Explorer) says an object copied "inherits permissions from its parent object" and only a move within one volume keeps the original permissions; that is the general rule, not a robocopy statement, and the article holds no tagged fact for it. Needs a probe on NTFS volumes. (topic: windows/robocopy)

## gitlab/protected-branches-tags

- **Whether the post-merge deletion of a source branch that matches a protected pattern passes the "web interface" condition of the protected-branch deletion check, and where the project ability `destroy_protected_branch` is granted.** Looked 2026-10-20 in the protected branches page (S444), the merge requests API page (S-4abgkvor) and GitLab master @56c82a97: `branch_check.rb`, `user_access.rb`, `delete_source_branch_worker.rb`, `delete_branch_worker.rb`, `delete_service.rb` (S-m5gqjzvs, S-fi4hv5qp, S-7wdbu4r4, S-hxmsaqdu, S-onmjrpyd); the ability is not named in `project_policy.rb` or `ee/.../project_policy.rb` at that commit, and the protocol of the Gitaly hook call is in Gitaly, not read. Needs the Gitaly `UserDeleteBranch` hook environment, or a run that merges a request with source-branch removal on a throwaway branch under a protected pattern, once as a Developer and once as a Maintainer token. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/protected-branches-tags)
  - Resolved 2026-10-21 from code @GitLab 56c82a97 and Gitaly 3d210758: (1) the "web interface" condition passes: Gitaly's updateref update_with_hooks.go (internal/gitaly/hook/updateref) sets the hook payload Protocol to the fixed value "web" for every reference update with hooks, and UserDeleteBranch (internal/gitaly/service/operations/user_delete_branch.go) uses it; GitLab's updated_from_web? is protocol == 'web'. (2) destroy_protected_branch is granted by config/authz/roles/maintainer.yml (project raw_permissions; Owner inherits Maintainer), used by Gitlab::UserAccess#can_delete_branch? for protected branches. So the deleting user needs Maintainer; a Developer fails the can_delete_branch? check. A real run not done. Written to gitlab/protected-branches-tags.md:59 and gitlab/protected-branches-tags.md:60. (topic: gitlab/protected-branches-tags)

  - Tried 2026-10-21, still open: no new official source; the behaviour against a real forge needs a push to a GitLab and a GitHub test project from Windows and Linux. git-push and the pushRecurseSubmodules config were already read; no forge page states anything about a pre-push submodule check because the check runs in the client, not the server. Not run, since a push needs write access to a forge project and the work is read-only. (topic: gitlab/multi-repository-layouts)
- **What `git submodule update` prints and exits with when the forge refuses the reader (HTTP 401 or 403, an SSH permission denial), and whether a credential prompt blocks a headless run.** Looked 2026-10-19 in gitsubmodules, git-submodule and the protocol configuration at v2.55.0 (S-tzjw5vut, S-vvarw4uu, S-umm7t5f4) and GitLab's CI submodules page (S-7ozb4qou): none states the exit or message for an authentication failure; a local probe covered a missing path only. Needs a run against a private project with no credential, and the `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT` documentation. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/multi-repository-layouts)
  - Partly resolved 2026-10-19: `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT` and `GIT_ASKPASS` are documented in git(1) at v2.55.0 (S-dcbs6vpj) and GCM's `GCM_INTERACTIVE` in its environment page (S-6mfmfuft); a probe on git 2.50.1 (macOS) with a stand-in HTTP server and a stand-in ssh command recorded the messages and exits (`clone` 128, `submodule update --init` 1 after two attempts, no prompt) in the topic. Still open: the same against a real forge's 401, 403 or SSH refusal, on Windows and Linux, with GCM or a credential helper present, and whether GCM honours `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT` (its pages do not mention it). (topic: gitlab/multi-repository-layouts)
- **Whether Git for Windows ships `git subtree`, and how submodule paths behave on Windows beyond `core.longpaths`.** Looked 2026-10-19 in git-subtree at v2.55.0 (S-lu3mudsw), which lives in `contrib/` and says nothing of packaging: no Git for Windows page was read. Needs the Git for Windows release notes or installer component list. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/multi-repository-layouts)
  - Partly resolved 2026-10-19: the path-length limit (S1591) and the case rules of NTFS, `fsutil` and `core.ignoreCase` (S-gt67rjc2, S-zflhytlw) are in the topic, with a derived budget for `_cache/roots/<name>` and `kb/<name>`. Still open: a run on Windows of a submodule under a deep clone path with and without `core.longpaths`, whether Git for Windows ships `git subtree` and GCM by default, and how a case-only difference between `.gitmodules` and the disk behaves on NTFS. (topic: gitlab/multi-repository-layouts)
- **Whether commits pushed to other branches of a GitLab fork can be read by id through its upstream project, and whether GitHub lets a separate private repository (outside the upstream's fork network) open a pull request into a public upstream.** Looked 2026-10-19 in GitLab's forking workflow, creating merge requests, merge request troubleshooting and allow-collaboration pages (S-p6qwirq5, S-esk5mh7z, S-3o2ashyn, S-7vhtlusy) and GitHub's forks, pull request from a fork, checking out pull requests and branches pages (S-nhku2azk, S-acpw7zsh, S-7pysmrqf, S-askrbg64): they state the merge request `head` ref and GitHub's shared network Git data, but not what a project reads of a fork's other branches, and describe a pull request only across forks. Needs a run against a GitLab and a GitHub test project, fetching a fork-only commit id from the upstream and opening a pull request from an unrelated repository. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/multi-repository-layouts)
  - Tried 2026-10-21, still open: github/docs main (commit efd4783c2a) content/pull-requests/reference/forks.md says "Commits pushed to any repository in a network can be accessible from other repositories in that network, including the upstream repository" and "Git data from any repository in a network may be accessed from any repository in the same network ... even after a fork is deleted", which covers the GitHub fork-network half. Whether a pull request can come from an unrelated private repository is not stated on the pull-request pages searched (only "compare across forks"). GitLab: doc/administration/repository_storage_paths.md (master) describes object pools shared by forks, but no page says a fork-only commit id can be read through the upstream project. A test project pair would answer both. (topic: gitlab/multi-repository-layouts)
- **What a forge does with a pushed gitlink: whether GitLab or GitHub refuses, rewrites or displays a gitlink whose commit or url it cannot read, and whether the bitmap and path-walk packing paths of git skip a gitlink like the traversal in `list-objects.c`.** Looked 2026-10-19 in `list-objects.c`, `pack-objects.c` and `connected.c` at v2.55.0 (S-7otaqc5c, S-rl2loiqt, S-mhjsbdfr) and a probe on git 2.50.1 with local bare repositories (macOS): the plain traversal ignores a gitlink and a receiving bare repository accepted gitlinks to unreadable and to made-up commits with a clean `fsck`; no forge page read states a gitlink check, and `rev-list`'s own call into the traversal and the bitmap and path-walk paths were not read. Needs a push of a superproject with such a gitlink to a GitLab and a GitHub test project and the two packing paths read. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/multi-repository-layouts)
  - Tried 2026-10-21, still open (read in git code, not written to an article): at git v2.55.0 the packing paths also skip gitlinks. path-walk.c add_tree_entries has "Skip submodules. if (S_ISGITLINK(entry.mode)) continue;", and pack-bitmap-write.c fill_bitmap_tree has a default case "Gitlink, etc; not reachable" that sets no bit; list-objects.c process_tree_contents keeps "ignore gitlink". Not read: pack-bitmap.c's read side (it falls back to the list-objects traversal) and rev-list's own call. The forge half is still open: no GitLab or GitHub page read states a gitlink check; a push of a superproject with an unreadable gitlink to a test project would show it. (topic: gitlab/multi-repository-layouts)

## gitlab/commit-signing-and-provenance

- **What author name and email a commit pushed over git with a GitHub App installation token carries, and whether GitHub shows it as the app's bot account.** Looked 2026-10-19 in the deploy keys page and the installation authentication page (S-3466yhgq, S-6sw5hnns): they say API requests are attributed to the app, not what a git commit's author is. Needs GitHub's page on commits made by apps. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/commit-signing-and-provenance)
