Still open: whether GitHub renders such a commit as the bot account; no page read states it, and GitHub's page on commits made by apps was not found. Needs a push with an installation token to a test repository and a look at the commit page. (topic: gitlab/commit-signing-and-provenance)

- **Whether a Windows install of Git sets `core.autocrlf` at system level, so a checkout of an LF-stored file with no `.gitattributes` gets CRLF.** Looked 2026-10-24 in `core.adoc` and `gitattributes.adoc` at v2.55.0 (S-zflhytlw, S-vmz33l7n) and `environment.c` (S-zpfwc342): the code default is `AUTO_CRLF_FALSE`, and the docs give the Windows default `eol=crlf` only for a path with `text` set. Git for Windows' installer choice and its system config were not read; the Git for Windows release notes or installer page would state it, or a run of `git config --show-origin core.autocrlf` on a Windows host. [UNK] (topic: gitlab/multi-repository-layouts)

## claude/mcp-tool-search

- **The token size of one MCP tool definition and of a whole `tools/list` response in Claude Code.** Looked 2026-10-20 in the MCP page (S1862), the Agent SDK tool search page (S-sug3dznu), the context window guide (S-bisz7fay) and the cost guide (S2132): they give the `auto` threshold (10% of the context window) and the description cut limits, and no figure for a definition or a list. Needs a measurement of the server in question (`/context` with the server deferred and with `alwaysLoad`) or a Claude Code page that states a figure. [UNK] (topic: claude/mcp-tool-search)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: a text search of the MCP page for tokens per tool, tokens each and tool definitions finds only authentication tokens, and no Claude Code page read states a figure for a definition or a `tools/list` response; the `auto` threshold (10% of the context window) stays the only number. Needs a measurement with `/context` or a page that states one (topic: claude/mcp-tool-search)
- **Whether the Claude Desktop chat app (not the Code tab) puts every MCP tool definition into the context at session start or defers it.** Looked 2026-10-27 in the Claude Code docs (desktop page and MCP page) and by web search: the Code tab is Claude Code, so the tool search rules of `claude/mcp-tool-search.md` apply to it, and it loads the servers of `claude_desktop_config.json` into local Code sessions; no official page read says how the chat app loads tool definitions (a search for a Claude help centre article returned only third-party guides on the per-tool toggles under "Search and tools"). Two user-filed Claude Code issues (anthropics/claude-code 86284: a stdio server's `alwaysLoad: true` ignored in Desktop sessions; 67890: the Desktop Code tab on Bedrock never defers MCP tools) are reports, not documentation. Needs a probe (`/context` in a Desktop Code session and a tool count in a chat session) or a Claude help centre page. [UNK] (topic: claude/mcp-tool-search)
- **Whether VS Code with GitHub Copilot defers MCP tool definitions at session start.** Looked 2026-10-27 in the VS Code pages "Use tools with agents" (https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/agents/run/tools) and the MCP developer guide (https://code.visualstudio.com/api/extension-guides/ai/mcp): the first says "A chat request can have a maximum of 128 tools enabled at a time", names the error "Cannot have more than 128 tools per request", and points to deselecting tools or servers in the tools picker and to the setting `github.copilot.chat.virtualTools.threshold` to "automatically manage large tool sets", without saying how the threshold works or whether definitions are deferred; the second says VS Code "supports dynamic tool discovery, allowing servers to register tools at runtime" and does not mention `listChanged`, deferral or a tool limit. Tool search with a per-server `deferTools` setting (`auto` or `never`, `toolSearch: false` to turn it off, below about 30 tools everything loads) is documented for Copilot CLI only (docs.github.com, "Loading tools on demand with tool search", read 2026-10-27), a different client. The MCP servers page of the VS Code docs was not found at the path tried (HTTP 404). Needs the VS Code docs page that names the virtual tools threshold's default and behaviour, or a probe with more than 128 tools. [UNK] (topic: claude/mcp-tool-search)
- **Whether Cursor loads every MCP tool definition at session start, and whether it handles `tools/list_changed`.** Looked 2026-10-27 in Cursor's post "Dynamic context discovery" (https://cursor.com/blog/dynamic-context-discovery, dated 2026-01-06) and its MCP page (https://cursor.com/docs/context/mcp): the post says "we support dynamic context discovery for MCP by syncing tool descriptions to a folder" (one folder per server), so the agent starts with "a small bit of static context" and looks tools up when a task needs them, and reports a 46.9% cut in total agent tokens in an A/B test counted only over runs that called an MCP tool, with high variance by number of MCP servers installed; the MCP page says only that Cursor "automatically uses MCP tools listed under `Available Tools` when relevant" and covers approval, and says nothing about a tool limit, deferral, tool search or list-change notifications. Whether the folder sync is the default for every user and every model, and what a server can do to mark tools, is not stated. Needs a Cursor docs page on the mechanism or a probe. [UNK] (topic: claude/mcp-tool-search)
- **Whether any client other than Claude Code reads a server-side marker that a tool is deferrable.** Looked 2026-10-27 in the MCP specification pages for tools at 2026-07-28 and 2025-11-25 and the client best practices page (https://modelcontextprotocol.io/docs/2026-07-28/develop/clients/client-best-practices): the specification defines no field for it (a text search of the tools page finds `annotations`, `_meta` and the caching hints `ttlMs` and `cacheScope`, nothing on deferral); the client best practices describe progressive discovery as a host pattern (the host calls `tools/list`, withholds the definitions, gives the model a `search_tools` meta-tool, and switches by a threshold the page suggests as 1%-5% of the context window). The only server-side markers read are Claude Code's `_meta["anthropic/alwaysLoad"]` and the user-side `alwaysLoad` and `deferTools` settings (Claude Code, Copilot CLI). Needs a read of each client's docs if one adds a marker. [UNK] (topic: claude/mcp-tool-search)

## python/process-orphans-and-cwd

- **Which process becomes the parent of an orphan on macOS, and whether a macOS process can declare itself a subreaper.** Looked 2026-10-20 in POSIX.1-2024 `_Exit` (S-xp4b2g5v: an implementation-defined system process, `init` with pid 1 only as the historical case) and the Linux `getppid(2)` and `PR_SET_CHILD_SUBREAPER` pages (S-fcdwaly4, S-6xelj6je): Linux only. No Apple page on `launchd` or reparenting was read. Needs an Apple developer page or `launchd.plist` documentation that states it, or a run on a macOS host (start a child that outlives its parent, read `ps -o ppid`). [UNK] (topic: python/process-orphans-and-cwd)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: a web search found no Apple documentation: only an Apple X11-users list post of 2003 (orphans become children of init) and Claude Code issue reports of PPID 1 after a parent exits on macOS, not documentation; that macOS has no `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG` equivalent is a third-party claim. Still needs an Apple developer page or `launchd.plist` documentation, or a macOS run (topic: python/process-orphans-and-cwd)
- **Whether macOS's own `lsof` and `proc_pidinfo(PROC_PIDVNODEPATHINFO)` return another user's process working directory without elevation.** Looked 2026-10-20 in the upstream `lsof` 4.99.5 manual (S-ppkjvumt) and the xnu `libproc.h` and `proc_info.h` headers at xnu-12377.1.9 (S-ifgjakdt, S-4qybivhq): they give the `cwd` descriptor and the flavour, and no access rule. Needs Apple's `lsof(1)` and `proc_pidinfo` man pages, or a run as a standard user against a root-owned process. [UNK] (topic: python/process-orphans-and-cwd)
  - Tried 2026-10-21: a web search found xnu's `proc_listpidspath.c` calling `PROC_PIDVNODEPATHINFO` and handling only `ESRCH`, and a Homebrew FFI page that says a different `proc_pidinfo` flavour is not restricted to the current user's processes; no Apple page states an access rule for this flavour or for `lsof`. Still needs Apple's `lsof(1)` and `proc_pidinfo` man pages, or a run as a standard user against a root-owned process (topic: python/process-orphans-and-cwd)
